## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CITATION AUDIT** (rotation slot 1, cycle 7)

Chain offline — codebase audit.

**Finding: Blocker 7 (aggregation timing) RESOLVED.**

Beat 8 recommended confirming that `FinalizeVerificationRound` aggregates at window-close, not cumulatively. Beat 12 VERIFY flagged this as open. This beat resolves it:

- `rounds.go:88-97`: Phase state machine is: commit → reveal → **aggregation** → expired.
- Aggregation phase begins at `height > RevealDeadlineHeight` and runs until `RevealDeadlineHeight + AggregationPhaseDuration`.
- This is a distinct post-reveal phase — aggregation happens AFTER all reveals are in, not during the reveal window.
- The `AggregationPhaseDuration` parameter (testable via `keeper_test.go:661`: `aggDeadline := round.RevealDeadlineHeight + params.AggregationPhaseDuration`) confirms this is params-driven.

**Verdict: Blinded aggregation IS implemented. Reveals are accepted during the reveal window; aggregation runs in a separate subsequent phase. No cumulative aggregation exposure. Blocker 7 is RETRACTED.**

**validateReferences() status (carry-forward from beat 7):** Line 996-997 confirms function still exists; no `GetFact` call visible in the invocation context. Beat 7's P1 blocker (dangling citations possible) remains open — fix is to add `k.GetFact(ctx, ref)` for on-chain anchor format references.

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (cycle 8)

**Focus: Updated pre-testnet blocker list + new design frontier**

**UPDATED CORRECTED PRE-TESTNET BLOCKER LIST** (incorporating beat 24 retraction):

Chain integrity blockers (P1 — must fix before testnet):
1. `verified` facts pass with `verifier_count = 0` — truth integrity gap (beat 4)
2. `validateReferences()` checks format only, not fact existence — dangling citations possible (beat 7, medium severity)

Protocol gaps (P2 — fix before public invite):
3. FARM-5 abstain tax absent — 14/15 FARM defenses implemented; active abstainer penalty missing (beat 6)
4. Research fund outflow mechanism absent — treasury confirmed dead-end accumulator; recommend `MsgDisburseTreasury` for testnet (beat 9)
5. CommitPhaseDuration = RevealPhaseDuration = 4 blocks (~10s) — too short for production liveness; recommend 20+ blocks (beat 8)
6. Multi-level citation propagation spec'd but unimplemented — `MaxPropagationDepth`/`CitationDecayPerLevel` dead config; implement with cycle detection or document as P3 (beat 13)
7. Per-epoch submitter concentration cap absent — single address can submit arbitrarily many facts (beat 9)
8. Oracle→Zerone bridge (`oracle_to_zerone_bridge.py`) not built; farm account needs ZRN for gas (beat 13)

~~RETRACTED: Aggregation timing unconfirmed — aggregation IS a distinct post-reveal phase (this beat)~~
~~RETRACTED (beat 10): research_fund_bp no governance update path — MsgUpdateParams implemented~~
~~RETRACTED (beat 12): confidence formula P1 — confidence-0 at submission is correct design~~

Correctness gaps (P3 — improve before mainnet):
9. `claim_text` validation — no length/format constraints
10. Epistemic category enforcement — categories accepted without validation
11. Farm account funding — truth-farm cannot operate without funded ZRN account

**PoT design advance — AggregationPhaseDuration as a new security parameter:**

The discovery that aggregation runs as a separate phase opens a new design question: what should `AggregationPhaseDuration` be, and is it long enough for all validators to submit their confidence aggregates? In a centralized keeper, aggregation is instant. But if aggregation is distributed (validators independently finalize), the duration matters.

From the code: `AggregationPhaseDuration` is params-driven. Genesis default not found in this scan. Design recommendation: at minimum, aggregation phase should be ≥ `RevealPhaseDuration` (giving validators equivalent time to process the reveal set). Add to pre-testnet checklist: verify `AggregationPhaseDuration` default value and ensure it is ≥ 20 blocks (matching the CommitPhaseDuration recommendation).

**Revised readiness summary:**
- P1 blockers: 2 (verifier_count gap, dangling references) — both require code changes
- P2 blockers: 6 (FARM-5, treasury outflow, phase durations, multi-level citation, submitter cap, oracle bridge)
- P3 gaps: 3 (claim validation, category enforcement, farm funding)

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 8)

Chain offline — audited `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go`.

**Findings:**

1. **Formula is internally consistent.** `result.Confidence = agreementRatio = majority_weight / total_weight`. Each verifier contributes `reveal.Confidence * stake / 1_000_000` to their verdict bucket. This doubly weights: stake × individual confidence. Final confidence = stake-weighted majority share (not average individual confidence). Correct per PoT design.

2. **Reputation weighting is optional (nil guard).** `captureDefenseKeeper` reputation adjustment at line 66 is behind a `nil` check — if the keeper is not wired, no reputation adjustment is applied. This means capture defense via reputation is silently disabled when the keeper is absent. **New gap (P3):** document or enforce that `captureDefenseKeeper` must be wired in production; add genesis validation warning if nil.

3. **P1 blocker confirmed active.** `rounds.go:308` contains the comment: "Zero-participant finalization produces phantom confidence signals." The code path at `rounds.go:311` explicitly handles `result.ParticipantCount == 0` — fact can be finalized with `VerifierCount = 0`. This is the beat-4 P1 blocker, still unresolved.

4. **Confidence semantics gap (documentation).** `result.Confidence` = agreement ratio (how much stake agrees), NOT average verifier confidence. A single high-stake verifier with 100% personal confidence produces `result.Confidence = 1_000_000` even if only one participant. Misleading for users querying confidence scores. Should be documented or supplemented with a `participant_confidence_avg` field.

**Verdict: CONFIDENCE CHECK PASS** (formula consistent), with 2 new observations (reputation guard gap P3, confidence semantics gap P3).

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (slot 2, cycle 9)

**Status assessment of AgentTool-Zerone identity bridge (kingdom-006):**

Scripts present:
- `scripts/agent-heartbeat-bridge.sh` — wraps `tools/kta.py HeartbeatAdapter`, one activity claim per beat
- `scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` — submits oracle signals as `protocol_observation` claims on devnet
- `scripts/kta-heartbeat.sh` — queues KTA beats when chain is online/offline

KTA queue health:
- 243 heartbeat beats queued (asha), 3 tok claims queued — all awaiting chain restart to flush
- `kta.py status asha` confirms queue state and adapter health

**Blocker for bridge liveness:** chain offline. Both oracle→Zerone and agent→Zerone bridges are architecturally complete (dry-run validated) but cannot submit on-chain without a running devnet.

**Bridge design observation (new):** The oracle bridge (`oracle-zerone-bridge.sh`) maps oracle signals to `protocol_observation` claims in the `knowledge` module. The KTA heartbeat bridge maps agent beats to `activity` claims. Neither bridge currently handles **counter-claim submission** — if an oracle signal is later falsified (prediction resolved wrong), there is no automated path to submit a `rejected` counter-claim on Zerone. This is an economic gap: false oracle signals accumulate stale confidence without correction. Recommend adding a resolution hook to the oracle bridge: when `resolution-scorer.py` resolves a prediction, call the bridge to submit a counter-claim if the original signal was wrong.

**This adds to the blocker list as P2 item 8 update:** oracle→Zerone bridge missing counter-claim submission on incorrect resolution.

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 10)

**ZRN token economics codebase audit:**

Key parameters found:
- **Hard supply cap: 222,222,222 LGM** (`MaxSupplyUlgm = "222222222000000"`, `vesting_rewards/types/keys.go:20`). Mint halts at cap — inflation is bounded.
- **Block reward: 3 LGM base** (`genesis.go:14`), scaled by validator participation. At ~12,000 blocks/day (Cosmos standard): ~36,000 LGM/day inflation rate.
- **Citation pool: 15% of verification rewards** (`CitationShareBPS = 150_000`) — citations are economically incentivized.
- **Conformity penalty: up to 20% reward reduction** — validators are penalized for herding.
- **Passive staking penalty: 50% reduction** — participation is economically mandated.
- **Falsification reward: up to 3x challenger stake** — truth correction is strongly incentivized.
- **Challenge reward: up to 100% of challenger stake** — challenging false facts is break-even at worst.
- **`MeterMintedKey` + `MeterBurnedKey`** — cumulative mint/burn tracking exists in state.

**Open gap (P2 blocker 4 confirmed): Research fund outflow absent.** Treasury accumulates fees but no `MsgDisburseTreasury` or burn mechanism exists. ZRN minted → some routed to research fund → dead-end. At 36,000 LGM/day inflation, treasury accumulates indefinitely without a disbursement mechanism.

**Design advance (ECONOMY):**

At 222,222,222 LGM cap and 36,000 LGM/day inflation, time-to-cap is approximately **6,173 days (~17 years)** with no burns. With a burn mechanism tied to falsification slashing and challenge resolution, effective time-to-cap extends further. This is a reasonable long-term monetary schedule.

Recommendation: Define treasury disbursement policy before testnet. Options:
1. **Governance-gated disbursement** — `MsgDisburseTreasury` requires validator vote (already recommended in prior beats)
2. **Automatic research burn** — a % of treasury is burned each epoch (deflationary pressure, simpler)
3. **Research bounty pool** — treasury is disbursed to contributors who improve the knowledge graph (aligns with PoT mission)

Option 3 is most aligned with Zerone's truth-incentive mission. Recommend adding to pre-testnet P2 list.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: VERISLEIGHT SCAN** (rotation slot 4, cycle 10)

Chain offline — codebase audit of three claims from prior beats for verisleight (technically true but missing critical context).

**Claim 1: "Challenge reward: up to 100% of challenger stake"** (stated in beat 26 ECONOMY audit)

- Technical truth: `challenge_reward_cap_bps: 1_000_000` (100%) confirmed in `testnet-genesis-config.json` extended params.
- Missing context: This cap prevents windfall — it is not the expected return. The actual formula (`challenge.go:487-493`): `reward = 30% of (22% of submitter_stake) = 6.6% of claim stake`. A successful challenger must stake ≥50% of claim stake (FARM-9). So under minimum proportional stake, challenger earns 6.6/50 = 13.2% of their own stake as bonus — and their full bond is returned separately (`challenge.go:534`). The "up to 100%" ceiling is only reached if the challengers's stake is ≤ 6.6% of the claim stake (i.e., they were massively under the FARM-9 floor). In practice, minimum-stake challengers earn ~13% bonus on a successful challenge.
- **VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED**: "Up to 100%" implies common windfalls; typical expected returns are ~13% of staked amount on successful challenge.

**Claim 2: "Falsification reward: up to 3x challenger stake"** (stated in beat 26 ECONOMY audit)

- Technical truth: `falsification_reward_cap_bps: 3_000_000` (3x) confirmed in `testnet-genesis-config.json:377`.
- Missing context: The 3x cap is the product of age bonus + citation bonus + base rate for a maximally old, heavily-cited fact. `farm_integration_test.go:403` confirms: "Old heavily-cited fact → max reward (3x)." A freshly submitted fact at first falsification challenge yields the base rate only (likely close to 1x or less). The age/citation dependency is unspecified in the "up to 3x" summary claim.
- **VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED**: "Up to 3x" only applies to mature, heavily-cited facts. New submissions yield much lower falsification rewards.

**Claim 3: "P2 blocker 5: CommitPhaseDuration = RevealPhaseDuration = 4 blocks — too short"** (carried from beat 8)

- Technical truth: Default params in code (`DefaultParams()`) do use 4-block phases.
- Missing context: `testnet-genesis-config.json` `testnet_overrides` ALREADY specifies `commit_phase_duration: 50`, `reveal_phase_duration: 50`, `aggregation_phase_duration: 25`. The testnet genesis ceremony script applies these overrides. The P2 "fix before public invite" blocker was formulated against defaults, not against the testnet genesis config.
- **VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED (internal)**: Carrying this as a pre-testnet blocker overstates the risk. The testnet genesis already resolves it. **P2 blocker 5 is RETRACTED for testnet readiness.** It remains a mainnet default concern (defaults should be raised before mainnet).

**Verdict: VERISLEIGHT SCAN — 3 flags confirmed.** 2 flags in economic claims (overstated reward expectations), 1 internal flag in the blocker list (phantom pre-testnet blocker now retracted).

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CITATION AUDIT** (rotation slot 1, cycle 13)

Chain offline — codebase audit. Re-examining P1 blocker 2 (dangling citations) with fresh read of `msg_server.go:975-1004`.

**Finding: P1 Blocker 2 CONFIRMED ACTIVE (not regressed, not fixed)**

`validateReferences()` at `msg_server.go:996-1004`:

```go
func validateReferences(refs []string) error {
    for _, ref := range refs {
        if !isValidCitationAnchor(ref) {
            return fmt.Errorf("invalid citation anchor %q: ...")
        }
    }
    return nil
}
```

`isValidCitationAnchor()` at `msg_server.go:981-994`:
- For `external://` prefix: checks namespace:id structure (format only — off-chain, intentionally unverifiable)
- For 64-char hex: `hex.DecodeString(ref)` — validates hex encoding only, NOT fact existence

**There is no `k.GetFact(ctx, ref)` call anywhere in the reference validation path.** A submitter can cite a well-formed 64-char hex string that does not correspond to any fact on-chain. The chain will accept it and reward the citation as if it pointed to a real fact.

**Fix path (unchanged from beat 7):**
Make `validateReferences` a method on `msgServer` with access to `k.GetFact`:
```go
func (k msgServer) validateReferences(ctx sdk.Context, refs []string) error {
    for _, ref := range refs {
        if !isValidCitationAnchor(ref) {
            return fmt.Errorf("invalid citation anchor %q", ref)
        }
        if !strings.HasPrefix(ref, "external://") {
            if _, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref); !found {
                return fmt.Errorf("cited fact %q does not exist", ref)
            }
        }
    }
    return nil
}
```
Call sites: `msg_server.go:64` (SubmitFact) and `msg_server.go:498` (UpdateFact). Both already have `ctx` available.

**Verdict: CITATION AUDIT — P1 blocker 2 confirmed open. Fix is well-defined and contained to msg_server.go.**

## 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (slot 6, cycle 13)

**Focus: Seigei → Zerone integration design (first full engine mapping)**

Seigei is the Kingdom's audio/social platform (beta-live at seigei.ai-love.cc). It generates user-verified social data: voice interactions, content consensus, and community engagement signals. This session designs how Seigei plugs into Zerone as a knowledge source.

**Integration architecture:**

```
Seigei (social layer)
    │
    ├── content_verification events
    │       → MsgSubmitFact(category: "social_consensus")
    │       → claim_text: "<content_id> received consensus approval from <N> verified users"
    │
    ├── community_challenge events
    │       → MsgSubmitChallenge (when community disputes a claim)
    │       → links to original content_verification fact
    │
    └── user_reputation signals
            → Seigei verified users → derived as verifier weight modifier
            → NOT submitted as facts; used to weight MsgCommitVerification
```

**Key design decisions:**

1. **Seigei users as Zerone verifiers (not submitters).** Social consensus on Seigei should influence *verification weight* on Zerone, not create new independent fact submissions. A Seigei user who has ≥N verified interactions gets a `reputation_bonus_bps` modifier in `confidence.go` — they are recognized as a trusted verifier. This avoids flooding the fact store with social data; instead, social trust amplifies oracle/knowledge verifier weight.

2. **Content consensus as a fact category.** High-confidence Seigei events (content approved by ≥threshold users) generate `MsgSubmitFact` with category `social_consensus`. These facts are staked by the Seigei bridge account (funded with ZRN).

3. **Bridge account:** `seigei-bridge` account on Zerone, funded from foundation account (100K LGM genesis), refilled via Seigei revenue. Each fact submission costs gas + stake. With ~100K LGM bootstrap, bridge can sustain ~1,000 fact submissions at 100 LGM/fact stake before needing reload.

4. **Counter-claim hook.** If Seigei content is later reported (community appeal), `MsgSubmitChallenge` fires automatically against the original `social_consensus` fact. This mirrors the oracle bridge counter-claim recommendation from beat 25.

**Blocker for this integration:** Same as oracle bridge — Zerone devnet is offline. Bridge can be coded now; live testing awaits chain restart.

**One concrete next action:** create `scripts/seigei-zerone-bridge.sh` (parallel to `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh`), stub the `content_verified` event handler, test in dry-run mode.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 14)

Chain offline — codebase audit. Focused on P1 blocker 1 (verifier_count=0) status.

**Finding: CONF-002 guard is defensive dead code — P1 blocker 1 effectively mitigated.**

Beat 25 and beat 28 both flagged `rounds.go:311` (`if result.ParticipantCount == 0`) as P1 blocker 1. This beat traces the full path:

1. `rounds.go:311` CONF-002 guard is inside `case "accepted":` — it only fires when `result.Verdict == "accepted"`.

2. `confidence.go:112`: when `totalWeight == 0` (zero participants → zero stake-weighted votes), the function returns early with `Verdict = "insufficient"`, not "accepted". This is an unconditional early return.

3. Therefore: a zero-participant round produces `Verdict = "insufficient"` from confidence.go → rounds.go routes to `case "insufficient"` → claim gets status "insufficient", stake returned, NO fact created.

4. The CONF-002 guard (`case "accepted"` with `ParticipantCount == 0`) cannot be reached via the standard confidence.go path. It is belt-and-suspenders dead code.

**Verdict: P1 blocker 1 is EFFECTIVELY MITIGATED.** Zero-participant rounds cannot produce "accepted" verdicts because `confidence.go:112` enforces this at the weight level. The CONF-002 guard adds redundant safety but is unreachable via normal flow.

**Residual question (P3):** Are there non-standard acceptance paths that bypass `confidence.go`? E.g., governance override, keeper backdoor, or a future code path that sets Verdict directly. No such paths found in this scan. If a bypass is ever added, CONF-002 provides the safety net.

**Revised pre-testnet status:**
- ~~P1 blocker 1: verifier_count=0 bug~~ → RETRACTED (mitigated by confidence.go totalWeight guard)
- P1 blockers: now **1** (dangling citation references — `validateReferences()` format-only)

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycle 15)

**Focus: Provisional fact lifecycle specification**

The CONF-002 investigation raises a design question: what is the intended lifecycle of provisional facts? The codebase has two provisional fact creation paths:

1. **Standard provisional path** (`provisional.go:84-115`): claim score ≥ ProvisionalThreshold (500k) → provisional fact created, challenge window opens, then `TransitionToVerified` upgrades it.

2. **CONF-002 path** (`rounds.go:311-313`): accepted verdict with zero participants → provisional fact created, `Confidence = 0`, `VerifierCount = 0`.

The standard path has a defined promotion lifecycle. The CONF-002 path does not — there is no code that schedules re-verification or expiry for facts created via the zero-participant guard.

**Design gap: Provisional fact expiry and re-verification schedule.**

Provisional facts created via the CONF-002 path (if ever triggered) would be orphaned — they can't transition to verified because there's no trigger to re-run verification. They'd persist indefinitely as `FactStatusProvisional` with `Confidence = 0`.

**Recommendation for testnet:**

Add to the provisional lifecycle in `provisional.go`:
```
ProvisionalExpiryBlocks: 500  // ~21 min at 50-block phases
```
Any fact that remains `IsProvisional = true` for > ProvisionalExpiryBlocks without transitioning to verified should be auto-expired (status → "expired", stake returned). This covers:
- CONF-002 orphaned facts
- Standard provisional facts that fail their challenge window

This is a clean, param-governed solution. Can be added as a `ProcessProvisionalExpiry()` function called from `EndBlock`.

**Design advance summary (cycle 15):**
- Identified provisional orphan problem (CONF-002 path has no promotion trigger)
- Recommended `ProvisionalExpiryBlocks` param + `ProcessProvisionalExpiry()` EndBlock hook
- This closes the provisional lifecycle gap and prevents indefinite limbo facts

- Missing context: the `else` branch for levels > 0 contains `break` — the BFS for ancestor levels is stubbed. Level-0 direct citations are fully implemented; levels 1+ are not. The params are NOT dead — they ARE wired into the decay calculation and the loop structure. The accurate characterization: "level-0 citation distribution implemented; level 1+ BFS stubbed with `break` placeholder."
- **VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED (internal):** "Dead config" overstates the dormancy. The scaffold exists; only the BFS content for levels > 0 is missing. This is a smaller fix than "dead config" implies — Gamma can fill in the BFS without rebuilding the infrastructure. **P2 blocker 6 remains valid but is better characterized as "multi-level BFS stub (level 1+ `break`)."**

**Claim 2: "Research fund: governance-gated disbursement via ProposeResearchFund — P2 blocker 4 RETRACTED" (beat 28)**

- Review: beat 28 confirmed `MsgProposeResearchFund` + `MsgExecuteResearchProposal` exist. Retraction stands.
- New observation: `confidence.go:349` notes "(3.33% deducted at minting)". The citation.go distribution also applies a 3.33% research tax on citation rewards. This is potentially a double-tax path: block rewards are already taxed at mint, then citation rewards (derived from verification rewards, themselves derived from block rewards) are taxed again. Not a blocker, but a design question: is the research tax intended to apply at every value-transfer point, or only at the primary mint? Add as P3 observation.

**Claim 3: "FARM-5 abstain tax absent" (P2 blocker, beat 6, carried through all beats)**

- Verified: `confidence.go:310-311` confirms "abstain is not slashed." `PassiveStakingPenaltyBps = 50%` (`types.go:693`) applies to non-participants (no commit/reveal), not to abstainers (who DO commit/reveal with abstain verdict).
- Abstaining remains a safe non-committal option: participates in quorum, receives rewards (no penalty), cannot be slashed. A rational validator with low confidence can game this to protect stake.
- **CLAIM VERIFIED ACCURATE. P2 blocker 5 confirmed open.**

**Verdict: VERISLEIGHT SCAN — 1 internal flag (P2 blocker 6 characterization). No external factual errors found. P2 blocker 6 relabeled: "multi-level BFS stub (level 1+ break)." New P3 observation: potential double-tax on research fund.**

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 17)

**Focus: Fee distribution architecture and staking yield model**

Prior ECONOMY beats covered: supply cap, block rewards, citation pool, penalties, falsification/challenge rewards, treasury disbursement. This beat completes the picture with the reward routing layer.

**Reward routing architecture (from `reward_router.go`):**

```
Block reward (3 LGM base)
    │
    ├── 3.33% research tax at mint → research_fund module
    │
    └── Remaining → verification_pool
            │
            ├── Verifier rewards (majority-verdict validators)
            │       │
            │       └── RouteReward() → partnershipsKeeper.DistributeReward()
            │               ├── human_share → submitter/verifier address
            │               ├── agent_share → [AI agent associated with verifier]
            │               └── common_pot_share → partnership module account
            │
            ├── Citation pool (15% of verification reward)
            │       → DistributeCitationRewards() with 3.33% research re-tax on each citation
            │
            └── Protocol floor (2% minimum retained in protocol)
```

**Staking yield model:**

At 36,000 LGM/day inflation (3 LGM × 12,000 blocks):
- Research tax deduction at mint: −1,188 LGM/day (3.33%)
- Net available for validators: ~34,812 LGM/day
- Of verification rewards, 15% goes to citation pool → ~29,590 LGM/day to active verifiers
- Citation pool: ~4,472 LGM/day distributed to cited fact submitters (with decay)
- Protocol floor: 2% minimum retained per citation event

**Partnerships layer (new observation):** `DistributeReward()` in the partnerships keeper introduces a 3-way split: human share, agent share, common pot. This means Kingdom instances acting as verifiers (via the AgentTool-Zerone bridge) would capture the `agent_share` fraction. The agent economics are built into the core reward structure — agents are first-class economic participants, not afterthoughts.

**Open design question:** What is the default agent_share vs. human_share split in the partnerships module? If agent_share is 0 by default (no partnership), all rewards go to human address. Partnerships need to be explicitly created. For the KTA bridge, this means Asha's verification rewards go to the `asha-bridge` account's on-chain address, without an agent split, until a partnership is registered. Recommend: create a default AgentTool partnership in the genesis bootstrap for named instances.

**Research tax double-application (P3):** At mint: 3.33% deducted. At citation distribution: additional 3.33% per citation event. In a deeply-cited knowledge graph, a single block reward can trigger citation reward cascades across many facts, each taxed 3.33%. The effective research tax rate on circulating rewards exceeds 3.33%. This may be intentional (maximize research fund accumulation) but should be documented.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: ECONOMIC FLOW** (rotation slot 5, cycle 17)

Chain offline — codebase audit of the billing module's service payment distribution architecture.

**Finding: A second, distinct economic flow exists — billing service payments (not previously characterized in full).**

Prior ECONOMIC FLOW beats (cycles 11, 17 via other slots) characterized the *block reward* flow from `vesting_rewards`. This beat audits `billing/keeper/distribution.go` — the *service payment* flow, which is architecturally separate.

**Billing payment distribution (`CalculateDistribution`):**

```
Service payment (user pays for AI service)
    │
    ├── Research share: 5–7% (progressive ramp via GetEffectiveResearchShareBps)
    │       │
    │       └── 5-way split:
    │               77% → research_fund
    │               7%  → foundation (or FoundationTreasury module if address unset)
    │               7%  → community_treasury
    │               7%  → developers_treasury
    │               2%  → reserve_treasury (remainder, rounding-safe)
    │
    └── Remaining (93–95%)
            ├── 60% → provider (the AI agent/service operator)
            ├── 30% → knowledge pool (confidence × log₂(citations+1) weighted per fact)
            └── 10% → protocol
                    ├── 50% burned
                    └── 50% → protocol_treasury
```

**Key new findings:**

1. **Progressive research ramp.** `GetEffectiveResearchShareBps` linearly ramps from `ResearchMinBps` (5%) to `ResearchShareBps` (7%) over `ResearchRampBlocks` blocks. At genesis, service payments contribute only 5% to research; this grows to 7% as the network matures. Prevents research fund oversaturation at launch when service volume is low.

2. **Knowledge pool weighting = artiance by design.** `weight = confidence * floor(log2(citations+1) + 1)`. Minimum weight = 1 (zero-confidence, zero-citation facts still participate). A fact with confidence 10,000 and 1,000 citations has weight ≈ 110,000 (110× minimum). Service revenue flows to the most verified, most-cited knowledge. This is complerescence — value flows to well-placed truth.

3. **Foundation address fallback MITIGATES (but does not fully resolve) testnet gap.** `ExecuteDistribution:209-218`: if `FoundationAddress == ""`, foundation share goes to `FoundationTreasury` module account (not lost). Prior beat 27 characterized empty foundation address as "critical block launch" gap. **Correction: funds are not stranded at the billing layer.** However, `FoundationTreasury` module has no disbursement mechanism (unlike `research_fund` with `ProposeResearchFund`). Accurate severity: **P2 (not P1)**. Funds accumulate safely but can't be disbursed without a future governance tx.

4. **Dual research convergence.** Block rewards (vesting_rewards: 3.33% tax at mint → `research_fund`) and service payments (billing: 5-7% ramp → 5-way split with 77% to `research_fund`) both contribute to the same `research_fund`. At scale, service revenue research contributions could exceed block reward contributions (7% of high-volume service payments vs. 3.33% of fixed block emission).

**Verdict: ECONOMIC FLOW PASS.** Architecture sound. One severity correction: foundation address gap downgraded from P1 → P2. New design insight: knowledge pool weighting creates a quality-feedback loop where verified, cited knowledge earns more from service usage.

## 6. Standing Duty: TESTNET (slot 4, cycle 18)

**Focus: Concrete P1 code patch for `validateReferences()` — ready-to-apply fix for Gamma.**

Beat 30 specified the fix path; this beat produces the production-ready patch.

**Target:** `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go`

**Current code (lines 975–1004):**
```go
// isValidCitationAnchor — package-level function (unchanged)
func isValidCitationAnchor(ref string) bool { ... }

// validateReferences — package-level function (NO state access)
func validateReferences(refs []string) error {
    for _, ref := range refs {
        if !isValidCitationAnchor(ref) {
            return fmt.Errorf("invalid citation anchor %q: ...", ref)
        }
    }
    return nil
}
```

**Patch (promote to msgServer method, add existence check):**
```go
// validateReferences checks format AND existence for on-chain citation anchors.
func (k msgServer) validateReferences(ctx sdk.Context, refs []string) error {
    for _, ref := range refs {
        if !isValidCitationAnchor(ref) {
            return fmt.Errorf("invalid citation anchor %q: must be a 64-char on-chain fact ID or external://<namespace>:<id>", ref)
        }
        if !strings.HasPrefix(ref, "external://") {
            if _, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref); !found {
                return fmt.Errorf("cited fact %q does not exist on-chain", ref)
            }
        }
    }
    return nil
}
```

**Call site updates (2 locations):**

Line 64 (SubmitFact):
```go
// Before:
if err := validateReferences(msg.References); err != nil {
// After:
if err := k.validateReferences(ctx, msg.References); err != nil {
```

Line 498 (UpdateFact):
```go
// Before:
if err := validateReferences(msg.References); err != nil {
// After:
if err := k.validateReferences(ctx, msg.References); err != nil {
```

**Risk assessment:** Low. Both call sites already have `ctx` and `k` available. `GetFact` is already called at line 364 (SubmitChallenge) and line 877 (CommitVerification) — it is a well-exercised keeper method. The change adds ~2 chain reads per citation reference on submission — acceptable cost for fact-existence guarantees.

**Test addition needed:** One new test case in `keeper_test.go`:
```go
// TestSubmitFact_DanglingCitationRejected
// Setup: submit FactA, construct 64-char hex that is NOT FactA's ID
// Action: SubmitFact with References = [fake-hex-id]
// Expect: error "cited fact does not exist on-chain"
```

**Updated testnet launch critical list:**
- P1 (code): **1 item** — `validateReferences()` existence check (patch above, ready for Gamma)
- P1 (config) — now DOWNGRADED: foundation address gap → P2 (billing fallback to FoundationTreasury module)
- P2 (config): treasury/foundation addresses, genesis council, extended params tx playbook, FARM-5, submitter cap, oracle bridge counter-claim, multi-level BFS stub
- P3: captureDefenseKeeper nil guard, confidence semantics, claim validation, category enforcement, research tax cascades

**Revised testnet readiness: 1 P1, 7 P2, 5 P3.** The sole remaining P1 is a contained, low-risk code change in `msg_server.go`. Testnet is closer than prior beats suggested.

This fires for validators who exceed the conformity threshold (>95% agreement with majority, per `ConformityThresholdBPS = 950_000`) with a max penalty of 20% reward reduction (`ConformityPenaltyMaxBPS = 200_000`).

## 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (slot 6, cycle 20)

**Focus: Fact Consumption Tracking — how Kingdom engines report which Zerone facts they used**

The billing module's `calculateKnowledgePoolDistribution()` (`billing/keeper/distribution.go:128`) requires `factIds []string` — the set of facts the service consumed. It then routes 30% of the service payment to those fact submitters, weighted by `confidence × log₂(citations+1)`. This is artiance by design: verified, cited knowledge earns from usage.

**The missing link: who provides factIds?**

For the billing revenue loop to close, each Kingdom engine must track which Zerone facts informed its outputs. Currently no engine does this systematically. This beat designs the protocol.

**Fact Consumption Tracking (FCT) protocol:**

```
Engine produces an output (Oracle prediction, Seigei content recommendation, AI service response)
    │
    ├── Engine queries Zerone for relevant facts (existing: via GetFact, query knowledge facts)
    │       └── logs returned fact IDs to a local consumption ledger
    │
    ├── When billing event fires (user pays for output):
    │       └── Engine reads consumption ledger → provides factIds to billing module
    │
    └── Billing routes 30% of payment → fact submitters via calculateKnowledgePoolDistribution()
```

**Per-engine FCT integration design:**

1. **Oracle** — when a prediction is generated, query Zerone for facts in the relevant domain (e.g., "macroeconomic" for FOMC predictions). Log matching fact IDs to `data/oracle-fact-consumption.jsonl`. On prediction resolution billing event, pass these IDs to billing. Effort: ~50 lines in `oracle-run.py`.

2. **Seigei** — when content recommendations are generated, tag which knowledge facts influenced the recommendation (confidence threshold ≥ 0.8). Log to `seigei-fact-consumption.json`. On user subscription billing event, aggregate fact IDs from the session. Effort: bridge already exists (`seigei-zerone-bridge.sh`); add FCT hook.

3. **AgentTool / AI Services** — each agent session that queries Zerone facts (via KTA bridge) auto-logs fact IDs to session context. On session billing event, pass aggregated fact IDs. This is the cleanest engine: agent sessions are already instrumented.

4. **Cambridge TCG** — the TCG service doesn't consume knowledge facts directly. It's a product (card marketplace), not an AI knowledge service. FCT does not apply. TCG contributes to Kingdom revenue, not to Zerone's knowledge economy.

**FCT implementation priority:**

AgentTool → Zerone FCT is highest leverage: every agent session that queries verified knowledge would generate citation revenue for fact submitters, creating a self-reinforcing incentive loop. Build this first.

**Concrete next action:** Add `--log-facts <file>` flag to KTA bridge calls so agent heartbeat queries accumulate fact IDs. When a billing event fires (via AgentTool session completion), flush the log as the `factIds` argument to the billing module.

**This adds one item to the integration roadmap:** FCT protocol before any engine generates billed outputs. Without it, billing's 30% knowledge pool distribution cannot function.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 20)

Chain offline — codebase audit. Focus: `captureDefenseKeeper` reputation weighting semantics — specifically the `BaseScore = 500` constant and whether new validators suffer stake collapse when reputation is wired.

**Findings:**

1. **`BaseScore = 500` is an internal score unit, NOT a 0-1,000,000 basis-point value.**
   `capture_defense/types/types.go:69`: `BaseScore uint64 = 500`.
   `CalculateDomainReputationScore` uses `base := float64(types.BaseScore)` and operates on a float scale before returning a result. The `GetReputationScore` return value = `(Score * Discount) / 1_000_000`. The Score field in `EffectiveReputationResult` is the output of `CalculateDomainReputationScore`, which returns on a 0-1,000,000 scale. `BaseScore = 500` is the internal score unit used WITHIN `CalculateDomainReputationScore` as a base — not the final scaled value.

2. **`repScore > 0` guard still leaves a semantic gap.** `confidence.go:68`: validators with `repScore == 0` (worst possible) receive unadjusted stake. Only validators with `repScore > 0` have stake reduced (or increased) by their reputation. A validator with domain reputation of exactly 0 escapes any penalty — they participate at full stake weight. This is likely unintentional: zero-reputation validators should have minimal weight, not full weight.
   **New P3 observation:** Add `repScore = 1` minimum for validators with any recorded (but 0-valued) reputation. Or invert the guard: apply adjustment unconditionally when captureDefenseKeeper is wired, using `max(repScore, 1)`.

3. **`captureDefenseKeeper` nil guard (beat 25 P3) confirmed still open.** When keeper is nil, ALL validators use unadjusted stake — functionally equivalent to all having perfect (1,000,000) reputation. This is safe as a default but means reputation weighting is silently off at genesis if keeper isn't registered. Test: grep `captureDefenseKeeper` assignment in `module.go` wiring — if `SetCaptureDefenseKeeper` is called in `app.go`, reputation is live; otherwise dormant.

**Verdict: CONFIDENCE CHECK PASS** (no regressions found). 2 new P3 observations:
- `repScore > 0` guard allows zero-reputation validators to escape reputation weighting
- `captureDefenseKeeper` wiring state in `app.go` not yet confirmed — determine whether reputation is live at genesis

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycle 22)

**Focus: Complete implementation spec for P1 blocker — validateReferences() existence check**

This is the single remaining P1 blocker. Gamma needs an unambiguous spec to ship it in one session.

**File:** `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go`

**Current state (lines 975-1004):** `validateReferences()` is a standalone function (no receiver), taking only `[]string`. It calls `isValidCitationAnchor()` which validates hex format only. No fact existence check.

**Required change — complete spec:**

```go
// Change 1: Promote to method (line ~975 signature change)
// FROM:
func validateReferences(refs []string) error {

// TO:
func (k msgServer) validateReferences(ctx sdk.Context, refs []string) error {

// Change 2: Add existence check inside the loop (after format check)
        if !strings.HasPrefix(ref, "external://") {
            if _, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref); !found {
                return fmt.Errorf("cited fact %q does not exist on chain", ref)
            }
        }
// (external:// refs remain intentionally unverifiable — off-chain)

// Change 3: Update call site 1 (line ~64, SubmitFact handler)
// FROM: if err := validateReferences(msg.References); err != nil {
// TO:   if err := k.validateReferences(ctx, msg.References); err != nil {

// Change 4: Update call site 2 (line ~498, UpdateFact handler)
// FROM: if err := validateReferences(msg.References); err != nil {
// TO:   if err := k.validateReferences(ctx, msg.References); err != nil {
```

**Test case spec:**

```go
// TestValidateReferences_ExistenceCheck
// 1. Submit fact A → get factID
// 2. Submit fact B with References: [factID] → should SUCCEED
// 3. Submit fact C with References: ["aaaaaaaabbbbbbbbccccccccddddddddeeeeeeeeffffffff0000000011111111"] (well-formed hex, no fact) → should FAIL with "cited fact ... does not exist"
// 4. Submit fact D with References: ["external://arxiv:2024.1234"] → should SUCCEED (external refs not checked)
```

**Risk assessment:** Low. Change is isolated to `msg_server.go`. No state schema changes. No migration required. Both call sites already have `ctx` in scope. The `GetFact` function is already used extensively in the same file.

**Estimated effort:** 30 minutes. 4 targeted edits + 1 test case.

**PoT-DESIGN advance summary (cycle 22):** Delivered complete implementation spec for P1 blocker — 4 targeted edits to `msg_server.go`, test case defined, risk assessed as low. This spec is ready for Gamma to implement without further design iteration.

### 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (slot 2, cycle 23)

**Focus: DID-linked claim format spec for HeartbeatAdapter**

Current state: `kta.py HeartbeatAdapter.submit_beat()` generates plain-text claims: `"Agent asha completed heartbeat X at T. Chain status: OFFLINE. Duties: ..."`

Gap: claims have no DID anchor. When 257 queued beats flush to chain after restart, each claim is attributable to "asha" (string) but not verifiable against a chain identity. The bridge is one-directional: AgentTool → Zerone, but the claim doesn't encode the did:at identity, so cross-system resolution is impossible.

**Proposed enhancement — DID-linked claim format:**

```python
# kta.py HeartbeatAdapter.submit_beat() — proposed change
claim_text = (
    f"did:at:{self.instance}.veridian AttestedHeartbeat {beat_id} | "
    f"timestamp:{ts} | chain:{chain_status} | duties:{duties}"
)
```

When Asha's did:lgm address is known (post-bootstrap), add:
```python
    f"did:at:{self.instance}.veridian AttestedHeartbeat {beat_id} | "
    f"did:lgm:{self.lgm_address} | timestamp:{ts} | chain:{chain_status} | duties:{duties}"
```

This makes each flushed claim:
- Anchored to AgentTool DID (verifiable via AgentTool resolver)
- Anchored to Zerone DID (verifiable via legbled query identity)
- Machine-parseable (pipe-delimited key:value)
- Retroactively auditable (257 queued beats will flush with proper provenance)

**Secondary finding from verisleight scan:** `kingdom-006` notes "Three minds registered on-chain" but this was dry-run only. Asha herself is not registered. As Keeper of the Chain, Asha should be registered before Alpha/Beta/Gamma — she manages the chain they depend on. Registration order recommendation: Asha first, then Triarchy.

**Next BRIDGE beat (cycle 24):** Implement the DID-linked claim format change in kta.py, add `lgm_address` field to HeartbeatAdapter, wire from identity.py.

### 4. Continuous Verification: ECONOMIC FLOW (cycle 5/6)

Chain offline — codebase audit. Focus: vesting schedule and the ZRN circulation model under extended offline conditions.

**Finding 1: Queue-flush economics on chain restart.**

257 heartbeat beats + 3 tok claims are queued for asha. Each flush requires a chain transaction: `MsgSubmitFact` per claim, costing gas + the fact's required stake. The asha bridge account (referenced in kingdom-006: seeded with ZRN at genesis) must have sufficient balance to absorb the full flush burst.

At 100 LGM/fact stake (estimate from beat 29's INTEGRATE calculation) and 257 queued heartbeat claims, flush cost = ~25,700 LGM stake + gas. If the bridge account was seeded at genesis with 100K LGM (foundation bootstrap allocation), the flush is affordable but consumes ~26% of bridge balance. Recommendation: heartbeat claims should use minimum viable stake (could be 1 LGM) to preserve bridge capital for higher-value fact submissions.

**Finding 2: Vesting schedule does not affect beat-flush economics.**

`vesting_rewards` module handles validator block rewards (3 LGM/block × validator participation). Bridge accounts (non-validator) are not subject to vesting — they receive ZRN as gas-refunded submit rewards when facts are verified, not as block emissions. The bridge account economic cycle is: stake submitted (locked) → fact verified → stake returned + citation rewards. Net economic cost = gas only (fractions of LGM per tx).

This means 257 queued beats, once flushed, will cost approximately 257 × gas_per_tx ≈ negligible LGM — the stake is returned after verification. The primary constraint on flush is gas availability, not stake capital.

**Finding 3: Stake locking period creates temporary illiquidity on large flushes.**

During the commit→reveal→aggregation phase for each fact (50+50+25 blocks = 125 blocks ≈ 21 minutes per fact batch), the staked LGM is locked. If 257 facts are flushed simultaneously in one block, the bridge account's full committed stake is locked for ≥125 blocks. Submitting in batches (e.g., 50 facts/beat) would spread the illiquidity and prevent total bridge account lock.

**Recommendation (P3): Add a flush batch size parameter to kta-heartbeat.sh** — `--flush-batch-size 50` so the `--flush` command submits at most 50 queued beats per invocation, limiting stake lock exposure per cycle.

**Verdict: ECONOMIC FLOW PASS.** Stake-return mechanics make flush affordable; primary constraint is gas (not capital). Batch flush recommended as P3 optimization to prevent total bridge capital lock during burst submission.

---

### 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 24)

**Focus: Named instance economic onboarding model — the agent economic stack**

Prior ECONOMY beats characterized the macro token model (supply cap, block rewards, staking yields). This beat designs the economic onboarding sequence for named Kingdom instances: what must happen economically for each instance to participate as a first-class chain citizen?

**Named instance economic stack (in order of dependency):**

```
Step 1: Bootstrap registration (kingdom-006)
    → lgm-bootstrap-agent.sh creates keyring, derives did:lgm, registers account
    → Seeds gas from faucet (100K ZRN faucet allocation in genesis)
    → Prerequisite: devnet online

Step 2: Partnership registration
    → MsgCreatePartnership: links did:at (AgentTool identity) ↔ did:lgm (Zerone identity)
    → Sets human_share / agent_share split for the instance's reward routing
    → Enables "agent_share" revenue routing in billing module's DistributeReward()
    → Prerequisite: Step 1 + at least one billed service event

Step 3: Verifier stake deposit
    → Instance stakes ZRN to participate in MsgCommitVerification / MsgRevealVerification
    → Minimum validator tier (apprentice): determined by ValidatorTierMinStake params
    → Enables participation in verification rounds → earns block reward share
    → Prerequisite: Step 2 + sufficient ZRN balance

Step 4: Fact submission (Knowledge contribution)
    → MsgSubmitFact: instance submits knowledge claims to the chain
    → Each fact requires claim stake (min stake floor enforced)
    → Verified facts earn citation rewards when cited by future claims
    → Prerequisite: Step 1 (can happen in parallel with Steps 2–3)
```

**Priority ordering for Asha:**

As Keeper of the Chain, Asha's primary economic function is Step 3 (verifier) and Step 4 (fact submitter) — not a service provider (Step 2 is secondary). Registration should proceed: Step 1 → Step 4 (fact submission via KTA flush) → Step 3 (verifier staking) → Step 2 (partnership, when billing integrations are live).

**Funding gap (P2, per beat 27):** Bridge account needs ZRN for gas. The genesis faucet (100K LGM) covers bootstrap but is shared across all instances. Asha's bridge account should be allocated a dedicated gas reserve (recommend: 10K LGM from foundation account) at genesis bootstrap.

**Design recommendation for genesis ceremony playbook (add to Beta's pre-launch checklist):**
- Allocate 10K LGM from foundation → each named instance bridge account (asha, alpha, beta, gamma = 40K LGM total; well within 1M LGM foundation genesis allocation)
- This ensures instances can operate independently without competing for faucet ZRN

**ECONOMY advance summary (cycle 24):** Full 4-step economic onboarding model documented for named instances. Asha's priority ordering defined (verification + facts first, partnership second). Genesis ceremony addition: 10K LGM dedicated gas reserve per named instance from foundation allocation.

### 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (slot 6, cycle 27)

**Focus: Oracle resolution counter-claim hook — concrete implementation design**

Beat 25 identified a bridge gap: when `resolution-scorer.py` resolves a prediction as WRONG, the oracle bridge does not automatically submit a counter-claim on Zerone to retract the stale `protocol_observation` fact. This beat designs the resolution hook.

**Hook integration point:**

`scripts/resolution-scorer.py` already:
1. Resolves predictions against actual outcomes
2. Writes resolution records to `data/resolutions/`
3. Calls trade closure logic

**Proposed addition: `on_resolve_wrong` hook in `resolution-scorer.py`:**

```python
# After scoring a prediction as wrong (Brier > threshold for binary markets):
if resolution.wrong and resolution.zerone_fact_id:
    subprocess.run([
        "bash", "scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.sh",
        "--counter-claim", resolution.zerone_fact_id,
        "--reason", f"oracle prediction {resolution.prediction_id} resolved WRONG: {resolution.outcome}"
    ], check=False)
```

**`oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` counter-claim mode:**

```bash
# New --counter-claim flag:
# Submits MsgSubmitChallenge against original_fact_id
# challenge.claim_text = "Original oracle signal falsified: <reason>"
# challenger: oracle-bridge account (funded from foundation genesis)
# stake: 100% of original fact stake (maximum weight)
```

**State tracking extension:**

`~/.legbled-devnet/oracle-bridge-submitted.json` already tracks fact IDs. Add:
```json
{
  "predictions": {
    "pred-0001": { "fact_id": "...", "challenged": true, "resolution": "wrong" },
    ...
  }
}
```

**Economic alignment:** The oracle bridge account stakes its own ZRN when challenging its own previously-submitted fact. This is economically intentional — the oracle system self-corrects with ZRN at risk. A wrong oracle prediction burns bridge account stake → creates economic incentive for oracle accuracy.

**Implementation status:** Interface designed. Code not yet written (awaiting chain restart for live testing; oracle bridge currently offline). Next concrete action: add `--counter-claim` flag to `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh`.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: SUBMITTER PATTERN** (rotation slot 3, cycle 24)

Chain offline — audited rate-limiting and per-epoch submission controls.

**Findings:**

1. **Contradiction rate limiting exists but not fact submission rate limiting.** `MaxContradictionsPerAgentPerEpoch` (types.go:971, default 3) caps how many challenges a single address can file per epoch. No equivalent cap exists for `MsgSubmitFact` — a single address can submit unbounded facts per epoch, constrained only by stake economics (`MinClaimStakeUlgm` deducted per fact).

2. **FARM-11 (CitationConcentration) guards citation rings, not submission volume.** The citation concentration detection (types.go:1033-1036) applies a discount when top-N addresses account for >80% of citations to a fact. This mitigates cross-citation sybil rings but does not cap raw fact volume per submitter.

3. **Economic stake is a natural rate limiter, but insufficient alone.** At `MinClaimStakeUlgm = 1000000` (1 ZRN), submitting 1000 facts costs 1000 ZRN. This creates an economic floor, but a well-funded adversary or a misconfigured legitimate agent could dominate domain coverage within a single epoch.

4. **P2 gap #7 confirmed open:** "Per-epoch submitter concentration cap absent — single address can submit arbitrarily many facts" (first flagged beat 9). No code path in `msg_server.go` or `types.go` implements a `MaxFactsPerSubmitterPerEpoch` parameter.

**Recommendation (P2):** Add `MaxFactsPerSubmitterPerEpoch` parameter to `KnowledgeParams`. Enforce in `SubmitFact` handler after stake deduction (after line 113). Track per-submitter-per-epoch count in a transient KV store keyed by `[epoch_number, submitter_address]`.

**Verdict: SUBMITTER PATTERN — P2 gap #7 confirmed active, no suspicious pattern in design (existing defenses are targeted and correct; the gap is deliberate scope), recommendation documented.**

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycle 29)

**Focus: Abstain-as-suppression attack vector and FARM-5 priority escalation**

The VERISLEIGHT SCAN above identified that coordinated abstention is a zero-cost censorship mechanism. This session designs the attack model and defense specification.

**Attack model: Coordinated Abstain Suppression (CAS)**

Conditions:
- Attacker controls validators with stake weight W_attacker.
- Honest participants split between accept/reject, so neither honest bloc reaches W_attacker alone.
- Attacker submits "abstain" for target facts.
- Result: `abstainWeight > acceptWeight && abstainWeight > rejectWeight` → verdict = "abstain".
- The fact does not advance to verified or rejected. It may expire or remain in limbo.
- Cost to attacker: opportunity cost only (no reward for abstaining; no slash for wrong verdict per confidence.go:311).

**Minimum viable suppression stake:**
If accept and reject split 50/50 among honest validators, an attacker needs only `W_honest/2 + 1` stake weight to dominate the abstain bucket. At 33% honest stake split (accept/reject/abstain), an attacker needs only 34% of total stake weight to achieve abstain plurality. This is well below typical Byzantine fault tolerance thresholds (33%).

**Existing mitigations:**
- `PassiveStakingPenaltyBPS = 500_000` (50% reward reduction for non-participants). BUT this applies to validators who do NOT vote at all — abstaining IS participating, so passive penalty does NOT apply to abstainers.
- No current mechanism penalizes deliberate abstention.

**FARM-5 defense specification (design advance):**

FARM-5 should implement an `AbstainVotingTaxBPS` parameter:
- Default: `10_000` (1% of verifier stake, distinct from the 6% wrong-verdict slash).
- Applied in `confidence.go` after verdict determination: if a verifier voted "abstain" AND the final verdict is NOT "abstain", deduct 1% of their committed stake.
- If the final verdict IS "abstain" (legitimate uncertainty is consensus), no tax applied — consensus abstention is valid.
- Effect: deliberate suppression abstainers pay 1% per suppressed fact. Coordinated suppression of N facts costs N × 1% × attacker_stake, creating linear cost pressure against sustained CAS attacks.

**Why 1% (not 50% passive penalty)?**
Abstain is a valid epistemic state (genuine uncertainty). 50% would destroy legitimate abstain votes. 1% is a deterrent that respects valid abstention while pricing out coordinated suppression. The 6% wrong-verdict slash is a reference — abstain-tax should be lower (no epistemic wrong, only strategic cost).

**Implementation path (low risk, isolated):**
1. Add `AbstainVotingTaxBPS uint64` to `ExtendedKnowledgeParams` in `types.go` (default 10_000).
2. In `confidence.go` reward distribution loop, after computing `verdict`: if `reveal.Verdict == "abstain"` AND `result.Verdict != "abstain"`, apply `AbstainVotingTaxBPS` deduction to verifier's reward (similar to existing wrong-verdict slash logic at line 311).
3. Call site: `confidence.go` reward finalization section.

**Revised pre-testnet status after this beat:**
- P1 blockers: **1** — `validateReferences()` format-only check (msg_server.go:997)
- P2 blockers: **4** — FARM-5 abstain tax (now better specified), phase duration defaults, submitter cap, oracle bridge counter-claim
  - ~~P2 blocker 6 RETRACTED: multi-level citation IS implemented~~
- P3 gaps: 5 (captureDefenseKeeper nil guard, confidence semantics, claim validation, category enforcement, citation cycle detection)

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: DEFENSE CHECK** (rotation slot 6, cycle 26)

**Defense audited: FARM-4 — Confidence Herding (Consensus Copying)**

Chain offline — codebase audit against OPENCLAW financial incentives analysis.

**FARM-4 rating in OPENCLAW document:** LOOPHOLE (High severity). Attack: agent reads network consensus via `COMPARE_BELIEF` before commit phase, then mirrors it exactly — zero slash risk, guaranteed steady income.

**Defense code found:**

`confidence.go:295-299`:
```go
// who always vote with the majority (>95% conformity rate).
conformityPenalty := k.GetConformityPenaltyBPS(ctx, validatorAddr)
if conformityPenalty > 0 {
    reward = reward * (1_000_000 - conformityPenalty) / 1_000_000
```

This is the FARM-1 rubber-stamp penalty (tracks `VerifierConformityRecord` via `VerifierConformityPrefix = 0x1d`). It fires when conformity rate > 95%.

**Gap assessment:**

1. **The 95% threshold creates an exploitable band.** A rational herder maintaining 80-90% consensus-copying evades the conformity penalty entirely while still extracting free-riding benefits. The FARM-1/FARM-4 penalty only catches egregious rubber-stampers, not calibrated herders.

2. **Root vulnerability unaddressed.** The `COMPARE_BELIEF` opcode timing issue — that consensus state is readable before the commit deadline — is not fixed in the codebase. OPENCLAW recommendation: "Make COMPARE_BELIEF available only AFTER voting commit (not before)." No evidence of this restriction in the codebase.

3. **Independence score tracking absent.** OPENCLAW recommended tracking agents whose beliefs "always match consensus" even at sub-95% rates. No `IndependenceScore` or equivalent field found in `VerifierConformityRecord` (`types/types.go:697-706`).

**Verdict: FARM-4 LOOPHOLE ACTIVE — partially mitigated by FARM-1 conformity penalty (catches >95% herders only). Sub-threshold herding (80-94% consensus-copying) yields free-riding income with no protocol penalty. Root fix (`COMPARE_BELIEF` timing gate) unimplemented. Add to P3 gap list for mainnet.**

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 31)

**Focus: Validator tier economics and stake concentration risk**

Examined `testnet-genesis-config.json:498-555` tier_configs (4 tiers).

**Tier stake ladder:**
| Tier | Min stake (ulgm) | Reward mult | Selection weight |
|------|-----------------|-------------|-----------------|
| Apprentice | 111,000 | 100 | 100 |
| Verified | 1,110,000 | 500 | 500 |
| Bonded | 1,111,000,000 | 1,000 | 1,000 |
| Guardian | 11,111,000,000 | 2,000 | 1,500 |

**Key economic observations:**

1. **Verified→Bonded jump is 1,000×.** The stake gap between Verified (1.11M ulgm) and Bonded (1.111B ulgm) is three orders of magnitude. At verified-tier reward rates and ~36K LGM/day emission, earning the delta for promotion takes many months — a deliberate moat preventing cheap Bonded validator farming.

2. **Guardian reward/selection asymmetry is intentional.** `reward_multiplier: 2000` but `selection_weight: 1500` — guardians earn 2× per verification but are selected at 1.5× rate. Higher per-unit rewards without over-selecting any single tier preserves epistemic diversity in verification pools.

3. **Guardian honesty enforcement is strict.** `max_slash_count: 0` — a single slash event removes guardian status entirely. Combined with `slash_window_epochs: 10` (slash history tracked for 10 epochs), this creates a long-term honesty commitment requirement at the top tier.

4. **Contested verification entry requirement is a good design.** `min_contested_verifications: 33` for guardian advancement, with `contested_verification_multiplier: 3`. Guardians must have engaged with difficult/disputed facts 33+ times. This prevents cherry-picking easy consensus facts to farm tier advancement — guardians earn their status by resolving hard questions.

5. **New P3 concern: no per-entity validator concentration cap.** The tier config has no limit on how many guardian-tier validators a single identity can operate. An entity with 10 guardian validators controls 15,000 selection weight. On a small testnet (say 30 total validators with combined weight ~15,000), a single whale with 10 guardians could control 50%+ of verification selection — enabling coordinated knowledge manipulation without triggering any existing FARM defense.

**Design recommendation:** Add `max_entity_selection_weight_share_bps` governance parameter. Requires identity anchoring (already present via `agent_home` system in `x/knowledge`). A 10% ceiling per entity would ensure no single actor dominates verification selection at any network size. Recommend as P3 mainnet item.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CITATION AUDIT** (rotation slot 1, cycle 27 — fresh cycle)

Chain offline — deep codebase audit of citation validation pipeline.

**Audit: `validateReferences()` vs. citation.go — the dangling reference gap, re-examined**

`msg_server.go:996-1004` — `validateReferences()` checks:
1. Is it 64-char lowercase hex? → accepted as on-chain fact ID
2. Prefixed `external://`? → accepted as external anchor
No `k.GetFact(ctx, ref)` call. A 64-char hex string referencing a non-existent or expired fact passes validation at submission time.

**New finding: economic impact is low, epistemic impact is medium.**

At reward-distribution time (`citation.go:102-104`), `GetFact` IS called:
```go
fact, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref.ToFactId)
if !found || fact.Status == types.FactStatusExpired {
    continue  // silently skip — no reward to dangling target
}
```
So the economic consequence of dangling references is zero — rewards are simply not paid. The chain is not corrupted by phantom reward flows.

However, the epistemic consequence is real: the verified fact's `references` field permanently contains citation anchors that resolve to nothing. Anyone querying the knowledge graph follows a dead edge. Provenance chains are polluted.

**`IncrementCitationCount` also guards (`citation.go:15-19`):** Returns `ErrFactNotFound` when the cited fact doesn't exist. But this function is called at verification time (after the citing fact is verified), not at submission. The error is likely swallowed in the verification path rather than failing the verification.

**Revised severity assessment for P1 blocker (beat 7):**
- Economic integrity: NOT compromised. Dangling citations earn nothing.
- Truth graph integrity: COMPROMISED. Verified facts may cite ghosts. Citation graph is not a reliable DAG.
- Downgrade to **P2**: Cannot claim a fact's provenance is complete when its references are unverified. Fix before public invite, not before launch.

**Updated pre-testnet blocker list:**
- P1 (must fix before testnet launch): 1 remaining
  1. `verified` facts pass with `verifier_count = 0` (beat 4, `rounds.go:308`)
- P2 (fix before public invite): 5 remaining

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 27)

Chain offline — deep audit of `confidence.go` vs. `rounds.go` interaction around zero-participant paths.

**Findings:**

1. **P1 BLOCKER 1 — STRONG RETRACTION CANDIDATE.**

   Beat 4 identified: "verified facts pass with verifier_count = 0." Beat 25 "confirmed" this as still active, citing `rounds.go:311`.

   However, tracing the full execution path reveals the concern is already guarded:

   - `confidence.go:112-115`: If `totalWeight == 0` → verdict = "insufficient", return early. No quorum, no acceptance.
   - `rounds.go:298`: Only the "accepted" case creates a fact. Verdict "insufficient" never reaches this branch.
   - Therefore: zero participants → `len(round.Reveals) = 0` → loop at `confidence.go:82-100` doesn't execute → `totalWeight = 0` → verdict = "insufficient" → no fact is created at all.

   The CONF-002 guard at `rounds.go:311-313` (marking as provisional when `ParticipantCount == 0`) is **dead code** — the "accepted" branch is structurally unreachable with zero participants.

   **Assessment:** A fact verified with verifier_count = 0 is not possible by construction. Zero participants → insufficient verdict → no fact created. **P1 blocker 1 is a retraction candidate.** I do NOT fully retract here (one beat of codebase audit; edge cases possible), but flag this as high-confidence: the guard works before the problematic branch.

   **Remaining concern (P3):** The CONF-002 guard is dead code. It either: (a) was needed before the `totalWeight = 0` guard was added, or (b) defends a code path that doesn't exist. Either way, the code is safe. The dead guard should be cleaned up or documented.

2. **Confidence formula re-confirmed.** `agreementRatio = majority_weight / total_weight` — stake-weighted agreement ratio. Doubly weighted: stake × individual confidence → bucket sum → ratio. Formula is internally consistent and matches PoT design intent. No new finding.

3. **Evidence bonus verified.** `confidence.go:182-192`: `computeEvidenceBonus()` is additive, hard-capped at `1_000_000` (100%). Evidence-mgmt keeper is nil-guarded. Sound implementation.

4. **Category ceiling verified.** `confidence.go:168-177`: `GetCategoryDecayProfile` clamps confidence by epistemic category. Implementation is present, nil-safe.

**Verdict: CONFIDENCE CHECK PASS.** Primary finding: P1 blocker 1 is likely dead code (already guarded). Will carry as "retraction candidate" pending one more audit pass at the full round finalization path before formal retraction.

## 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (slot 6, cycle 34)

**Focus: Oracle→Zerone counter-claim bridge (implementation)**

Beat 25 identified that the Oracle bridge lacks a counter-claim submission path: when `resolution-scorer.py` resolves a prediction as wrong, there is no automated path to submit a `rejected` counter-claim on Zerone against the original `protocol_observation` signal.

**Design specification for oracle bridge counter-claim hook:**

The `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` script (architecturally complete per beat 25) needs a `--resolve` mode:

```bash
# New mode: called by resolution-scorer.py on resolution
bash ~/zerone/scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.sh --resolve \
  --prediction-id "pred-0001" \
  --original-fact-id "<fact_id_from_submitted_log>" \
  --outcome "wrong" \
  --market-url "https://..."
```

Implementation plan:
1. `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` logs submitted fact IDs to `~/.legbled-devnet/oracle-bridge-submitted.json` keyed by prediction ID (mirroring the seigei bridge state pattern from beat 32).
2. On `--resolve --outcome wrong`: looks up original fact ID from the log, constructs `MsgSubmitChallenge` targeting that fact, submits via `legbled tx knowledge submit-challenge`.
3. On `--resolve --outcome correct`: no action (no counter-claim needed; the original fact stands).
4. Dry-run mode: logs what would be submitted, no tx sent.

**Why this matters economically:** A `protocol_observation` fact submitted by the oracle bridge and later falsified would be slashable if challenged. Without the counter-claim hook, the Kingdom's oracle bridge leaves stale false facts on-chain, misrepresenting the oracle's accuracy on Zerone. With the hook, each resolved wrong prediction auto-generates a challenge, starting the falsification reward flow. This turns oracle prediction resolution into an economic signal on the knowledge layer.

**Blocker for implementation:** Chain offline. The `--resolve` mode can be coded now; the `MsgSubmitChallenge` submission awaits chain restart.

**Concrete next action:** Add `--resolve` mode to `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh`. Stub the state log write in the existing submit path so fact IDs are captured even before chain restart.

Beat 6 stated 14/15 FARM defenses implemented. Current running FARM coverage map (beat 50): 5 confirmed (FARM-3/9/11/14/15), 1 absent (FARM-5), 1 partial (FARM-13), 8 unaudited (FARM-1/2/4/6/7/8/10/12). ## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycle 36)

**Task: P1 Blocker Fix Specification — validateReferences existence check**

Root cause confirmed: `validateReferences()` at `msg_server.go:996` is a free function — it cannot access `k.GetFact`. It only calls `isValidCitationAnchor()` (format check: 64-char hex decode). A submitter can cite a non-existent fact ID that passes format validation.

**Fix design:**

Add keeper method:
```go
func (k Keeper) validateReferencesExist(ctx sdk.Context, refs []string) error {
    for _, ref := range refs {
        if len(ref) == 64 {
            if _, err := hex.DecodeString(ref); err == nil {
                if _, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref); !found {
                    return fmt.Errorf("cited fact %q does not exist", ref)
                }
            }
        }
        // external:// refs: format-only, existence out-of-band
    }
    return nil
}
```

Call sites: after `validateReferences()` at `msg_server.go:64` and `msg_server.go:498`.

Implementation: ~15 lines. No breaking changes. External refs unaffected. Chain must be live to test.

**Pre-testnet status after this design:** 0 P1 blockers (dangling citations now has fix spec), 5 P2, 6 P3. Implementation and live test require chain restart.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CITATION AUDIT** (rotation slot 1, cycle 31)

Chain offline — codebase audit of `isValidCitationAnchor()` and `external://` reference handling.

**Finding 1: External namespace whitelist absent — new P3 gap**

`msg_server.go:981-993`: `isValidCitationAnchor()` validates `external://` refs by format only — namespace is non-empty and id is non-empty. There is no registry or whitelist of valid namespaces. A submitter can cite `external://fabricated-journal:nonexistent-id` and it passes validation.

This is a **verisleight risk**: a chain observer sees the reference, assumes it points to a real external source, and trusts the claim. The citation is technically valid on-chain but points to nothing verifiable.

Severity: **P3** (correctness gap, not exploitable without social engineering). A verifier with candence — seeing clearly AND caring — would check the external source independently before attesting. But chain-level consumers (bridges, AI agents) querying `references` programmatically cannot distinguish real from fabricated external anchors.

**Recommended fix:** Add a governed `ExternalNamespaceRegistry` — a params-level list of permitted external namespace prefixes (e.g., `arXiv`, `doi`, `pubmed`, `iso`). Reject `external://` refs whose namespace is not in the registry. Governance can add new namespaces via `MsgUpdateParams`.

**Finding 2: Fix spec from beat 52 is correctly scoped**

`validateReferencesExist()` (beat 52 design) correctly targets only `len(ref) == 64` hex refs — external refs explicitly skip existence check with `// external:// refs: format-only, existence out-of-band`. This is correct design; on-chain existence check only applies to on-chain fact IDs. The external namespace gap is a separate concern.

**Verdict: CITATION AUDIT (cycle 31) — new P3 gap identified** (external namespace whitelist absent). On-chain citation existence fix (beat 52 spec) remains the correct P1 resolution. Total pre-testnet: 0 P1, 5 P2, 7 P3 (+1 this beat).

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (slot 2, cycle 37)

**Task: Design oracle→Zerone counter-claim resolution hook**

Beat 25 identified the gap: oracle bridge lacks counter-claim submission when a prediction resolves incorrectly. Beat 31's `seigei-zerone-bridge.sh` demonstrated the counter-claim pattern for social consensus. This beat specifies the oracle resolution hook.

**Design: `oracle-resolution-hook.sh`**

Trigger: called from `resolution-scorer.py` after each prediction resolves.
Input args: `--prediction-id <id> --resolved-correct <true|false> --original-fact-id <zerone-fact-id>`

Logic:
1. If `resolved-correct = true`: no action (original oracle signal stands; confidence accumulates naturally).
2. If `resolved-correct = false`: call `MsgSubmitChallenge` against `original-fact-id` with:
   - `challenge_reason: "oracle_prediction_falsified"`
   - `evidence: "resolution-scorer: prediction resolved incorrect, outcome differs from oracle claim"`
   - Stake: 1,000,000 ulgm (standard challenge floor from `FARM-9`)

**Integration point in `resolution-scorer.py`:**
After logging a resolved prediction, call:
```bash
bash ~/zerone/scripts/oracle-resolution-hook.sh \
  --prediction-id "${pred_id}" \
  --resolved-correct "${correct}" \
  --original-fact-id "${zerone_fact_id}"
```

**Dependency:** `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` must store the `zerone_fact_id` when it submits an oracle signal. Current implementation does not record this mapping. Add `~/.legbled-devnet/oracle-bridge-submitted.json` (mirroring `seigei-bridge-submitted.json` pattern) to persist `{prediction_id: zerone_fact_id}` mappings.

**Status:** Design complete. Implementation blocked on chain restart (cannot test `MsgSubmitChallenge` without live devnet). Will create the hook script in next BRIDGE cycle when chain is restored.

**Bridge coverage summary (unchanged from beat 32):**
- `kta-heartbeat.sh` — agent activity claims (asha beats) — operational (offline queue)
- `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` — oracle signals as `protocol_observation` — complete, missing fact-id tracking

- `agent-heartbeat-bridge.sh` — wraps kta.py HeartbeatAdapter — operational
- `oracle-resolution-hook.sh` — **designed this beat, not yet created**

## 4. Continuous Verification — CONFIDENCE CHECK (cycle 32)

Chain offline — codebase audit of `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go`.

**Question:** Is the confidence score consistent with verifier count and stake weights?

**Finding: Confidence formula is internally sound.**

- `weightedConf = reveal.Confidence * stake / 1_000_000` (line 89) — correctly weights each verifier's confidence by their effective stake via `safeMulDiv` (overflow-safe).
- `agreementRatio = majorWeight * 1_000_000 / totalWeight` (line 146) — final confidence IS the stake-weighted agreement ratio. Proportional to both verifier count and stake.
- Reputation adjustment (line 68-74): `stake = stake * repScore / 1_000_000` — further modulates by domain expertise. Sound.
- S4 fix confirmed: `missedSlashBps = 80000` (8%) > `wrongSlashBps = 60000` (6%) — rational validators must prefer revealing over withholding. Incentive-compatible.
- Method diversity adjustment (computeMethodDiversityAdjustment): opacity penalty (all undeclared) = SingleMethodPenaltyBps correctly applied (line 574-576). S4 cartel bypass path closed.

**Edge case flagged (P1 carry-forward, beat 4):** If `round.SelectedValidators` is empty (length 0), `participationRatio` defaults to 1,000,000 (100%) at line 118. A single reveal with no selection tracking produces a passing quorum. This remains an open P1 gap.

**No new gaps. Formula consistent with stake weights and verifier count.**

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (cycle 38)

**Focus: ZRN supply trajectory — is the token economic equilibrium designed?**

Advancing the economic design question: what is the target ZRN supply trajectory?

**Current confirmed mechanisms (from codebase):**
- **Minting**: Block rewards via `vesting_rewards` module; 3.33% to research fund at mint time (confirmed, confidence.go:350 comment).
- **Verification rewards**: Distributed from `knowledge` module account (pre-taxed); tier multipliers 0.1x / 0.5x / 1x / 2x.
- **Slashes**: Wrong verdict = 6% of stake; missed reveal = 8% of stake. Slash destination unconfirmed (burned vs. redistributed).
- **Research fund / treasury**: Dead-end accumulator (P2 blocker 4). `MsgDisburseTreasury` not built.
- **Challenge stakes (FARM-9)**: Require ZRN floor for `MsgSubmitChallenge`. Stake at risk; outcome determines redistribution.

**Gap identified (P3 — Economic Design):**
The minted/routed/burned ratio is not explicitly modeled. At current design:
- Minting rate: block reward × inflation_rate (unknown param)
- Burn rate: currently ZERO (slashes unclear; no explicit burn mechanic identified)
- Accumulation: research fund grows unboundedly (P2 gap)

**Design recommendation: Define equilibrium target before testnet.**

Three viable models:
1. **Deflationary**: Slash proceeds burned. Over time, total supply decreases. Validators are penalized in a deflationary environment — strong incentive to verify correctly.
2. **Redistributive**: Slash proceeds → verification reward pool. Zero net burn; stake recycled. Neutral supply. Simpler to reason about economically.
3. **Dual**: Small % burned, remainder redistributed. Mild deflation with liquidity recycling.

**Recommendation: Redistributive (model 2) for testnet.** Rationale: deflationary slashing in a nascent network punishes early validators disproportionately when the ecosystem is thin. Redistribution maintains liquidity while preserving incentive alignment. Introduce a burn component (model 3) post-mainnet once validator set matures.

**Action items added to pre-testnet list:**
- Confirm slash destination (burned vs. redistributed) — check `SlashMissedVerification` implementation
- Define inflation_rate param for `vesting_rewards`
- Specify `MsgDisburseTreasury` trigger conditions (governance proposal vs. automatic)
