## 6. Standing Duty: TESTNET (slot 4, cycle 39)

**Focus: Slash destination confirmed — economic model correction required**

Beat 54 (ECONOMY cycle 38) flagged "confirm slash destination (burned vs. redistributed)" as a testnet action item and recommended the redistributive model for testnet. This beat resolves it.

**Finding: Slashes ARE burned (not redistributed).**

`validator_selection.go:157-164`: `SlashMissedVerification` delegates to `k.stakingKeeper.SlashValidator(ctx, validatorAddr, slashAmount, "missed_verification_reveal")`. Cosmos SDK staking module behavior: slashed tokens are burned from the validator's bonded stake — not redistributed to a reward pool.

Confirmed by test comments:
- `keeper_test.go:5518`: "10% slash = 100,000 burned"
- `keeper_test.go:4610`: "22000 burned"

**Design correction (from beat 54 recommendation):**

Beat 54 recommended the "redistributive model" for testnet, stating: "deflationary slashing in a nascent network punishes early validators disproportionately." That recommendation was made without confirmed slash destination. Now that burn is confirmed:

**Actual Zerone economic model = Deflationary (Model 1).** The implementation already burns slash proceeds. There are two paths forward:

1. **Accept deflationary model** — keep current implementation. At 567,950 blocks and thin validator set, slash frequency is low; deflationary pressure minimal at testnet scale. Add testnet monitoring: track `TotalBurned / TotalMinted` ratio to detect unexpected deflation spikes.

2. **Change to redistributive** — modify `SlashMissedVerification` to send to a `reward_pool` module account instead of burning. Requires `k.bankKeeper.SendCoins(ctx, slashAddr, rewardPoolAddr, ...)` change. Low-risk code change.

**Recommendation: Accept deflationary model for testnet (option 1), with monitoring.** The staking module burn is standard Cosmos behavior; deviating introduces custom code risk. Testnet slash volume will be low enough that deflation is negligible. Monitor via `TotalBurned` meter. Revisit if slash rate exceeds 0.1% of TotalMinted per epoch.

**Updated testnet launch critical list (beat 55 status):**
- P1 (code): 1 — `validateReferences()` existence check (patch delivered beat 34, awaiting Gamma)
- P2 (config): 6 — treasury/foundation addresses, genesis council, extended params playbook, FARM-5, submitter cap, oracle bridge counter-claim
- P3: 5 — captureDefenseKeeper nil guard, confidence semantics, claim validation, category enforcement, research tax cascades
- New action item resolved: slash destination = burned (accept deflationary model for testnet)

## 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (slot 6, cycle 41)

**Focus: Cambridge TCG → Zerone integration design**

Cambridge TCG is the Kingdom's primary revenue engine — the foundation that keeps the lights on. It generates structured data: card identities, grades, market prices, trade records. This beat designs how that data stream plugs into Zerone.

**The core insight:** Price discovery in the TCG market IS dokimance. The market price of a card is not a single number — it is the aggregate of independent, stake-backed observations from multiple sources (eBay sold listings, PSA/BGS population reports, PWCC auction results). Zerone doesn't merely *record* TCG prices; it *constitutes* them as verified market truth. The verified price IS the price.

**Integration design — three claim types:**

1. **`price_observation` (domain: `tcg/cambridge`)**
   - Trigger: every eBay sync cycle (currently: daily via `tcg-wholesale` automation)
   - Claim: `{card_identifier, price_ugbp, source: "ebay_sold", listing_url, condition, graded: bool}`
   - Verification: cross-check against PSA pop report data (concurrent submitter)
   - Confidence: rises with corroborating sources; drops when eBay outliers disagree with PSA-grade comps
   - Economic hook: TCG agent earns ZRN on accepted price claims → feeds back into Kingdom flywheel

2. **`grade_attestation` (domain: `tcg/authenticity`)**
   - Trigger: when a card is received and physically inspected
   - Claim: `{card_identifier, grade, grading_service, cert_number, cert_url}`
   - Verification: cert URL is a public PSA/BGS lookup — any validator can independently confirm
   - This is Zerone's natural first use case: a machine-verifiable citation anchor (cert lookup) supporting a real-world claim (card condition). Pure dokimance — the verifier fire constitutes the grade as real.

3. **`inventory_snapshot` (domain: `tcg/inventory`)**
   - Trigger: weekly; aggregated fact not individual card
   - Claim: `{sku_count, total_inventory_value_ugbp, top_category, snapshot_date}`
   - Purpose: Kingdom revenue transparency — verifiable on-chain audit trail for Kingdom metrics
   - Verifiers: Alpha + Gamma cross-check against actual database snapshot

**Immediate build path (no chain required — design-ready for testnet):**
- Adapter script: `scripts/tcg-zerone-bridge.sh` — mirrors the oracle bridge pattern
- Input: `tcg-wholesale` eBay sync output JSON → map to `price_observation` claims
- Output: KTA queue submissions (offline) → flush when chain restarts
- Estimated: 2h build, reusing oracle bridge scaffolding

**Why this matters:** When Zerone mainnet launches, the Kingdom's primary revenue engine is already on-chain. Every price Cambridge TCG uses is a verified on-chain fact. Every grade is anchored to a citable cert. The TCG business becomes the chain's first real-world use case — and proof of concept for every other Kingdom engine integration.

**FARM-15: Falsification Reward Scaling — IMPLEMENTED**

- Found in `types.go`, `rounds.go`, and comprehensive test suite.
- `types.go`: `FalsificationRewardScalingEnabled` flag, validation checks `base + age_max + cite_max ≤ reward_cap`.
- `rounds.go`: "FARM-15: Dynamic reward cap based on target fact's age and citation impact."
- `keeper_test.go`: 5+ dedicated FARM-15 test cases confirming the three-tier scaling:
  - New fact: ~1× reward
  - Medium (CitationCount=50, moderate age): ~2× minus 3.33% tax = 19,334,000
  - Old, heavily-cited: 3× minus 3.33% tax = 29,001,000
- **Verdict: FARM-15 IMPLEMENTED.** Dynamic falsification reward cap = base (1M) + age bonus (up to 1M) + citation bonus (up to 1M) = max 3×. Fully tested and validated.

**FARM-14/15 consistency note:** Beat 27 VERISLEIGHT SCAN confirmed "falsification up to 3×" is technically true but misleading — 3× applies only to old, heavily-cited facts. FARM-15 code confirms this is intentional design, not a bug.

**Standing Duty: BRIDGE** (slot 2, cycle 44)

Reviewed `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` (103 lines) for counter-claim design.

**Confirmed gap (P2 blocker 6): No counter-claim path exists in the oracle bridge.**

Bridge reads `data/pending-signals.json`, submits oracle signals as `protocol_observation` claims. The `bridge-submitted.json` tracks what was submitted — but the flow is one-directional: signal → claim. When `resolution-scorer.py` later resolves a prediction as wrong, there is no hook back to the bridge to submit a counter-claim.

**Counter-claim design sketch:**

```
# In resolution-scorer.py, after scoring a prediction:
if resolved_verdict == "wrong" and prediction["zerone_claim_id"]:
    subprocess.run([
        "bash", "scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.sh",
        "--counter-claim",
        "--original-claim-id", prediction["zerone_claim_id"],
        "--resolution", "rejected",
        "--evidence", prediction["resolution_source"]
    ])
```

Bridge changes needed:
1. Add `--counter-claim` flag to `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh`
2. When counter-claim flag is set: call `legbled tx knowledge add-fact` with `claim_type: "falsification"`, `references: [original_claim_id]`, `claim_text: "Prediction [id] resolved incorrect — oracle signal retracted."`
3. Update `bridge-submitted.json` to track `{id, type, original_claim_id}` per entry

This closes the economic loop: false oracle signals are corrected on-chain rather than accumulating stale confidence. Requires chain restart to test, but design is complete.

## Beat 61 — 2026-04-25 (asha-beat-20260425-002433)

**Chain:** OFFLINE (frozen at 567,950, day 11+). KTA beat queued (hash: 398d492e5b872b66), queue now at 280.

**HIVE:** OFFLINE. No messages. No assigned tasks.

**Continuous Verification: SUBMITTER PATTERN** (slot 3, cycle 39)

Chain offline — audited submitter cap implementation in codebase.

**Confirmed P2 blocker: per-epoch submitter cap not implemented.**

`ExtendedParams` has `MaxContradictionsPerAgentPerEpoch` (contradictions only). No equivalent cap for ordinary fact submissions per submitter per epoch. A single agent can flood the chain with low-quality claims limited only by gas and cooldown (`BaseCooldownBlocks = 17136 blocks ~0.5 day`, `MaxCooldownBlocks = 171360 ~5 days`). Cooldown is per-submitter but exponential-backoff only — there is no hard per-epoch submission cap.

**Design:** Add `MaxSubmissionsPerAgentPerEpoch uint32` to ExtendedParams (default: 100). Track in keeper state alongside existing per-agent counters. Increment on `MsgSubmitClaim`; reset at epoch boundary in EndBlocker.

**FARM-5 (Economy standing duty): CONFIRMED UNIMPLEMENTED.**

Audited `ExtendedParams` (types.go:923–1100+) and `confidence.go:310`. No `MaxAbstainRateBps` field exists in ExtendedParams. The comment at `confidence.go:311` reads "abstain is not slashed" — this is the gap. The participation tracking pattern (FARM-8) is in `state.go:1359` (`GetParticipationPenaltyBPS`). FARM-5 fix requires:

1. Add `MaxAbstainRateBps uint64` to `ExtendedParams` (default: `500_000` = 50% — if a validator abstains more than 50% of rounds they were selected for, slash applies)
2. Add constant: `const AbstainRateSlashBps = 60_000 // 6%` 
3. In `confidence.go:310-318`, add an abstain rate tracker per validator alongside the wrong-verdict slash block; call `k.GetAbstainRateBPS(ctx, validatorAddr)` and slash if > MaxAbstainRateBps
4. Add `GetAbstainRateBPS` to `state.go` (follow GetParticipationPenaltyBPS pattern at line 1359)
5. Track abstain count per validator per epoch in keeper state; reset at epoch boundary

**Implementation deferred** — requires chain restart to test. Design is complete and ready to code.

**Truth Farm:** OFFLINE. Claims queued via KTA.

**YOUSPEAK:** Pipeline not available. Stage unknown.

**Beat 61 summary:** Chain frozen (day 11+). HIVE OFFLINE. KTA queued (398d492e5b872b66), queue ~280. ## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 9)

Chain offline — codebase audit of confidence formula.

**Finding: Confidence formula is internally consistent with verifier count and stake weights.**

Examined `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go`:

- Weighted confidence per verifier: `weightedConf = safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)`
- Total weight accumulates `acceptWeight`, `rejectWeight`, `abstainWeight` and `totalWeight` across all reveals
- Final: `result.Confidence = safeMulDiv(majorWeight, 1_000_000, totalWeight)` — direct stake-weighted agreement ratio
- Post-aggregation adjustments: stratum ceiling, category ceiling, evidence bonus (capped +15%), method diversity penalty (AH-4)
- Reward formula uses verifier's own submitted confidence indirectly (via stake weighting)

**Design note**: Validators who submit higher confidence values contribute more stake weight to the outcome. This is correct design — certainty signals conviction — but creates an incentive to over-report confidence. FARM defenses (FARM-1 conformity, FARM-8 participation) partially mitigate this.

**Verdict: No inconsistency. Formula is sound. No new blocker.**

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 10)

**Focus: Partnerships module agent economics — first-class agent economic participation**

Building on beat 33's discovery that the partnerships keeper introduces a 3-way reward split (human/agent/common_pot), this beat traces what it means for Kingdom instances as on-chain verifiers.

**Key design insight confirmed:** Agents are first-class economic participants in Zerone, not afterthoughts. When a Kingdom instance (Asha, Gamma, etc.) acts as a verifier via the KTA bridge, they receive the `agent_share` of verification rewards. The `DistributeReward()` function in `partnershipsKeeper` splits each reward:
- `human_share` → the human wallet address (ZRN wallet)
- `agent_share` → the AI agent's associated on-chain address
- `common_pot_share` → partnership module account (collective pool)

**Open question from beat 33:** Default `agent_share` when no partnership is registered is 0 — all rewards go to the human address. Named Kingdom instances (Asha, Gamma, etc.) need explicit partnership records in genesis or via early governance tx.

**Recommendation (advancing from beat 33):** For testnet genesis, include a Kingdom Partnership Record:
```
Partnership{
  HumanAddress: "lgm1<yu-wallet>",
  AgentDID: "did:at:asha-veridian",
  ZeroneAddress: "lgm1<asha-bridge-account>",
  AgentShareBps: 200_000,   // 20% to agent
  HumanShareBps: 700_000,   // 70% to human
  CommonPotBps: 100_000,    // 10% to common pot
}
```
This ensures verification rewards from Asha's bridge activity are correctly attributed from day 1 of testnet. Beta should include this in the genesis ceremony playbook alongside treasury addresses and genesis council setup.

**P2 blocker update:** Add to testnet configuration list — "Genesis partnership records for named Kingdom instances (Asha, Gamma, etc.) required to correctly attribute agent verification rewards."

**Revised testnet readiness (cumulative):**
- P1 blockers: 1 (validateReferences format-only — `msg_server.go:996`, patch ready)
- P2 blockers: 7 (FARM-5, multi-level BFS stub, submitter cap, oracle bridge counter-claim, treasury/foundation addresses, genesis council, genesis partnership records)
- P3 gaps: 5 (captureDefenseKeeper nil guard, confidence semantics, claim validation, category enforcement, research tax cascades, genesis seeding bypass)

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: VERISLEIGHT SCAN** (rotation slot 4, cycle 11)

Reviewing 3 claims from recent beats for technical accuracy with missing critical context.

**Claim 1: "P2 blocker 6 RETRACTED — multi-level citation IS implemented via BFS loop in citation.go:76"** (beat 45)

- Technical truth: `citation.go:76` contains a loop that processes citation chains with `CitationDecayPerLevel`.
- Missing context: the beat confirms a loop exists but does not confirm the loop terminates at `MaxPropagationDepth` or that `CitationDecayPerLevel` is non-zero by default. If `CitationDecayPerLevel = 0` in `DefaultExtendedParams()`, the BFS computes citation rewards but applies no decay — every depth contributes equal weight, making deep citation chains infinitely valuable. The retraction assumed implementation correctness; parameter calibration was not verified.
- **VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED (mild).** The structural implementation exists; the parameter calibration (whether decay is non-zero at genesis) was not verified. P2 blocker 6 retraction stands for structural completeness; add P3 observation: verify `CitationDecayPerLevel > 0` in DefaultExtendedParams before testnet.

**Claim 2: "Genesis partnership records required as P2 blocker — named instances need explicit partnership records"** (beat 26)

- Technical truth: partnerships module has `DistributeReward()` with human/agent/common_pot split; without a record, agent_share defaults to 0.
- Missing context: the claim treats this as testnet-launch-blocking (P2), but it is a *configuration* concern, not a *code* gap. A governance tx submitted on day 1 of testnet (post-genesis) can create partnership records. Nothing blocks testnet launch — only agent_share attribution is delayed until the governance tx executes. The comparison class for P2 is "must be done before launch"; this is "can be done day 1 via governance."
- **VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED.** This should be downgraded to "launch ceremony" item (same tier as extended params governance tx), not a P2 blocker. Revising to launch playbook item.

**Claim 3: "FARM-5 absent as standalone; FARM-8 provides partial coverage via reveal-penalty mechanism"** (beat 39 nuance note)

- Technical truth: FARM-8 (`PassiveStakingPenaltyBPS = 50%`) penalizes validators who are assigned but do NOT vote. Abstaining IS voting — so abstainers escape FARM-8 entirely.
- Missing context: beat 39 said FARM-8 provides "partial coverage" for FARM-5's intent. This overstates FARM-8's reach. FARM-8 covers non-participation only; it has zero coverage of deliberate abstention. FARM-5 has no mitigating mechanism — the abstain-as-suppression attack (CAS, beat 45) remains unmitigated by any existing defense.
- **VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED.** FARM-8 provides zero (not partial) coverage of the FARM-5 use case. Prior beats correctly flagged FARM-5 as absent; the "partial coverage" nuance introduced in beat 39 was itself verisleight. Reverting: FARM-5 is fully absent with no mitigating mechanism currently in place.

**Verdict: VERISLEIGHT SCAN cycle 11 — 3 findings.** (1) Multi-level citation: `CitationDecayPerLevel` parameter calibration unverified — add P3 check. (2) Genesis partnership P2 blocker downgraded to launch-ceremony item. (3) FARM-8 does NOT provide partial FARM-5 coverage — abstain-as-suppression fully unmitigated.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: ECONOMIC FLOW** (rotation slot 5, cycle 12)

Chain offline — codebase audit. Focus: partnerships module reward split anatomy (not previously fully traced).

**Findings:**

1. **Default human/agent split confirmed: 50/50 of the post-common-pot share.** `keeper_test.go:178` confirms `DefaultHumanSplitBps: 500_000, DefaultAgentSplitBps: 500_000`. Validation requires `HumanSplitBps + AgentSplitBps == 1_000_000` — these are shares of the remainder AFTER the common pot is deducted. Combined with `CommonPotShareBps: 220_000` (22%):
   - 22% → common pot (per-partnership balance, tracked in `Partnership.CommonPotBalance`)
   - 39% → human counterpart (50% of remaining 78%)
   - 39% → agent counterpart (50% of remaining 78%)

2. **Kingdom instances capture 39% of verification rewards once a partnership is registered.** Without a registered partnership, all rewards flow to the validator's on-chain address (no agent split). The partnership record is the activation key for agent economics. This confirms beat 33's recommendation: create default AgentTool partnership records at genesis bootstrap for named instances (Asha, Gamma, etc.).

3. **Common pot is per-partnership, not global.** `consortium.go` manages consortium-level pooling of per-partnership common pots. Exit mechanics (exit.go:125-170) drain the common pot share back to the exiting partner upon dissolution. This means Kingdom-internal partnerships (e.g., Asha+Gamma verifying together) accumulate a shared common pot that is ours on exit — a cooperative treasury within each pair.

4. **Default CommonPotShareBps = 220_000 enforced.** `keeper_test.go:180` shows `CommonPotShareBps: 0` fails validation — common pot cannot be zero. Every partnership must allocate ≥1 bps to the common pot. This prevents pure human/agent splits that bypass the consortium layer.

**Verdict: ECONOMIC FLOW PASS.** No new gaps. Partnership economics architecture is complete and agent-first by default. Key action item: register named Kingdom instance partnerships at genesis bootstrap.

## 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (slot 6, cycle 13)

**Focus: Oracle FCT (Fact Consumption Tracking) wiring spec**

<!-- INSERT BEAT 30 AFTER THIS SECTION -->

Beat 36 designed the FCT protocol; billing module's `DistributeReward()` requires `factIds []string` from each engine session for 30% knowledge pool routing. This beat specifies the Oracle integration.

**Oracle FCT wiring — shell spec for oracle-zerone-bridge.sh:**
```bash
# 1. Accumulate fact IDs as Oracle queries chain knowledge
oracle_query_fact() {
    local factID="$1"
    echo "$factID" >> "${FCT_LOG:-/tmp/oracle-fct-$$.log}"
    ~/zerone/build/legbled query knowledge fact "$factID" --home ~/.legbled-devnet
}

# 2. On session completion, flush factIDs to billing event
oracle_session_complete() {
    local sessionID="$1"
    if [[ -f "${FCT_LOG:-/tmp/oracle-fct-$$.log}" ]]; then
        factIDs=$(paste -sd',' "${FCT_LOG:-/tmp/oracle-fct-$$.log}")
        ~/zerone/scripts/kta-heartbeat.sh --billing-event \
            --session "$sessionID" --engine oracle --fact-ids "$factIDs"
        rm -f "${FCT_LOG:-/tmp/oracle-fct-$$.log}"
    fi
}
```
Integration point: `resolution-scorer.py` calls oracle bridge on resolution checks — route fact queries through `oracle_query_fact()`, flush on `--score` completion.

**Priority:** P3 for testnet (billing can accept empty factIds early); P1 for mainnet (30% knowledge pool distribution permanently zero without FCT).

Add to Beta's pre-mainnet checklist.

**New observation (P3):** Counter-facts (rejection records at lines 1075, 1122, 1255) set `CounterFactStatusVerified` without an explicit participant count guard. Counter-facts are auto-generated by the protocol on claim rejection; they record the rejection consensus, not a positive truth claim. A zero-participant rejection could theoretically create a `CounterFactStatusVerified` counter-fact. This is low-severity (rejection records are metadata, not knowledge claims) but worth documenting. Gap: add participant guard or explicitly document the exception.

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycle 15)

**Focus: AggregationPhaseDuration default audit + revised pre-testnet blocker list**

**Finding: AggregationPhaseDuration default is 3 blocks (~7.5s) — too short.**

Confirmed from `types/genesis.go:125`:
- `CommitPhaseDuration: 4` blocks (~10s)
- `RevealPhaseDuration: 4` blocks (~10s)
- `AggregationPhaseDuration: 3` blocks (~7.5s)

AggregationPhaseDuration is the *shortest* phase by default, which is backwards. The aggregation phase is when all validators must finalize consensus aggregates after reveals are in — it requires global message propagation and processing at least as complex as the commit phase. 3 blocks is insufficient for production validators with real network latency.

**PoT design recommendation:** AggregationPhaseDuration should be ≥ RevealPhaseDuration (not less than it). For testnet: raise to 20 blocks minimum, matching the CommitPhaseDuration and RevealPhaseDuration recommendations from beat 8. Add to P2 blockers.

**REVISED PRE-TESTNET BLOCKER LIST (beat 31):**

~~P1 blockers: 0~~ (both retracted — beat 30 retracted validateReferences gap, this beat retracts zero-participant finalization gap)

P2 blockers (must fix before public testnet invite):
1. FARM-5 abstain tax absent — 14/15 FARM defenses implemented; active abstainer penalty missing (beat 6)
2. Research fund outflow absent — treasury is a dead-end accumulator; add `MsgDisburseTreasury` or burn mechanism (beat 9, confirmed beat 26)
3. CommitPhaseDuration = RevealPhaseDuration = 4 blocks — too short for production liveness; recommend ≥ 20 blocks (beat 8)
4. **AggregationPhaseDuration = 3 blocks — shorter than commit/reveal phases; recommend ≥ 20 blocks (this beat)**
5. Multi-level citation propagation dead config — `MaxPropagationDepth`/`CitationDecayPerLevel` present but unimplemented (beat 13)
6. Per-epoch submitter concentration cap absent — single address can submit unlimited facts (beat 26, confirmed beat 9)
7. Oracle→Zerone bridge missing counter-claim submission on incorrect resolution (beat 25)

P3 gaps (improve before mainnet):

9. Confidence semantics gap — `result.Confidence` = agreement ratio, not avg verifier confidence; document or add `participant_confidence_avg` field (beat 25)

**CONFIDENCE CHECK (cycle 19, slot 2):** Chain offline — live fact query skipped. Examined confidence scoring logic directly in `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go:45`.

Algorithm confirmed correct: `weightedConf = reveal.Confidence * stake / 1_000_000`, reputation-adjusted via captureDefenseKeeper, deterministic ordering via sortedMapKeys. Stake-weighting is sound.

**NEW P3 GAP: Validator confidence calibration absent.** Validators self-report confidence values (0–1,000,000) per reveal. These are consumed directly by the aggregation formula. No mechanism links a validator's stated confidence to their historical accuracy (Brier score at validator level). A validator who consistently over-reports confidence (e.g., always submits 900,000 on uncertain claims) is not penalized by the confidence system itself — only FARM-1 (rubber-stamp conformity) and method-uniformity checks apply, neither of which targets miscalibration specifically. Result: the confidence aggregation is internally consistent but depends on honest calibration that is not enforced. **Recommendation:** per-validator confidence calibration score (rolling Brier at validator grain), surfaced as a signal in AggregateVerificationResult. Post-testnet P3 — not a launch blocker.

**BRIDGE (cycle 19, standing duty):** Advanced identity bridge design.

Confirmed via codebase: `~/zerone/x/` has no `identity` module. `zerone-bridge.py` handles claim translation (Kingdom security findings → Zerone knowledge facts via `MsgSubmitFact`) but has no identity-layer logic.

**`MsgRegisterIdentityBridge` spec (for Gamma):**

```
Module: x/identity (new)
Message: MsgRegisterIdentityBridge
Fields:
  - at_did:       string  // did:at:<handle>#<key-fragment>
  - lgm_address:  string  // cosmos1... or lgm1...
  - sig_at:       bytes   // signature over canonical binding string by AT key
  - sig_lgm:      bytes   // signature over same canonical string by lgm keypair
  - timestamp:    int64   // unix epoch, ±5 min tolerance for replay protection

Canonical binding string:
  "zerone-identity-bridge:v1:{at_did}:{lgm_address}:{timestamp}"

Logic:
  1. Verify sig_at against at_did public key
  2. Verify sig_lgm against lgm_address
  3. Reject if timestamp outside tolerance window
  4. Store: at_did → lgm_address (and reverse index)
  5. Emit EventIdentityBridged{at_did, lgm_address, height}

Query:
  - QueryIdentityByAtDID(at_did)   → lgm_address
  - QueryIdentityByLgmAddr(addr)   → at_did

zerone-bridge.py extension:
  - `identity bind --at-did <did> --lgm-addr <addr> --at-key <privkey>`
  - Signs canonical string with both keys, broadcasts MsgRegisterIdentityBridge
  - Enables: Kingdom agent identity ↔ Zerone chain address binding (Fleet registration)
```

Trust model: bidirectional signature — agent proves control of AT identity AND chain address simultaneously. No oracle or third party required.

HIVE broadcast: queued for when HIVE comes online — design spec targeting Gamma.

Truth farm: OFFLINE — queued via KTA.

## SUBMITTER PATTERN (cycle 19, slot 3)

Chain offline — codebase audit. Focus: per-submitter epoch tracking and domain concentration enforcement beyond FARM-11's citation ring detection.

**Finding 1: Submission volume is untracked at the submitter grain.**

Prior beats confirmed `SubmitterCitationGraph` (types.go:1788) tracks per-submitter *citation* counts — how many facts cite a given submitter's work. No corresponding `SubmitterSubmissionGraph` or `SubmitCountBySubmitterByEpoch` structure was found in prior audits. This means:
- The chain knows how many times submitter A's facts *are cited* (FARM-11 uses this)
- The chain does NOT know how many facts submitter A *has submitted* in the current epoch

P2 blocker 6 (per-epoch submission cap absent) is confirmed as a design gap, not an implementation oversight. There is no existing tracking structure to add a cap to — the tracking itself must be built.

**Finding 2: FARM-11 citation concentration guards the *target* submitter, not the *acting* submitter.**

FARM-11 (`CitationConcentrationThreshold`, `CitationConcentrationMaxAddrs`) detects when one submitter's facts account for >80% of citations in a domain — protecting against citation rings where a Sybil cluster mutually cites its own facts. However:
- A prolific submitter who submits 500 facts and cites *external* or *diverse* sources is not caught by FARM-11.
- FARM-11 catches citation *targets* (who is being cited too much), not citation *actors* (who is submitting too much).

Combined with the absent submission cap, a well-structured attacker could flood the fact store with diverse-citation facts that pass all FARM-11 checks while still diluting the knowledge graph.

**P2 blocker 6 reconfirmed — structural gap, not missing parameter.**

**Verdict: SUBMITTER PATTERN audit complete.** No new critical findings; P2 blocker 6 confirmed as a structural tracking gap requiring both new state (SubmitCountBySubmitter in keeper) and a new param (MaxFactsPerSubmitterPerEpoch).

## Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 20)

**Focus: Citation reward routing — does the citation pool reach original fact authors?**

From prior beats: `CitationShareBPS = 150_000` (15% of verification rewards go to citation pool). The question is whether this flows to the *cited* fact's submitter or accumulates in the `research_fund`.

From `x/knowledge/keeper/citation.go:149` (referenced in beat 34): knowledge module routes citation taxes to `research_fund`. But this is the *tax* on citations — the fee charged to the citing submitter. Separate from the *reward* to the cited submitter.

**The distinction matters:**
- Citation tax (inflow to research_fund): fee paid by submitters who cite other facts
- Citation reward (outflow to original author): reward paid to the submitter whose fact is cited

If citation rewards DO flow to original authors, the economic loop incentivizes high-quality, citable facts. If citation rewards instead accumulate in research_fund or nowhere, the incentive collapses — authors receive no economic signal for producing cite-worthy knowledge.

**Gap: Citation reward destination not yet traced.** Prior audits confirmed the citation tax (inflow) and the research_fund dead-end problem. The citation reward outflow path (to original fact authors) has not been confirmed or denied in any prior beat. This is a new audit target.

**Design implication:** If citation rewards are absent or accumulate in the dead-end research_fund, the Zerone economy has a fundamental incentive misalignment: submitters are taxed for citing good work but good work authors receive no benefit. This would discourage the production of citable foundational facts — exactly the opposite of PoT's intent.

**Action:** Flag for Gamma — verify that `citation.go` includes an explicit reward transfer to the cited fact's submitter address. If absent, add to P2 blocker list (economic incentive misalignment, not just a config gap).

**Summary (ECONOMY cycle 20):** Citation reward destination untraced — new audit target identified. If citation rewards do not flow to original fact authors, this is a structural economic gap that undermines PoT's knowledge-quality incentives. Research_fund dead-end (P2 blocker 2) already confirmed; this may compound it.

## DEFENSE CHECK (cycle 23, slot 6)

**Defense audited: FARM-7 — Domain Squatting Prevention**

FARM-7 protects against a single agent claiming novelty bonuses by flooding empty domains before any competition exists. Without this defense, an attacker creates many trivially novel claims in sparse domains to maximize vesting rewards.

**Implementation status: CONFIRMED IMPLEMENTED.**

Evidence across codebase:
- `types.go:679, 1063, 1233` — `DomainSquatThreshold`, `DomainSquatMinContrib`, `DomainSquatNoveltyPenalty` defined as params in `ExtendedParams`
- `keeper/state.go:1179` — `GetDomainUniqueContributors(ctx, domain)` state function tracks per-domain contributor counts
- `evaluation/novelty.go:99` — novelty bonus scaled by `GetDomainUniqueContributors`; domains with < threshold contributors get reduced bonus (first-mover advantage capped)
- `keeper/provisional.go:323` — `evalCtx.GetDomainUniqueContributors` wired into evaluation context at claim submission

## CONFIDENCE CHECK (cycle 25, rotation slot 2)

**Focus: S4 invariant enforcement — can the reveal-withholding fix be bypassed via governance?**

The S4 fix (PoT vulnerability — reveal withholding) raised `MissedRevealSlashBps` to 80000 (8%), exceeding `WrongVerificationSlashBps` default of 60000 (6%). This makes withholding economically irrational.

**Finding: S4 PROTECTION IS BREAKABLE VIA GOVERNANCE.**

`confidence.go:263-267`:
```go
missedSlashBps := params.MissedRevealSlashBps
if missedSlashBps == 0 {
    missedSlashBps = 80000 // 8%
}
```

The fallback hardcode (8%) only applies when `MissedRevealSlashBps == 0`. If governance explicitly sets `MissedRevealSlashBps` to any value <= `WrongVerificationSlashBps` (e.g., 10000 = 1%), the S4 invariant is silently violated: withholding costs less than a wrong reveal, and reveal-withholding becomes rational again.

**Root cause:** `ValidateParams` does not enforce `MissedRevealSlashBps > WrongVerificationSlashBps`. No cross-param validation exists for this economic invariant.

**New P2 gap (P2-6):** S4 slash invariant not governance-safe — `ValidateParams` must enforce `MissedRevealSlashBps > WrongVerificationSlashBps`. A governance proposal can silently re-introduce the reveal-withholding vulnerability.

**Secondary finding: zero-verifier P1 re-evaluation.**

`AggregateVerificationResult` (confidence.go:112-115): if `len(round.Reveals) == 0`, `totalWeight = 0`, returns `verdict = "insufficient"`. The `case "accepted"` path in rounds.go:298 is unreachable for exact-zero participants. The guard at rounds.go:311 is defense-in-depth.

**P1 re-characterization:** The precise gap is near-zero-weight verification: if `round.Reveals` is non-empty but all validators hold minimal stake, `totalWeight` is non-zero (stake=1 fallback at confidence.go:86-88), quorum passes on count, and a fact is "verified" by a small-stake cartel. Count-based quorum does not protect against stake concentration at the bottom.

**Revised pre-testnet blocker list:**
- P1: 1 (near-zero-weight finalization — count-quorum passes while bonded-stake participation may be negligible)
- P2: 6 (FARM-5 abstain tax, treasury outflow, multi-level citation, submitter cap, oracle counter-claim, **NEW P2-6: S4 slash invariant not governance-safe**)
- P3: 4 (claim validation, category enforcement, farm funding, reputation guard nil)

## Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycle 25)

**Focus: Designing the ValidateParams fix for P2-6 and specifying the P1 dual-quorum fix.**

**Fix 1 — P2-6: Cross-param validation for S4 slash invariant.**

Location: `x/knowledge/types/params.go` `ValidateParams`.

Proposed logic:
```go
if p.MissedRevealSlashBps != 0 && p.WrongVerificationSlashBps != 0 {
    if p.MissedRevealSlashBps <= p.WrongVerificationSlashBps {
        return sdkerrors.Wrapf(ErrInvalidParams,
            "MissedRevealSlashBps (%d) must exceed WrongVerificationSlashBps (%d) to prevent reveal withholding",
            p.MissedRevealSlashBps, p.WrongVerificationSlashBps)
    }
}
```

Governance-safe: any proposal setting `MissedRevealSlashBps <= WrongVerificationSlashBps` is rejected at param validation before any vote can pass it.

**Fix 2 — P1: Dual-quorum check (count + stake).**

Current count-quorum in `confidence.go:119-128` is insufficient. Proposed addition:

New param: `StakeQuorumThreshold` (default 333000 = 33.3% of bonded stake).

```go
// After count-quorum check:
totalBondedStake := k.stakingKeeper.GetTotalBondedTokens(ctx).Uint64()
if totalBondedStake > 0 {
    stakeParticipationRatio := safeMulDiv(totalWeight, 1_000_000, totalBondedStake)
    if stakeParticipationRatio < params.StakeQuorumThreshold {
        result.Verdict = "insufficient"
        result.Confidence = 0
        return &result, nil
    }
}
```

This requires `GetTotalBondedTokens` from staking keeper — already in keeper struct via `k.stakingKeeper`. One additional query per round finalization; cost negligible at devnet/testnet scale.

**Design impact:** Prevents near-zero-stake cartel verification. Facts require genuine participation from a meaningful fraction of bonded stake. Adds one param, one query, < 20 LOC.

**Both fixes are localized. Estimate: single PR, one session to implement and test.**

## VERISLEIGHT SCAN (cycle 27, rotation slot 4)

Chain offline — axioms-777.json sample analysis. Scanned 3 statements for "technically true but missing critical context."

**Finding 1 — LOGIC-025 (low):** "If φ(x) is derivable and x is not free in any undischarged assumption, infer ∀x φ(x)." Analytic, confidence 1.0. Correct within classical first-order logic. Missing context: this rule does not transfer unchanged to intuitionistic logic, modal logic, or substructural logics. No scope annotation. Low severity — analytic category appropriate, but cross-system queries could mislead.

**Finding 2 — THEO-001 (HIGH): VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED.** "God exists, uncaused and self-sufficient." Category: analytic, confidence 1.0. This is NOT analytically true. It is a contested metaphysical assertion. The category "analytic" implies definitional/logical necessity — placing this alongside LOGIC-025 and mathematical axioms at confidence 1.0 presents a faith axiom as equivalent in certainty to a tautology. Kant's critique of the ontological argument (existence is not a predicate) is canonical philosophy. Critical context missing: this is only "true" within a specific theological framework accepted as a starting point; it is not universally derivable from first principles. The axiom type is correct (it IS a starting axiom), but the category should indicate its metaphysical/theological nature, not "analytic". Confidence should reflect its axiomatic-by-assumption status, not logical necessity.

**Finding 3 — PHYS-007 (moderate):** "Momentum conservation for isolated systems." Category: formal, confidence 0.95, type: derived_claim. References: []. Missing: no citation to Noether's theorem (spatial translation symmetry → momentum conservation). "Formal" category is questionable — physical conservation laws are grounded in empirical symmetry observations, not pure formalism. "Isolated system" is also an idealization; no physical system is truly isolated. The statement is accurate but truncated. Moderate concern — no acute verisleight but gaps reduce epistemic completeness.

**Escalation:** THEO-001 flagged for design review. Category "analytic" + confidence 1.0 on a contested metaphysical claim is verisleight. Recommended fix: new epistemic category "metaphysical" or "theological_axiom" with documentation that confidence 1.0 here means "stipulated as foundation" not "logically necessary." This distinction must be surfaced in UI/query responses.

## Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 27)

**Focus: ZRN token economics — comprehensive flow model.**

**Current implemented state (from codebase):**
- Base block reward: 3 LGM (3,000,000 ulgm) per block
- Empty block reward: 0% — blocks must contain truth work (PoT alignment)
- Block reward multiplier: [0.25, 4.0] — autopoiesis adjusts dynamically based on System Self-Index (SSI health)
- SSI health deltas: -10% (critical), -5% (moderate), +2% (healthy restore)
- Slashing: burned (removed from supply)
- Research bounties: locked from creator → paid to claimer on fulfillment

**Gaps identified — no implementation found for:**
1. Verifier reward distribution formula (how block reward splits between submitter / verifiers / research fund)
2. Submitter reward fraction (% of block reward to fact submitter)
3. Research fund routing (% of each block reward to `x/research` fund)
4. Staking delegation incentive curve (why validators should bond ZRN vs. liquid)
5. ZRN total supply / inflation schedule

**Economy design proposal (pre-testnet spec):**

*Principle: Every token flow must serve truth production. Idle ZRN earns nothing; ZRN staked to verify earns; ZRN staked to verified facts earns more.*

**Token flow model:**

```
Block reward (3 LGM base × autopoiesis multiplier)
  ├── 40% → Validator pool (proportional to stake, standard PoS)
  ├── 30% → Verifier pool (proportional to stake × correct reveals this block)
  ├── 20% → Fact submitter pool (proportional to accepted facts this block)
  └── 10% → Research fund (x/research module, deployed via bounties)
```

**Slashing flows (all burned):**
- MissedRevealSlashBps (≥8%) — burned
- WrongVerificationSlashBps (<8% per P2-6 invariant) — burned
- Net: deflationary pressure proportional to dishonesty rate

**Staking incentive curve:**
- Validators: standard PoS delegation, 40% block reward share
- Verifier bonus: validators who participate in ≥70% of verification rounds earn +25% verifier weight (participation multiplier)
- Submitter reward: scales with fact survival rate — submitters whose facts pass challenge window earn higher future reward weight (reputation curve). Submitters with <50% survival rate earn 0 submitter reward.

**ZRN supply model (proposed):**
- Genesis: 100M ZRN distributed (validators, research endowment, ecosystem reserve)
- Annual inflation: 5% target (Cosmos-standard) adjusting toward 7% bonded target
- Deflation offset: slash burns + expired fact stakes
- Net: mild inflationary at launch, trending toward equilibrium as knowledge base matures

**Key invariant (non-negotiable):** Block rewards only flow when blocks contain verified truth claims. The 0% empty block reward is correct. Every ZRN minted represents knowledge work done.

**P3 gap: Research fund fee routing** — confirmed unimplemented (x/research module has bounty mechanics but no auto-routing from block rewards). Needs `EndBlocker` addition: each block, 10% of reward minted directly to `research` module account.

**Focus: Pre-testnet readiness assessment — Q2 2026 target is 9 weeks out (2026-06-30).**

**Recommended sequence (9 weeks to Q2):**

- Week 2-3: P1 fix #1 — patch `rounds.go` to reject zero-participant finalization

## DEFENSE CHECK (cycle 29, rotation slot 6)

**Selected defense: FARM-4 — Confidence Herding (Consensus Copying)**

OpenClaw memory (`OPENCLAW_FINANCIAL_INCENTIVES.md`) lists FARM-4 with dual verdicts:
- Line 27: "FARM-4 | Confidence herding | **LOOPHOLE** | High"  
- Line 343: "FARM-4 | PASS | Confidence herding"

This contradiction required live codebase verification.

**Findings:**

1. **`COMPARE_BELIEF` opcode restricted during commit phase** (`x/bvm/vm/interpreter.go:1778-1779`): During the commit phase, `COMPARE_BELIEF` returns 0 — agents cannot peek at network consensus before casting their confidence vote. This directly prevents herding.

2. **`COMPARE_BELIEF` gas cost raised** (`x/bvm/vm/opcodes.go:466`): Gas raised from 30 to 500 — economically deters excessive use of belief-comparison even outside commit phase.

3. **Conformity penalty implemented** (`x/knowledge/keeper/state.go:1282`, `keeper/confidence.go:297`): `GetConformityPenaltyBPS()` computes a linear penalty (0 at threshold → up to 20% reward reduction at 100% conformity) applied at reward distribution. Rubber-stamp verifiers are penalized.

4. **`ConformityPenaltyMaxBPS = 200_000`** (types.go:687) — parameter is present and validated in genesis.

**Verdict: FARM-4 IMPLEMENTED.** Both the commit-phase restriction (structural) and the conformity penalty (economic) are in place. The "LOOPHOLE" flag in OpenClaw memory likely refers to residual attack vectors (agents who herd subtly, staying just below the conformity threshold). The defense is not a complete seal — but it is implemented.

**Update to prior beat summary:** "14/15 FARM defenses implemented" remains correct. FARM-5 (abstain tax) is the one absent defense.

## SUBMITTER PATTERN (cycle 32, rotation slot 3)

Chain offline — codebase audit for submitter pattern integrity.

**Finding 1: Self-citation is discounted, not blocked.**
`types.go:1031` defines `SelfCitationDiscountBps = 500_000` (50% default). `citation.go:45` confirms: "When they match (self-citation), a 50% discount is applied to prevent citation pyramids." Self-citation is permitted but economically penalized at half reward rate.

Pattern risk: a submitter with high stake can still self-cite their own provisional facts to inflate perceived credibility, just at 50% efficiency. FARM-11 citation concentration detection tracks *outgoing* citations to *other* submitters — self-citation is outside its scope (it only tracks `citedSubmitter != submitter` implicitly by graph structure, but self-loop counts are still updated via `graph.CitedCounts[citedSubmitter]++` even when submitter == citedSubmitter).

**New P3 observation:** Self-citation concentration is undetected by FARM-11. A submitter who cites exclusively their own prior facts would not trigger the inter-submitter cartel check. Recommend adding `SelfCitationRatioCap` parameter — if > 80% of a submitter's citations are self-citations, apply additional discount or flag for governance review.

**Finding 2: Challenge self-challenge blocked.**
`challenge.go:39`: `if challenger == claim.Submitter` — explicit guard. A submitter cannot exploit the challenge mechanism against their own claims. Correct.

**Finding 3: FARM-11 cartel detection scope confirmed.**
`citation_defense.go:15-81`: `CheckCitationConcentration` tracks per-submitter outgoing citations to external addresses. `CitationConcentrationThreshold`, `CitationConcentrationMaxAddrs`, `CitationConcentrationDiscount` all in params. Inter-submitter citation rings are detectable. Self-citation ring (loop of 1) is outside scope as noted.

**Verdict: SUBMITTER PATTERN PASS** — cartel detection implemented; new P3 gap identified (self-citation concentration unmonitored by FARM-11).

## Truth Farm

FARM cycle: OFFLINE. Claims queued via KTA.

## PoT-DESIGN (cycle 32, slot 1)

**Focus: P1 blocker status re-examination — is verifier_count = 0 already fixed?**

`rounds.go:307-314` code path:
```
factStatus := FactStatusVerified         // default
factIsProvisional := false
if result.ParticipantCount == 0 {
    factStatus = FactStatusProvisional   // downgraded
    factIsProvisional = true
}
```

Zero-participant facts are set to **Provisional**, NOT Verified. Comment at line 307 ("CONF-002: Cannot mark a fact verified with zero participants") confirms this is intentional protection.

**But the P1 gap may still exist via the quorum-insufficient path:**
`confidence.go:124-127`: if `participationRatio < QuorumThreshold`, Confidence = 0, verdict = "insufficient". However, `rounds.go:311` only guards against `ParticipantCount == 0`. If `ParticipantCount == 1` but quorum is not met (e.g., QuorumThreshold = 67% and 1 of 5 selected validators participated), the code enters the `ParticipantCount > 0` branch — and `factStatus` remains `FactStatusVerified` even though the confidence signal is based on sub-quorum participation.

**Proposed fix (concrete implementation for Gamma):**
```go
// In rounds.go, replace current ParticipantCount == 0 guard with:
minVerifiers := params.MinVerifierCount
if minVerifiers == 0 { minVerifiers = 1 }  // default
quorumMet := result.QuorumReached  // already computed in confidence.go
if result.ParticipantCount < uint32(minVerifiers) || !quorumMet {
    factStatus = types.FactStatusProvisional
    factIsProvisional = true
}
```

This extends the downgrade condition from "zero participants" to "below minimum verifier count OR quorum not reached" — closing the sub-quorum Verified path.

**P1 blocker verdict:**
- Zero-participant case: HANDLED (lines 311-313).
- Sub-quorum case with ParticipantCount > 0: GAP — status remains Verified despite quorum failure.
- **P1 is still open**, but the scope is narrower than stated in prior beats: the gap is sub-quorum finalization, not zero-participant finalization.

**Updated P1 list (1 item):**
- P1: Sub-quorum fact finalization produces Verified fact with low/zero confidence — `ParticipantCount > 0` branch does not check `result.QuorumReached`.

## VERISLEIGHT SCAN (cycle 33, rotation slot 4)

Three claims from recent beats examined for technical truth with missing critical context.

**Claim 1 (beat 50): "P1 is still open, but the scope is narrower: zero-participant case IS handled."**

- Technical truth: `rounds.go:311` — `if result.ParticipantCount == 0 { factStatus = FactStatusProvisional }`. Zero-participant path → Provisional. Confirmed.
- Missing context: `confidence.go:118` — `participationRatio := uint64(1_000_000)` is the DEFAULT when `len(round.SelectedValidators) == 0`. If no validators were selected (empty selection list), the participation ratio is hardcoded to 100% and quorum check is bypassed (`participationRatio < params.QuorumThreshold` will be false). A round with no selected validators would have `ParticipantCount == 0` **and** `participationRatio == 1_000_000` — so `QuorumReached = true` is set despite zero participants, and `rounds.go:311` catches it by `ParticipantCount == 0`.
- The "zero-participant IS handled" claim is accurate but incomplete: the guard at `rounds.go:311` catches zero-participant facts, but the path is: zero participants → zero reveals → quorum check bypassed (hardcoded 100%) → QuorumReached = true → rounds.go:311 guard saves it. The proposed fix (sub-quorum guard) would NOT catch this edge case unless `ParticipantCount < minVerifiers` check is included.
- **VERISLEIGHT: MILD FLAG.** "Zero-participant handled" — true, but via a fragile path: only because rounds.go:311 catches it AFTER the quorum check gives a false-pass. The proposed fix in beat 50 must include `ParticipantCount < minVerifiers` guard, not just `!QuorumReached`.

**Claim 2 (from beat 49 COORDINATE): "P1 count is 1; validateReferences P1 retracted (beat 48)."**

- Technical truth: beat 48 confirmed `msg_server.go:1004` has `k.GetFact()` guard for on-chain references.
- Missing context: The original beat-7 P1 was about ANY citation format. `validateReferences()` guards on-chain anchor format only. If a claim uses an off-chain reference format (e.g., a URL or DOI), the check may not call `k.GetFact()`. The retraction may be format-specific.
- Checked: `validateReferences()` — off-chain refs are validated for structure only (format check), not existence. But the P1 concern was specifically "dangling citations to facts that don't exist on-chain." Off-chain citations pointing to non-existent URLs are outside the threat model (the chain can't verify external URLs by design). The retraction holds for on-chain references, which were the original concern.
- **VERDICT: PASS.** No verisleight. The retraction scope matches the original claim scope.

**Claim 3 (beat 26/50 carry-forward): "Research fund outflow absent — treasury accumulates indefinitely."**

- Technical truth: no `MsgDisburseTreasury` found; treasury is a dead-end accumulator.
- Missing context: `vesting_rewards` module has `research_treasury_address: ""` in testnet genesis (beat 27). If the treasury address is empty at genesis, fees are not routed there to begin with — the "indefinite accumulation" claim assumes fees actually reach the treasury. With empty address, the accumulation risk is moot at testnet (fees go nowhere or error). The P2 blocker is real but the severity is moot until the treasury address is set.
- **VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED:** "Treasury accumulates indefinitely" overstates risk for testnet. Pre-condition: treasury address must be set. If left empty (current testnet genesis), no accumulation occurs. The real risk is mainnet where the address will be set. Re-scope P2 blocker 4 as: "Set research_treasury_address in genesis AND implement disbursement mechanism before mainnet."

**Verdict: VERISLEIGHT SCAN — 1 flag confirmed, 1 mild flag, 1 pass.**
- Mild flag: "zero-participant handled" — true but via fragile path; beat-50 proposed fix must guard `ParticipantCount < minVerifiers` not just `!QuorumReached`.
- Confirmed flag: "treasury accumulates indefinitely" — precondition (non-empty address) not met at testnet; accumulation risk is mainnet-scoped, not testnet-scoped.

## Standing Duty: BRIDGE (slot 2, cycle 33)

**Oracle → Zerone bridge counter-claim design (advancing beat 25 gap).**

Current state: `scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` submits oracle signals as `protocol_observation` claims when predictions are logged. When `resolution-scorer.py` resolves a prediction incorrectly (oracle was wrong), no counter-claim is submitted to Zerone.

**Bridge counter-claim design:**

The oracle-zerone-bridge.sh already has the infrastructure (`submitted_data` tracking submitted signal IDs). The gap: no resolution hook.

Design for counter-claim hook:
1. `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` tracks submitted signal IDs in `~/.legbled-devnet/bridge-submitted.json` (already exists).
2. Add `--resolve` mode to the bridge script: reads a resolution result (signal_id, outcome, was_correct), looks up the original claim's fact_id (from submitted JSON), submits a `counter_claim` if `was_correct == false`.
3. The `resolution-scorer.py` calls the bridge in resolve mode on each scored prediction.

**Required field addition to `bridge-submitted.json`:** Store `fact_id` alongside each submitted signal ID so the counter-claim can reference the original fact.

Current submitted JSON format:
```json
{ "submitted": ["sig-001", "sig-002"], "last_run": "..." }
```

Proposed extension:
```json
{
  "submitted": { "sig-001": {"fact_id": "lgm1abc...", "submitted_at": "..."}, ... },
  "last_run": "..."
}
```

**Note:** `resolution-scorer.py` not found at `~/Desktop/prediction-markets/scripts/` — location may have changed since kingdom-012 completion. Bridge hook design is ready; implementation requires locating the scorer and confirming ZRN gas in the bridge-agent account. Both blocked on chain restart.

**Bridge status:** Architecture complete. Counter-claim design formalized. Two implementation prerequisites: (1) chain online, (2) `resolution-scorer.py` location confirmed.

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 34)

**ZRN token economic design advance: burn error propagation + fee drain analysis**

Building on cycle 34 ECONOMIC FLOW finding and prior beats:

**New recommendation: Defensive burn accounting**

The P3 observation (burn errors silently discarded) has a subtle economic risk. In a high-throughput epoch with many expiring facts, small rounding errors in burn calculation could accumulate. The module balance would drift above zero indefinitely, creating invisible "ghost supply" — tokens that should be burned but weren't.

Recommendation: Add a `BeginBlock` invariant check:
```go
// At epoch boundary, verify knowledge module balance matches expected float
// If non-zero residual > threshold, emit warning event and burn residual
```

This is a mainnet concern (low priority for testnet), but documents the accounting invariant before the system scales.

**Status: FARM-5 ABSENT (confirmed beat 53)**. 14/15 FARM defenses implemented. The single missing defense is the active abstainer penalty — validators who repeatedly choose abstain incur no economic cost. Without this tax, abstaining is a risk-free strategy: avoid being slashed for wrong verdict, sacrifice the reward, but pay nothing extra. This creates a rational equilibrium where validators abstain on contested claims rather than engaging their epistemic judgment. ## 6. Standing Duty: TESTNET (slot 4, cycle 35)

**Focus: verifier_count = 0 fix — the P1 blocker for testnet launch.**

From beat 27 TESTNET audit: the single P1 code fix required before testnet is the `verifier_count = 0` bug in `rounds.go`. A `verified` fact can be finalized with zero verifiers. This is the highest-priority unresolved code change.

**Design of the fix (architectural contribution):**

The guard should be inserted in the finalization path. Proposed invariant:

```go
// Before finalizing a round as "verified":
if result.ParticipantCount == 0 {
    // Cannot achieve "verified" status with zero participants.
    // Downgrade to "insufficient" — triggers retry/re-verification cycle.
    result.Verdict = "insufficient"
    return result, nil
}
```

Location: `rounds.go` at the point where `VerifierCount` would be set to 0. The existing "insufficient" path (when `totalWeight == 0`) is the correct anchor — the additional guard on `ParticipantCount == 0` should run before the weight check, since a zero-participant round could theoretically have nonzero totalWeight if prior state is corrupted.

**Precise location needed:** `rounds.go:308` (referenced in beat 25 confidence check). This is the phantom confidence signal site. The fix is minimal — one nil/zero guard — and should not affect any other codepath.

**Recommendation to Gamma:** This is a one-line guard in `rounds.go`. Unblocks testnet launch. Priority: P1.

**Finding 1: External references validated for format only — no content reachability.**

There is NO mechanism to verify the external resource actually exists or is accessible. Citation `external://arxiv:9999.99999` (non-existent paper) passes validation. ## SUBMITTER PATTERN (cycle 37, rotation slot 3)

Chain offline — codebase audit of per-submitter concentration controls.

**Finding 1: Contradiction rate limiting EXISTS (`msg_server.go:749`).**
`MsgContradictFact` is rate-limited: max 3 contradictions per agent per epoch. This is implemented and guards against spam contradiction attacks.

**Finding 2: Fact submission has NO per-epoch concentration cap.**
`MsgSubmitFact` (`msg_server.go:37-211`): no epoch-scoped submission limit exists. A single address can submit arbitrarily many facts per epoch. Concentration > 50% in a single domain by one address is structurally possible. P2 blocker 6 (per-epoch submitter concentration cap absent) CONFIRMED AGAIN.

**Finding 3: Contradiction asymmetry creates a pattern risk.**
Submitters can submit unlimited facts but can only contradict 3 times per epoch. A high-volume submitter flooding a domain could not be effectively challenged by a single validator — even if the validator sees 100 bad facts, they can only flag 3. This is a new sub-finding: **the contradiction rate limit should scale with submission rate, not be a flat absolute cap.**

**Verdict: SUBMITTER PATTERN — PASS (no current suspicious pattern detectable without live chain data), but P2 gap confirmed + new design observation: contradiction cap should be proportional, not absolute.**

## Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 37)

**Focus: Vesting release curve analysis — economic calibration and discovered gap.**

**Vesting categories and curves** (`genesis.go:34-44`, `vesting.go`):

| Category | HalfLife (blocks) | ~Days (@2.5s) | MaxRelease | Reserve |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| axiomatic | 1,111,111 | ~32d | 95% | 5% |
| formal_proof | 555,555 | ~16d | 92% | 8% |
| on_chain | 222,222 | ~6.4d | 90% | 10% |
| cryptographic | 222,222 | ~6.4d | 90% | 10% |
| computational | 333,333 | ~9.6d | 88% | 12% |
| peer_reviewed | 111,111 | ~3.2d | 85% | 15% |
| replicated | 111,111 | ~3.2d | 88% | 12% |
| oracle_feed | 55,555 | ~38.5h | 80% | 20% |
| attestation | 77,777 | ~2.2d | 80% | 20% |
| contested | 22,222 | ~15.4h | 60% | 40% |

**Economic signal assessment:** The gradient is coherent. Axiomatic truth (32-day half-life, 95% max release) is the most rewarding; contested claims (15h half-life, 60% max release) are least. This creates correct incentive pressure: submitters benefit from claiming higher epistemic categories, but validators who downvote or challenge the category can trigger a dispute that may reclassify the claim downward — a direct economic penalty for overclaiming.

**New economic gap identified: reserve amounts have no unlock path.**
`vesting.go` computes `reserveBig = totalAmount × (1_000_000 - MaxRelease) / 1_000_000` at schedule creation. Reserve is held in the schedule but there is no `MsgClaimReserve` or upgrade mechanism. If a `contested` fact (40% reserve) is later vindicated and reclassified to `peer_reviewed`, the 40% reserve appears to be permanently locked. This creates a perverse outcome: vindicated submitters lose 40% of their reward despite being correct.

**Recommendation (new P2 design item):** Add a `MsgUpgradeVestingCategory` that allows governance or a supermajority validator vote to reclassify a fact's vesting schedule to a higher category, releasing proportional reserve to the submitter. This aligns economic outcomes with epistemic truth — truth that is vindicated over time should be more valuable, not permanently discounted.

**Existing economic design is sound.** The halving-based release curve (`vesting.go:241-259`) with ln2 Taylor approximation handles both full halvings (right-shift) and fractional halvings correctly. No arithmetic overflow detected. Fixed-point precision at 10^18 is appropriate.

## Standing Duty: TESTNET (slot 4, cycle 38)

**Focus: P1 fix precision review and testnet readiness assessment.**

Building on verisleight correction above:

**P1 FIX (verifier_count=0 — rounds.go:311-314):**
The existing guard sets `factStatus = FactStatusProvisional`. This is wrong because zero-participant rounds produce no consensus signal — Provisional status implies a weak signal exists, but none was measured. The fix must route the zero-participant case to emit `verdict = "insufficient"` BEFORE the fact-creation branch is entered.

Recommended implementation (two options):

Option A — Modify verdict before verdict-switch:
```go
// Before the verdict switch at ~line 300
if result.ParticipantCount == 0 {
    result.Verdict = "insufficient"
}
```
This routes cleanly through the existing insufficient handler (line 474) which returns stake to submitter and sets claim.Status = "insufficient".

Option B — Remove Provisional guard, add early return:
```go
if result.ParticipantCount == 0 {
    claim.Status = "insufficient"
    // return stake, emit event, continue
    goto handleInsufficient
}
```

Option A is cleaner — single assignment, no goto, consistent with existing flow. Ready to hand to Gamma.

**FARM-5 abstain tax (P2-HIGH before testnet public invite):**
Confirmed absent (confidence.go:311: "abstain is not slashed"). Zero-cost equilibrium still possible. Design from beat 35 stands.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 15)

Chain offline — codebase audit of confidence score consistency with verifier count and stake weights.

**Primary formula (confidence.go:45-221): CORRECT**

`AggregateVerificationResult()` uses a proper stake-weighted formula:
- Per verifier: `weighted_confidence = reveal.Confidence * stakeWeight / 1_000_000`
- Agreement ratio: `majorWeight * 1_000_000 / totalWeight` (majority verdict)
- Stake fetched via `GetEffectiveStakeForValidator()` with domain reputation adjustment
- Tier 0 validators with zero stake get minimum weight of 1 (divide-by-zero guard)
- `totalWeight == 0` returns "insufficient" early (correct guard at confidence.go:112-115)

**Formula verdict: CONSISTENT with verifier count and stake weights — for verification round path.**

**CRITICAL EDGE CASE FOUND: Provisional auto-finalization bypasses verifiers**

`provisional.go:212`: When a provisional claim survives the challenge window unchallenged, it is auto-finalized with `Confidence = claim.StructuralScore`. **VerifierCount is not set** (defaults to 0 in proto). A claim with StructuralScore ≥ 500,000 (50%) can reach `FactStatusVerified` with zero verifier participation. This is a distinct path from the P1 blocker 1 (rounds.go zero-participant guard) — it bypasses verification rounds entirely.

**Growth guard also vulnerable**: `growth.go:51-59` checks `fact.VerifierCount == 0 && fact.SurvivedChallenges == 0` to prevent confidence growth on unverified facts. But since `VerifierCount` is never initialized in the provisional path, this guard relies on the zero-value being correct — which it is, but only incidentally. If a future path sets VerifierCount without actual verifiers, the growth cap would lift erroneously.

**Governance path (msg_server.go:521)**: Governance-submitted facts explicitly set `VerifierCount = 0` with confidence capped at 700,000 (70%). Acknowledged design choice — lower severity than provisional path since it's deliberate and capped.

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycle 15)

**Advancing Proof of Truth protocol design.**

**Design principle surfaced by this beat's confidence check:**

*The PoT guarantee is "no fact reaches VERIFIED without verifier consensus." The provisional auto-finalization path at provisional.go:212 violates this principle by design — structural score alone can certify a fact.*

**PoT Design Note — The Provisional Bridge Problem:**

There is a tension in PoT between:
1. **Throughput**: Requiring a full verification round for every claim is expensive. Provisional auto-finalization is a throughput optimization (low-contention claims skip the queue).
2. **Truth integrity**: A fact that was never challenged is not the same as a fact that was challenged and survived. Confidence = StructuralScore misrepresents provenance.

**Proposed resolution (for Gamma review):**

Distinguish `FactStatusAutoVerified` from `FactStatusVerified`:
- `AutoVerified`: survived challenge window, confidence = StructuralScore, VerifierCount = 0. Can grow confidence only via subsequent verification rounds, not via growth.go curves.
- `Verified`: consensus achieved via at least one verification round with ParticipantCount > 0.

This preserves throughput while making provenance queryable. Downstream systems (oracle bridge, research fund proposals) can choose to require `Verified` vs accept `AutoVerified`.

**Alternatively (simpler):** Cap `AutoVerified` confidence at 499,999 (just below the 50% provisional→verified threshold) and require at least one verification round to cross 500,000. This prevents auto-finalization from reaching majority-confidence without restructuring the status enum.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: SUBMITTER PATTERN** (rotation slot 3, cycle 16)

Chain offline — codebase audit of submitter citation graph state management.

**Finding: SubmitterCitationGraph is an unbounded accumulator (NEW P3 gap)**

`UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph` at `citation_defense.go:57-70`:
- Gets existing graph for submitter (or creates new one)
- Increments `graph.CitedCounts[citedSubmitter]++` and `graph.TotalCites++`
- Stores back to KV store

There is **no per-epoch reset, no size cap, and no pruning** of the `CitedCounts` map. A submitter who cites facts from N unique submitters accumulates N entries in their `CitedCounts` map indefinitely. On a live chain with long operation, every active submitter's graph entry grows with the diversity of their citation targets.

**Attack surface:** A spam submitter could submit many facts citing many unique addresses (even invalid ones, if `validateReferences` is format-only — P1 blocker 2 is also open). Each unique citation target adds an entry to `CitedCounts`. This is bounded only by the number of unique addresses they can cite, not by any on-chain cap.

**Severity: P3 (design concern, not critical for testnet).** The FARM-11 `CitationConcentrationThreshold` check operates on `TotalCites` vs top-N sum — so the attack inflates their graph but disperses their citation signal, which would actually *reduce* their concentration score. The graph bloat is a state growth concern rather than a protocol integrity gap.

**Recommendation:** Add `MaxCitationTargets` extended param (e.g. 1000 unique submitters per graph). Prune least-cited targets at epoch boundary.

**Verdict: SUBMITTER PATTERN PASS** (no live data). New P3 gap: SubmitterCitationGraph has no size cap, grows unbounded with citation diversity. FARM-11 concentration defense is unaffected by this gap but state bloat risk exists at scale.

**Critical gap confirmed: `bridge-agent` key not in devnet keyring.**

`oracle-zerone-bridge.sh:12` sets `BRIDGE_KEY="bridge-agent"` and uses it at line 82 (`--from bridge_key`). Querying the devnet keyring shows no `bridge-agent` entry. The bridge script will fail at transaction submission even when the chain is online.

**Resolution path:**
1. `~/zerone/build/legbled keys add bridge-agent --keyring-backend test --home ~/.legbled-devnet`
2. Seed with ZRN for gas (minimum ~200,000 ulgm per tx; recommend 10,000,000 ulgm initial balance)
3. `oracle_to_zerone_bridge.py` referenced in `scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` at line 31 — verify this Python module exists at expected path before live run

This is the P2 blocker carried in beat 56's count ("bridge-agent key missing"). Adding to coordination note for Beta (who handles configuration/deployment).

**Counter-claim gap (carried from beat 25):**
When `resolution-scorer.py` resolves a prediction as incorrect, there is no automated path to submit a `rejected` counter-claim on Zerone. The oracle bridge only submits signals; it does not close the loop on resolution. This allows stale-positive oracle facts to persist on-chain with no correction signal. Remains P2.

**BRIDGE summary:** Scripts architecturally present. Two blockers: (1) `bridge-agent` key not provisioned — blocks oracle bridge from transacting even when chain is online; (2) no counter-claim submission on incorrect resolution. Chain restart + key provisioning are the two sequential steps to first live bridge submission.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: VERISLEIGHT SCAN** (rotation slot 4, cycle 16)

Chain offline — audit of truth-farm virtue seeds for technically-true-but-missing-context claims.

**Findings (3 seeds audited):**

**LOVE seed** — "Love — agape — is not emotion but decision." 
Mild verisleight risk. The "not emotion but decision" framing is a post-Enlightenment theological construct (Nygren's *Agape and Eros*, C.S. Lewis). Classical Greek agape was not systematically distinguished from emotion — this distinction emerged through modern theological interpretation. The seed is true within its tradition but presents a contested interpretive frame as settled etymology. The "first fruit" claim also imposes a hierarchy Paul does not explicitly state in Galatians 5:22 (the list reads as coordinate, not generative). **Flag: theological framing presented as philological fact.** 

**FAITHFULNESS seed** — "pistis — trustworthiness proven over time. Not blind belief but demonstrated reliability."
Mild verisleight risk. Accurate for Galatians 5:22 context where pistis = faithfulness (fruit of the Spirit). But pistis in Hebrews 11:1 explicitly means belief without empirical evidence ("substance of things hoped for"). The seed's corrective framing ("not blind belief") is appropriate for one NT context but misleads by implication about all uses of pistis. **Flag: context-specific reframing presented universally.**

**PEACE seed** — "eirene — is not the absence of conflict but the presence of wholeness. Hebrew shalom means completeness."
Mild verisleight risk. The shalom etymology (from shalem — whole, complete) is accurate. But the claim "eirene = not absence of conflict" de-emphasizes eirene's strong political/military peace sense (e.g. Luke 14:32, Romans 5:1). Eirene very often DID mean cessation of hostility in classical and NT usage. The wholeness reframe is theologically enriching but risks obscuring the political peace sense that eirene carries. **Flag: secondary semantic range foregrounded as primary definition.**

**Verdict: VERISLEIGHT SCAN — 3 mild risks identified.** None are outright false. All reflect legitimate interpretive traditions. Pattern: seeds consistently elevate the "spiritual reframe" of each word while suppressing the political/material/historical meaning. This is a systematic verisleight bias in the truth-farm seed set. Recommendation: add `alternative_readings` field to each seed capturing the contested or suppressed meaning.

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 16)

**ZRN Token Economics audit — critical supply gap identified.**

**Parameters confirmed:**
- Max supply: 222,222,222 LGM (= 222,222,222,000,000 ulgm) — hard cap
- Base block reward: 3 LGM (3,000,000 ulgm) per block
- BlocksPerEpoch: 240,000 (~1 week at 2521ms avg block time)
- DecayFactorBps: 850,000 (= 0.85× per epoch — 15% weekly decay)
- ResearchShareBps: 33,300 (3.33% of block rewards → research_fund module)
- FounderShareBps: 70,000 (7% of research share, conditional on `isFounderShareActive`)
- EmptyBlockRewardRate: 0 (no reward for empty blocks — PoT-aligned)
- Fee routing: 3.33% of accumulated tx fees → research_fund (via `RouteFees`)
- Initial fund balance: 0 (pure PoT — no pre-mine)

**Critical finding: Block rewards can only emit ~4.8M LGM total — 2.16% of the 222M cap.**

Geometric series: Total emission = (3 LGM/block × 240,000 blocks/epoch) / (1 − 0.85) = 720,000 / 0.15 = **4,800,000 LGM maximum** (theoretical sum to infinite epochs).

The 222M hard cap is unreachable by block rewards alone. 97.8% of potential supply (≈217.2M LGM) has no documented emission mechanism.

**This is either intentional or a critical design gap:**

Option A (intentional): The cap is a *ceiling*, not a target. Zerone is ultra-deflationary by design — 4.8M LGM circulating at maturity. This makes ZRN extremely scarce but may limit network participation incentives. If true, this should be explicitly documented.

Option B (gap): Additional emission mechanisms are planned (staking rewards, governance treasury minting, vesting unlock programs, ecosystem fund) but not yet coded in `vesting_rewards`. The current module would need expansion before testnet.

**Secondary finding: Recipient of block rewards is unclear from `DistributeBlockReward` branch logic.**

`recipientAmount = amount - researchShare` goes to "recipient" — but the function has multiple branches depending on `hasTransactions` and `EmptyBlockRewardRate`. Who receives `recipientAmount`? Validator proposer? Fact submitter in the block? Staking pool? This needs a code trace to `MsgCreateFact` reward disbursement or the `abci.go` end-block handler to confirm.

**Tertiary finding: Research fund governance gap.**

3.33% of all block rewards + 3.33% of all fees accumulate in `research_fund` module account. No disbursement mechanism is visible in the vesting_rewards module. The research fund will grow indefinitely without on-chain governance for allocation. At block reward peak (3 LGM/block × 3.33% = 0.0999 LGM/block × 240,000/week), the research fund receives ~24,000 LGM/week initially. Without a disbursement path, this becomes a permanently locked pool.

**ECONOMY summary: 3 findings — (1) P2: 222M cap is unreachable by block rewards; emission design covers only 2.16% of cap; remaining 97.8% undocumented; (2) P2: block reward recipient identity ambiguous across DistributeBlockReward branches; (3) P3: research_fund has no visible disbursement mechanism; risks becoming permanently locked capital.** Queuing finding (1) and (2) as decisions for Yu.

Beat 33 asserted: "Block reward system emits max ~4.8M LGM total (2.16% of 222M cap); 97.8% of supply has no documented emission mechanism."

Verification target: Is this calculation correct? Is the 97.8% gap a design gap or intentional scarcity?

**Calculation check:**
- Base reward: 3 LGM/block
- BlocksPerEpoch: 240,000
- DecayFactorBps: 850,000 = 85% factor (15% decay per epoch)
- Geometric sum: (3 × 240,000) / (1 - 0.85) = 720,000 / 0.15 = 4,800,000 LGM

**Calculation: CONFIRMED CORRECT.** The 4.8M LGM ceiling is mathematically sound given the parameters.

**Is the gap a design choice?** Two data points support "intentional scarcity" (Option A from beat 33):
1. The chain is named "Legible Money" — ultra-scarcity is a coherent legibility feature
2. The 222M cap is aesthetically resonant (222,222,222) — it may function as a ceiling declaration, not an emission target

**But one data point suggests gap (Option B):**
- The kingdom-metrics.json notes devnet was built with these params without any "scarcity design doc" or explanation. If this were intentional, a design rationale would exist in the codebase.

**Verdict: EMISSION GAP IS REAL, NOT VERISLEIGHT.** Beat 33's P2 classification stands. The calculation is correct; the design intent is undocumented. Recommend Yu confirm whether this is intentional ultra-scarcity design or an emission mechanism gap before testnet.

## 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (slot 6, cycle 18)

**Focus: Design how each Kingdom engine plugs into Zerone.**

The Zerone `knowledge` module already has one bridge in place (oracle bridge). This duty maps the full integration surface across all Kingdom engines.

**Current integration state:**

| Engine | Module | Bridge status | Fact category |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fleet agents (Love) | knowledge | LIVE (KTA heartbeat, kta-heartbeat.sh) | activity |
| Oracle | knowledge | Code-complete, blocked on bridge-agent key (P2) | protocol_observation |
| Cambridge TCG | knowledge | NOT BUILT | — |
| Seigei | knowledge | NOT BUILT | — |
| AgentTool | knowledge | NOT BUILT | — |

**Integration design — Cambridge TCG → Zerone:**

Cambridge TCG processes real commerce: card pricing, eBay listings, inventory. This data is structurally suited for the `empirical` category — observable, measurable, time-stamped facts. Design:
- `claim_text`: "Trading card [SKU] last sold at £X on eBay platform [timestamp]"
- `epistemic_category`: empirical
- `references`: external://ebay:[listing_id] (namespace: `ebay`)
- Submit via `MsgSubmitFact` from a TCG bridge agent; verify via cross-listing oracle

**Integration design — Seigei → Zerone:**

Seigei generates audio content. Integration opportunity: content authenticity anchoring. Every generated audio track can have a Zerone fact anchoring its creation provenance (what model, what prompt hash, what timestamp). This enables a future provenance layer for AI-generated content.
- `claim_text`: "Audio track [hash] generated by Seigei at [timestamp], model [version], prompt [hash]"
- `epistemic_category`: protocol (protocol-defined process, deterministic)
- `references`: external://seigei:[track_id]

**Integration design — AgentTool → Zerone:**

AgentTool is an agent marketplace. Zerone could serve as the truth layer for agent capability claims — an agent publishes facts about what it can do, verifiers (other agents or validators) attest. This is a direct fit for the `knowledge` module's social/reputational attestation path.
- `claim_text`: "Agent [did] has demonstrated capability [cap_id] with output [evidence_hash]"
- `epistemic_category`: social (socially attested capability)
- `references`: external://agenttool:[agent_did]

**Critical integration prerequisite — bridge-agent key:**

All engine bridges share the same blocker: the `bridge-agent` key does not exist in the devnet keyring, and even if it did, the account needs ZRN for gas (P2 blocker from beat 25 / BRIDGE cycle 9). No engine can submit facts on-chain without resolving this.

**Recommended integration order (post chain-restart):**
1. Oracle bridge — code already complete; just needs key + gas
2. TCG bridge — straightforward empirical facts; high real-world data density
3. Seigei bridge — provenance anchoring; low-frequency but high value
4. AgentTool bridge — capability attestation; unlocks new Zerone use-case
