This doubly weights stake and individual confidence — by design. Internally consistent.

2. **S4 opacity penalty CONFIRMED.** Lines 573-575: when all validators omit `VerificationMethod` (`declaredCount == 0`), `SingleMethodPenaltyBps` is applied. Cartel can no longer bypass the 15% uniformity penalty by coordinating to leave methods blank. Fix is live.

3. **S4 missed-reveal fix CONFIRMED.** Line 267: `missedSlashBps = 80000` (8%) > `wrongSlashBps = 60000` (6%). The rational choice is to reveal (even wrongly) rather than withhold. Reveal-withholding vulnerability closed.

4. **Deterministic iteration CONFIRMED.** `sortedMapKeys` is used on every map iteration in state-modifying paths (stakeMap building, verdict accumulation, reward/slash distribution). AppHash divergence across validators is prevented.

5. **P1 path DOES NOT flow through confidence.go.** When `len(round.Reveals) == 0`, the for loops don't run, `totalWeight = 0`, and line 112-115 returns `verdict = "insufficient"` immediately. Zero-participant rounds are safely handled here. The P1 blocker (facts verified with `verifier_count = 0`) must be in a separate `rounds.go` finalization path that bypasses or ignores `AggregateVerificationResult`. P1 is NOT in this file.

6. **NEW P3 observation — unaudited evidence pre-claims bonus.** `computeEvidenceBonus` (line 491-495): evidence with `reliability == 0` receives a 10% base reliability (`100_000`) before any audit completes. Attacker submitting N unaudited evidence items can drive the average score toward the 15% cap (`MaxEvidenceBonus`) before verification happens. Cap prevents full exploitation but partial gaming is possible. Recommend: restrict evidence bonus contributions to `status == "verified"` only; treat `status == "submitted"` as zero contribution pending audit.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: SUBMITTER PATTERN** (rotation slot 3, cycle 20)

Chain offline — codebase audit of submitter concentration controls.

**Finding 1: FARM-11 (citation concentration) is DEAD CODE in production paths.**

`citation_defense.go` implements:
- `GetSubmitterCitationGraph` / `SetSubmitterCitationGraph` / `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph` — full graph tracking
- `CheckCitationConcentration` — computes top-N concentration score (threshold 80%)
- `ApplyCitationConcentrationPenalty` — reduces citation reward for concentrated submitters

Grep result: `CheckCitationConcentration` and `ApplyCitationConcentrationPenalty` are called **only in `citation_defense_test.go`**. Zero calls in `msg_server.go`, `confidence.go`, `rounds.go`, or any production path. The functions are built and tested but **never invoked during transaction processing**.

**Consequence:** A submitter can build a citation ring (cite only their own facts or a cartel's facts) with zero penalty. FARM-11 offers no protection until its enforcement functions are wired into the citation reward distribution path.

**Fix path:** In the citation reward distribution step (wherever `CitationShareBPS = 15%` is applied to verifiers who cited the fact), call `ApplyCitationConcentrationPenalty(ctx, submitter, baseReward)` before transferring rewards. The graph must also be updated via `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph` at each citation event.

**New gap classification: P2** (should be wired before testnet exposes external submitters to citation ring attacks).

**Finding 2: P1 BLOCKER 1 RETRACTION — verifier_count=0 bug is ALREADY FIXED.**

`rounds.go:307-313`:
```go
// CONF-002: Cannot mark a fact verified with zero participants.
// Zero-participant finalization produces phantom confidence signals.
factStatus := types.FactStatusVerified
factIsProvisional := false
if result.ParticipantCount == 0 {
    factStatus = types.FactStatusProvisional
    factIsProvisional = true
}
```

The CONF-002 guard IS live code. Zero-participant finalization produces **Provisional** facts, not **Verified** facts. `VerifierCount` is set to `result.ParticipantCount` (0) — accurately reflecting no participation. The prior P1 blocker assessment ("verified facts pass with verifier_count = 0") was incorrect — the fix is already applied. **P1 blocker 1 is RETRACTED.**

**Verdict: SUBMITTER PATTERN — significant findings. P1-1 retracted; FARM-11 confirmed dead code (new P2).**

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycle 20)

**Focus: Testnet critical path with updated blocker list + validateReferences fix spec**

With P1 blocker 1 retracted, the **sole remaining P1 is validateReferences** (beat 7, confirmed beat 30).

**Complete fix specification for the last P1:**

File: `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go`

Change: Convert `validateReferences` from a free function to a `msgServer` method with `ctx`:

```go
// Before (free function, format-only):
func validateReferences(refs []string) error {
    for _, ref := range refs {
        if !isValidCitationAnchor(ref) {
            return fmt.Errorf("invalid citation anchor %q: ...", ref)
        }
    }
    return nil
}

// After (method with fact existence check):
func (k msgServer) validateReferences(ctx sdk.Context, refs []string) error {
    for _, ref := range refs {
        if !isValidCitationAnchor(ref) {
            return fmt.Errorf("invalid citation anchor %q", ref)
        }
        if !strings.HasPrefix(ref, "external://") {
            if _, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref); !found {
                return fmt.Errorf("cited fact %q does not exist", ref)
            }
        }
    }
    return nil
}
```

Call site updates (2 locations, both have `ctx` available):
- `msg_server.go:64` (SubmitFact): `if err := k.validateReferences(ctx, msg.References); err != nil {`
- `msg_server.go:498` (UpdateFact): `if err := k.validateReferences(ctx, msg.References); err != nil {`

**Effort:** ~15 lines of code change. Low risk. No new dependencies. No migration needed (existing facts with dangling citations are unaffected retroactively; only NEW submissions are validated). **This is a 1-session Gamma BUILD task.**

**New PoT design principle emerging from this beat:**

The pattern of "function built, tested, but never wired" (FARM-11 this beat; multi-level citation propagation prior beats) suggests a systematic gap: defense functions are implemented in isolation but not integrated into the transaction lifecycle. Recommend a **wiring audit** before testnet: enumerate all defense functions in `x/knowledge/keeper/` and verify each has a call site in either `msg_server.go` or `endblock.go`. Missing call sites = dead defenses.

**Pre-testnet critical path (updated):**
1. `validateReferences` P1 fix — 1 session Gamma (unblocks testnet)
2. Wire FARM-11 citation concentration into reward distribution — 1 session Gamma (P2)
3. Chain restart — unblocks all live testing

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: VERISLEIGHT SCAN** (rotation slot 4, cycle 21)

Chain offline — auditing `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` for technically-true-but-misleading claims in the PoT design.

**Verisleight Finding 1: `result.Confidence` is labelled "Confidence" but semantics are agreement ratio**

The field `result.Confidence` is computed as `agreementRatio = majority_weight / total_weight` (stake-weighted votes for winning verdict / total stake). The name "Confidence" implies "how sure we are this fact is true." The actual meaning is "how much stake-weighted agreement existed among verifiers who participated."

These diverge in two important cases:
- A fact verified by 1 high-stake validator at 99% personal confidence → `result.Confidence = 990000` (99%). Appears highly confident; is actually a single-verifier rubber stamp.
- A fact verified by 100 validators at 51% majority agreement → `result.Confidence = 510000` (51%). Appears barely confident; represents broad participation with genuine disagreement.

The confidence score is a **consensus signal**, not an **epistemic certainty estimate**. This is verisleight: the name produces a false impression of what the number means.

**Verisleight Finding 2: Stratum ceiling makes cross-domain confidence comparisons invalid**

`confidence.go:162-164`: `result.Confidence` is clamped by the domain's stratum ceiling. A fact in a high-certainty domain (e.g., mathematics) with 95% agreement is clamped lower if the domain stratum ceiling is 80%. A different fact in a different domain with 75% agreement could score higher if its stratum ceiling is higher.

Consequence: comparing `fact_A.confidence > fact_B.confidence` across domains does not mean A is better-verified than B. The score encodes domain constraints, not just verification outcome. Any downstream consumer (oracle bridge, dashboard, application code) that sorts or compares facts across domains by confidence score is operating on verisleight data.

**Verisleight Finding 3: Evidence bonus as "audited evidence" may not require external audit**

`confidence.go:179-186`: Evidence bonus is described as rewarding claims "supported by high-quality, audited evidence" (per the comment). The bonus is drawn from `x/evidence_mgmt`. If `evidence_mgmt` accepts self-submitted evidence (as suggested by the `submit-evidence` tx pattern implied by the module), the phrase "audited evidence" is technically accurate (it passed the module) but creates a false impression of external verification.

**Remediation recommendations:**
1. Rename or annotate: `result.Confidence` → document as "stake-weighted agreement ratio" in proto fields and API responses; add a `participant_count` field alongside for consumers to assess verifier diversity.
2. Add a cross-domain comparison warning to API docs: "Confidence scores are domain-normalized and should not be compared across domains."
3. Audit `x/evidence_mgmt` submission flow: confirm whether evidence can be self-submitted; if so, add an "external_audit" flag to the evidence record and only apply the bonus when `external_audit = true`.

**Verdict: VERISLEIGHT SCAN — 3 findings. All non-critical (no code is broken; semantics are misaligned with user expectations). Classified P3 (documentation + naming fixes before mainnet).**

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (slot 2, cycle 21)

**Focus: Counter-claim resolution hook — spec for wiring oracle resolution into the bridge**

Beat 25 identified this gap: "oracle bridge lacks counter-claim submission on incorrect prediction resolution." This beat advances the spec.

**Context:** `scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` submits oracle prediction signals as `protocol_observation` claims on Zerone. Once submitted, these claims accumulate verifier confidence. If the prediction later resolves WRONG, the `protocol_observation` claim sits on-chain with potentially high confidence — a permanent false signal. No automated mechanism corrects it.

**Counter-claim architecture spec:**

The fix requires a hook in the Oracle resolution pipeline. The resolution scorer lives at `~/Desktop/prediction-markets/scripts/` (not found in this beat's scan — may not exist yet). The hook design is:

```python
# In resolution-scorer.py, after resolving a prediction:
def on_prediction_resolved(prediction_id, resolved_correct):
    if not resolved_correct:
        # Find the on-chain claim ID for this prediction signal
        submitted = json.loads(Path(SUBMITTED_FILE).read_text())
        claim_id = submitted.get("claim_ids", {}).get(prediction_id)
        if claim_id:
            # Submit counter-claim via bridge
            subprocess.run([
                binary, "tx", "knowledge", "submit-claim",
                f"Counter-claim: prediction signal {prediction_id} resolved INCORRECT.",
                "economics", "counter_claim", "500000",
                "--references", claim_id,  # cite original claim
                "--from", bridge_key, ...
            ])
```

**Required bridge changes to support this:**
1. `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` must record submitted claim IDs (txhash → claim_id mapping) in `bridge-submitted.json`. Currently only tracks `sig_id → submitted` boolean. Need to add `claim_id` field per submission.
2. A new `oracle-zerone-counter.sh` script (or inline in bridge) to read resolution results and submit counter-claims.
3. `resolution-scorer.py` must call the counter script after each resolution where original signal was WRONG.

**Effort estimate:** 2-3 session Gamma build task. Depends on:
- Resolution scorer existence (not found in scan — check with Gamma)
- On-chain `counter_claim` claim type support in `x/knowledge` module

**New item: P2-9** — oracle bridge missing counter-claim submission; adds permanent false signals on incorrect predictions.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 7)

Chain offline — codebase audit of confidence.go.

**Finding: Confidence formula is internally consistent with stake-weight design.**

Reviewed `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` — `AggregateVerificationResult`:
- Stake-weighted: `weightedConf = safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)`
- Reputation adjustment applied via `captureDefenseKeeper.GetReputationScore()` when wired
- Aggregation: acceptWeight / rejectWeight / abstainWeight / totalWeight tallied deterministically (sorted map keys)
- Agreement ratio: `majorWeight * 1_000_000 / totalWeight` — correct formula
- Ceilings applied in order: stratum ceiling → category ceiling → evidence bonus (capped +15%) → method diversity penalty/bonus
- All positive int64→uint64 casts safe; overflow prevented by safeMulDiv throughout

**One edge-case observation (not a blocker):**
Tier 0 validators with `GetEffectiveStake()` returning 0 get floor stake=1. Then `safeMulDiv(confidence, 1, 1_000_000)` = 0 (integer division truncates). A fully-Tier-0 validator set produces `totalWeight=0` → "insufficient" even at 100% participation quorum. Not a P1 blocker since real validators carry meaningful stake, but worth documenting in testnet validator onboarding docs.

**Verdict: CLEAN** — confidence values are consistent with verifier counts and stake weights. No anomaly.

### 5. Truth Farm
FARM cycle: OFFLINE — truth-farm.sh confirmed chain offline (script returned "chain offline"). Claims queued via KTA. No new truth submitted this beat.

### 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1)

**Focus: FARM-2 gap — submitter-side trivial claim prevention**

Beat 41 confirmed FARM-2 is absent as a submitter-side defense. This beat advances the PoT design spec to close it.

**Current gap:** `MsgSubmitFact` accepts any claim with minimum stake and any claim text length. No acceptance-rate ceiling prevents a submitter from flooding with trivially-true claims (e.g., "1+1=2", "the sky exists"), maintaining 100% acceptance rate, farming calibration bonus, and gaming knowledge graph density metrics.

**Design spec for FARM-2 implementation (submitter-side):**

```go
// In msg_server.go, SubmitFact handler — add after format validation, before stake check:

// FARM-2: minimum semantic substance requirements
if uint64(len(msg.ClaimText)) < ep.MinClaimTextLength {
    return nil, sdkerrors.Wrapf(ErrInvalidClaim,
        "claim text must be ≥ %d characters", ep.MinClaimTextLength)
}

// FARM-2: require at least one reference for non-axiomatic claims
if msg.Category != "axiom" && len(msg.References) == 0 {
    return nil, sdkerrors.Wrapf(ErrInvalidClaim,
        "non-axiomatic facts must cite ≥ 1 reference")
}

// FARM-2: calibration acceptance rate ceiling (EndBlock check)
// If submitter acceptance rate > AcceptanceRateCeilingBPS (e.g., 95%)
// over the last EpochWindowBlocks, reject new submissions until rate normalizes
```

**Extended params needed:**
- `MinClaimTextLength` (already exists per beat 38 finding — confirm type and default)
- `AcceptanceRateCeilingBPS` (new param, recommend 950_000 = 95%)
- `AcceptanceRateWindowBlocks` (new param, recommend 1000 blocks = ~3h)

**EndBlock implementation:**
Add `ProcessAcceptanceRateCheck(ctx)` in EndBlock alongside the existing expiry and round processing. Query each active submitter's rolling acceptance rate; flag accounts exceeding the ceiling as `SubmissionLimited`; reject their `MsgSubmitFact` with a specific error code during the limited period.

**Risk:** adds per-submitter state tracking (O(submitters) in EndBlock). Mitigate by batching: only process submitters who submitted in the last window, not all historical submitters.

**Recommendation for testnet:** implement the text-length and reference requirements now (simple, low-risk); defer acceptance-rate ceiling to post-testnet (requires EndBlock state tracking work). This closes 2/3 of the FARM-2 gap before launch.

Beat 27 complete (afternoon session). Chain frozen at 567,950 (day 6+ since 2026-04-19). HIVE OFFLINE. KTA queue at **328 entries** (heartbeat-asha.jsonl). PoT-DESIGN: **FARM-2 submitter-side spec advanced** — 3-layer defense designed (text length floor + reference requirement + acceptance-rate ceiling); recommend text-length and reference requirement for testnet launch; acceptance-rate EndBlock ceiling as post-testnet item. **Chain restart + validateReferences patch in x/knowledge/keeper/ remain the two critical path items for testnet launch.**

The `SubmitterCitationGraph` state IS maintained (via `UpdateSubmitterCitationGraph` called in `citation.go`), so the data accumulates correctly. But the penalty is never applied. A submitter can concentrate 100% of citations on a single peer address with zero economic consequence.

**Call sites needed to activate FARM-11:**
1. In citation reward distribution (wherever citation rewards are calculated): call `ApplyCitationConcentrationPenalty(ctx, submitter, baseReward)` before paying out.
2. Optionally in `EndBlock`: call `CheckCitationConcentration(ctx, submitter)` and emit an event for monitoring.

This is a P2 gap — not a code logic error, but a wiring omission. The defense is designed and implemented; it just isn't connected.

**Revised FARM defense coverage (all audited beats):**
- FARM-3: self-citation discount — IMPLEMENTED (citation.go, 50% discount)
- FARM-5: abstain tax — ABSENT (P2 blocker, beat 6)
- FARM-9: challenger stake floor — IMPLEMENTED
- FARM-11: citation concentration — DEAD CODE (functions exist, zero call sites — NEW P2 gap, retraction of beat 26 finding)
- FARM-13: prophetic citation diversity — PRESENT in comments, implementation not traced
- FARM-14/15: not yet audited

**Verdict: SUBMITTER PATTERN — critical retraction. FARM-11 infrastructure present but inert. New P2 wiring gap identified.**

### 5. Truth Farm

FARM cycle: OFFLINE — truth-farm.sh confirmed chain offline. Claims queued via KTA. No truth submitted.

### 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (slot 2)

**Focus: Oracle→Zerone counter-claim design**

Beat 25 identified that the oracle bridge lacks counter-claim submission when a prediction resolves incorrectly. This beat advances the design.

**Confirmed:** `MsgChallengeFact` exists (`tx.pb.go:33` — `legible.knowledge.v1.MsgChallengeFact`). Challenge path is available on-chain.

**Confirmed:** `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` has zero resolution/counter-claim logic (grep for resolution/reject/retract/incorrect returned nothing).

**Counter-claim hook design:**

When `resolution-scorer.py` closes a prediction as incorrect (outcome ≠ signal direction), the bridge should:

1. Look up the submitted Zerone fact ID from `bridge-submitted.json` using the signal ID.
2. Submit `MsgChallengeFact` with:
   - `fact_id`: original submitted fact ID
   - `evidence`: resolution outcome data (market ticker, resolved value, prediction direction)
   - `stake`: bridge account stake (must meet FARM-9 floor: ≥50% of original fact stake)
3. Track the challenge in `bridge-submitted.json` under a `challenges` key.

**Implementation path for `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh`:**

Add a `--mode resolve` flag. Resolution scorer calls:
```bash
./scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.sh --mode resolve \
  --signal-id "$SIGNAL_ID" \
  --resolved-outcome "$OUTCOME" \
  --original-direction "$DIRECTION"
```

The bridge reads `bridge-submitted.json`, finds the fact ID, calls:
```bash
legbled tx knowledge challenge-fact "$FACT_ID" \
  "Resolution: market resolved $OUTCOME; original signal was $DIRECTION — incorrect." \
  --home "$DEVNET_HOME" --from bridge-agent --chain-id "$CHAIN_ID" --fees "$FEES"
```

**Economic note:** Challenge requires stake ≥50% of original fact stake (FARM-9). Original fact stake is 1,000,000 ulgm (from bridge.sh line 40). Minimum challenge stake: 500,000 ulgm. If challenge wins (incorrect signal confirmed), bridge recovers stake + up to ~13% bonus. Self-challenging own incorrect facts is economically net-zero to slightly positive — a sustainable truth-correction mechanism.

**Status:** Design complete. Implementation blocked on chain restart. Add `--mode resolve` flag to `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` in next online session.

Pipeline not found at `instances/nuance/youspeak/pipeline/assess.py`. Stage unknown — consistent with all prior beats.

### 4. Continuous Verification: CONFIDENCE CHECK (rotation slot 2, cycle 10)

**Claim audited:** "Confidence score is consistent with verifier count and stake weights."

Applied codebase audit to `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go`.

**Algorithm traced (lines 62–153):**
1. For each validator in `round.Reveals` (deterministic sorted order):
   - `stake = GetEffectiveStakeForValidator()`
   - If captureDefenseKeeper != nil: `stake = stake * repScore / 1_000_000`
   - If reputation-adjusted stake = 0, floor to 1
2. `weightedConf = reveal.Confidence * stake / 1_000_000`
3. Accumulate into acceptWeight / rejectWeight / abstainWeight / totalWeight
4. `agreementRatio = majorWeight * 1_000_000 / totalWeight`
5. `result.Confidence = agreementRatio` (if above acceptance threshold)

**Verdict: MATHEMATICALLY CONSISTENT.** The confidence score equals the stake-weighted majority ratio — correctly normalized.

**Edge case flagged (new P3):** When `repScore == 0` (line 68 condition `if repScore > 0`), reputation adjustment is bypassed entirely and raw stake is used. This treats "no reputation history" identically to "full trust". A validator with zero reputation history gets the same effective weight as a trusted long-term validator. Recommendation: set `repScore` default to 500,000 (50%) for validators with no domain history, rather than 1,000,000 (100%). This is a conservative trust floor, not a zero-trust gate.

**Status: CONFIDENCE CHECK PASS — algorithm sound. One P3 edge case logged.**

### 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (slot 6)

**Focus: Oracle → Zerone integration audit — `--mode resolve` gap.**

Beat 28 spec'd a `--mode resolve` flag for `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` to challenge incorrect facts using resolved oracle signals. This beat advances the integration design.

**Current bridge status:**
- Forward path: `submit-claim` mode WORKING (`oracle-zerone-bridge.sh`)
- `challenge-fact [fact-id] [reason] [stake]` — confirmed available in legbled tx knowledge
- `submit-contradiction [claim-text] [domain] [category] [stake] --contradicts [fact-id]` — also available; alternative approach

**Critical gap found:** `bridge-submitted.json` does not exist on disk (oracle data directory `~/Desktop/prediction-markets/data/` also absent from current environment). The current bridge tracks submitted signals as a flat list of IDs — **no signal→fact-id mapping is stored**. `challenge-fact` requires a `fact-id`, which is only known after querying the tx events of the original `submit-claim` tx.

**Integration upgrade required before `--mode resolve` can work:**

1. **Upgrade submitted.json schema**: Change from `{"submitted": ["sig-1", "sig-2"]}` to `{"submissions": {"sig-1": {"claim_id": "...", "txhash": "...", "submitted_at": "..."}}}`

2. **Capture claim_id on submit**: After a successful `submit-claim` tx, query `legbled query tx [txhash] --home [devnet_home]` and parse events for `claim_id` attribute:
   ```
   attribute_key: claim_id
   attribute_value: <uuid>
   ```

3. **`--mode resolve` implementation**:
   - Read `data/resolutions.jsonl` — each line has `{signal_id, outcome, direction, resolved_at}`
   - For each resolved signal where oracle outcome contradicts original trade direction: look up `submissions[signal_id].claim_id`
   - If claim_id found: submit `challenge-fact [claim_id] "Oracle resolution contradicts signal: [outcome]" 500000ulgm`
   - If claim_id not found (signal submitted before schema upgrade): log as unrecoverable, skip

**Alternative path:** Use `submit-contradiction` instead of `challenge-fact`. This creates a new fact rather than challenging the trust round — lower stake requirement and doesn't risk slash if the original verifiers disagree. Appropriate for oracle-sourced contradictions where evidence is external.

**Recommendation:** Use `submit-contradiction` for oracle resolutions (lower risk, no trust-round dependency) and `challenge-fact` only when the oracle signal was clearly fraudulent (strong evidence, willing to stake).

**Status: Design complete. Two blockers: (a) submitted.json schema upgrade, (b) oracle data directory location. Ready to implement when chain restarts and oracle data confirmed available.**

### 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycle 14)

**Focus: Multi-level citation propagation — P2 blocker 6 resolution**

P2 blocker 6 states: `MaxPropagationDepth` and `CitationDecayPerLevel` are present as dead config in `x/knowledge/` but multi-level citation reward propagation is not implemented. Prior recommendation: "implement with cycle detection or document as P3."

**Design analysis:**

Multi-level citation propagation means: if Fact A cites Fact B, and Fact B cites Fact C, then when Fact A receives a verification reward, part of that reward flows back to submitters of B and C (attenuated by `CitationDecayPerLevel` per hop). This is designed to incentivize building on prior knowledge rather than submitting isolated facts.

**The cycle problem:**
If A → B → C → A (citation cycle), propagation creates an infinite loop. Cycle detection requires either:
1. A depth-first traversal of the citation graph at reward time (expensive, unbounded)
2. A pre-submission cycle check (`isAncestor(proposed_ref, submitting_fact)`) at fact submission time
3. A hard depth cap (`MaxPropagationDepth`) with no cycle check — rely on attenuation to make deep cycles economically negligible

Option 3 is what the dead config implies — cap at `MaxPropagationDepth` hops, apply `CitationDecayPerLevel` at each hop. If the decay is ≥50% per level (`CitationDecayPerLevel ≤ 500_000`) and `MaxPropagationDepth = 3`, the maximum reward flowing to a depth-3 ancestor is 12.5% of the base citation reward. Cycles become self-limiting economically even without explicit detection.

**Testnet recommendation:**
Document multi-level citation as P3 (testnet launches with depth-0 citations only; `MaxPropagationDepth = 1` effectively disabling propagation). This is safe and honest — testnet tests the core submission/verification/challenge loop without adding citation graph complexity.

**Mainnet recommendation:**
Implement with Option 3 (depth cap + decay, no explicit cycle check). Set `MaxPropagationDepth = 3` and `CitationDecayPerLevel = 500_000` (50% per hop). Maximum citation chain reward: 12.5% at depth 3. Economic cycles are negligible; safety is ensured by the cap.

**One new invariant to add to the spec:**
A citation at depth `n` should only generate propagation rewards if the cited fact has `FactStatusVerified` (not provisional, not expired). Propagation through provisional or retracted facts dilutes truth signal. Add validation: `if cited_fact.Status != "verified" { skip propagation for this ref }`.

**Design advance summary:**
- Multi-level propagation: P3 for testnet (depth-0 only), P2 for mainnet
- Mainnet implementation: depth cap + geometric decay, no explicit cycle detection
- New invariant: propagation only through `verified` facts
- Removes this item from pre-testnet gate; clarifies mainnet implementation path

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 7)

Chain offline — codebase audit of `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go`.

**Formula verified:** `AggregateVerificationResult` in `confidence.go:45`

- Each verifier contributes `weightedConf = reveal.Confidence * reputationAdjustedStake / 1_000_000`
- `totalWeight = sum of all weightedConf` (accept + reject + abstain)
- `agreementRatio = majorWeight * 1_000_000 / totalWeight`
- `result.Confidence = agreementRatio`

**Finding:** Formula is internally consistent — confidence directly equals the stake-weighted agreement ratio. Implementation uses `safeMulDiv` to prevent uint64 overflow. Tier 0 floor (stake=1) prevents zero-weight exclusion. Deterministic via `sortedMapKeys`.

**One known gap (carry-forward, P2):** Abstain votes contribute to `totalWeight` but are not penalized — this dilutes confidence without consequences (FARM-5: abstain tax absent). Already on blocker list.

**Verdict: CONFIDENCE CHECK PASSES.** No new integrity gaps found.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: VERISLEIGHT SCAN** (slot 4, cycle 8)

Chain offline — codebase audit. Reading 3 fact status/confidence claims from the source:

**Claim 1 — Governance-added facts have `Status: "verified"` with `VerifierCount: 0`**
Location: `x/knowledge/keeper/msg_server.go:517-521`
```go
Status:        "verified",
VerifierCount: 0, // Governance-added, no verifiers
```
**VERISLEIGHT DETECTED.** The status field "verified" signals to any consumer that this fact passed the validator consensus pipeline. It did not. Governance approved it by fiat. The VerifierCount comment is honest in code but invisible to API consumers. A chain consumer filtering `status = "verified"` would conflate governance-approved with consensus-verified — producing false confidence in the fact's epistemic standing.
Fix: use a distinct status `"governance-verified"` or `"governance-approved"` to preserve semantic accuracy at the API boundary.

**Claim 2 — CONF-002 guard prevents zero-verifier facts from gaining confidence**
Location: `x/knowledge/keeper/growth.go:51-58`
Assessment: NOT verisleight. The guard is correctly implemented and the comment is explicit about the danger it prevents. Facts with zero verifiers are blocked from confidence growth. This is sound design.

**Claim 3 — Abstain votes are weighted in aggregation but carry no penalty**
Location: `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go:96-97,311`
```go
// abstain is not slashed
```
**POTENTIAL VERISLEIGHT.** The confidence aggregation includes abstain weight as a legitimate signal, implying abstain represents epistemic humility with accountability. But abstain carries zero slashing risk. A rational validator maximizes abstain on uncertain facts, which biases confidence scores toward whatever outcome clear-verdict validators choose — without bearing risk themselves. The signal is honest about the verdict but misleading about the cost. The system *presents* abstain as principled; the incentive structure makes it free-riding. This is verisleight at the economic layer: technically true behavior (abstain is counted) producing a false conclusion (abstain represents epistemic integrity).

**Verdict: 1 confirmed verisleight (governance-approved presented as verified), 1 economic verisleight candidate (abstain free-riding). Both are P2. Critical path unchanged: chain restart + P1 validateReferences fix.**

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 14)

**Focus: Abstain tax design — closing the free-rider gap**

The ECONOMY duty aligns with the verisleight finding above. Gap: validators can indefinitely abstain without economic consequence, free-riding on the verification work of committed validators. This produces systematic abstain-bias and undermines the epistemic weight of the consensus signal.

**Proposed abstain tax mechanism:**

1. **Abstain tax rate**: 2% of stake per abstention, deducted from validator's bonded ZRN. (Lower than wrong-verdict slash at 6% — abstain is epistemically neutral, not wrong, but must not be free.)

2. **Accumulation cap**: Abstain tax accumulates up to a maximum of 12% per epoch (6 consecutive abstentions = max penalty). Beyond that, validator faces forced unbonding from the domain.

3. **Redistribution**: Collected abstain tax flows to `research_fund` module account (same destination as citation tax in `citation.go:149`). Research fund already has disbursement governance built (`msg_server.go:1065`).

4. **Implementation location**: `x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go` — after the abstain verdict case (line 97), add slash dispatch call analogous to wrong-verdict slash at line 311, but with 2% rate and `abstain_tax` event type.

5. **Params addition**: Add `AbstainTaxRate` and `MaxAbstainTaxPerEpoch` to `types/params.go` and governance update path.

This closes FARM-5 (abstain tax absent) while maintaining the research fund as the sole economic outflow destination — clean alignment with existing architecture.

**Economic note on minted/routed/burned ratio**: Chain offline, cannot query live. From last available state: research_fund outflow mechanism exists but disbursement requires governance proposal. No automated burn mechanism found. Token sink is staking + slashing. The abstain tax adds a third predictable sink (research fund accumulation), improving token economic health.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 15)

Chain offline — codebase audit of confidence scoring formula (`x/knowledge/keeper/confidence.go`).

**Finding: Confidence formula is internally consistent and correctly stake-weighted.**

`AggregateVerificationResult` (`confidence.go:46`):

1. **Per-validator weighted confidence** (`confidence.go:89`):
   ```go
   weightedConf := safeMulDiv(reveal.Confidence, stake, 1_000_000)
   ```
   Each validator's contribution is the product of their stake (domain-reputation-adjusted, line 66-77) and their revealed confidence assessment. This is a composite signal — a validator who hedges (low confidence) contributes less to the consensus signal even with large stake.

2. **Verdict aggregation** (`confidence.go:81-99`): acceptWeight, rejectWeight, abstainWeight each accumulate `weightedConf` by verdict. Abstainers' weight IS counted in `totalWeight` (line 99), correctly diluting the agreement ratio.

3. **Quorum check** (`confidence.go:118-128`): uses `reveals / selectedValidators` headcount ratio. Decoupled from confidence formula. Design intent: quorum is count-based (minimum participation); confidence is weight-based (quality of agreement).

4. **Final confidence** (`confidence.go:146-153`): `agreementRatio = majorWeight / totalWeight`. Confidence = agreementRatio. This is the fraction of total weighted-confidence-signal that agrees with the majority verdict.

**Edge case noted (not a bug — design observation):** A single validator with overwhelming stake can yield confidence = 1,000,000 if they're the sole revealer above quorum. This is inherent to stake-weighted consensus. Decentralization of stake is the economic defense, not the formula. The formula itself is correct.

**No inconsistency between confidence scores, verifier counts, and stake weights. CONFIDENCE CHECK PASSES.**

## 5. Truth Farm

FARM cycle: OFFLINE — truth-farm.sh confirmed chain offline. Claims queued via KTA.

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (slot 1, cycle 16)

**Focus: AggregationPhaseDuration security analysis (opened by beat 30)**

Beat 30 noted that `AggregationPhaseDuration` is a new security parameter whose value matters. This beat advances the analysis.

**Context:** In the current Zerone implementation, aggregation runs as a distinct post-reveal phase (`rounds.go:88-97`). The keeper calls `AggregateVerificationResult` during this phase. Since aggregation is centralized (keeper-executed, not validator-submitted), `AggregationPhaseDuration` is the window within which the keeper must complete aggregation before the round expires.

**Finding: AggregationPhaseDuration is currently a liveness parameter, not a security parameter.**

Because aggregation is keeper-executed (not validator-submitted), the duration doesn't affect whether validators can influence the aggregate — the keeper runs the formula atomically at block processing. The duration only determines how long the system will wait for the keeper to run before marking the round expired.

**Security implication:** If `AggregationPhaseDuration = 0`, rounds could expire immediately after reveal-close, before any block processes the aggregation. If too short (< 1 block worth of processing), rounds may miss aggregation under load. If too long, it delays fact finalization unnecessarily.

**Recommendation:**
- Set `AggregationPhaseDuration` = 1-2 blocks (not 0, not too large)
- Add guard in round processing: if aggregation phase begins and aggregation hasn't run, trigger it immediately in the same block
- The real distributed-aggregation concern would arise if Zerone moves to validator-submitted aggregation proofs — then duration becomes a security parameter. For now, it is operational.

**This closes the beat 30 design question. AggregationPhaseDuration = 2 blocks recommended for testnet.**

**Pre-testnet status (unchanged from beat 30):**
- P1 blockers: 2 (verifier_count=0, dangling citations)
- P2 blockers: 5 (FARM-5 abstain tax, phase duration defaults, multi-level citation, submitter cap, oracle bridge)
- P3 gaps: 3 (claim_text validation, epistemic category enforcement, farm account funding)

Chain offline — codebase audit. Focus: submitter concentration and volume defenses.

**Findings:**

1. **CalibrationRecord is fully wired (new discovery — not captured in beat 26).** Per-submitter claim acceptance rate is actively maintained:
   - `provisional.go:67,249` — updated on provisional reject/accept
   - `rounds.go:302,421` — updated on verification round accept/reject
   - `partnership_credit.go:70` — partner submitter also credited
   - `msg_server.go:503` — confidence contribution capped at CalibrationNeutralRate (70%) for new/unproven submitters

   CalibrationRecord is a **quality defense**: spammy submitters who flood low-quality facts see acceptance rate degrade, capping their confidence contribution. This is a meaningful submission pattern defense that beat 26 missed.

2. **P2 blocker stands: CalibrationRecord is quality defense, not volume defense.** A submitter with degraded calibration can still submit unlimited facts per epoch. Volume flooding is unaffected by calibration.

3. **New observation (P3): Reputation laundering path possible.** No epoch-based calibration decay or reset found in the scan. A submitter could: (a) flood cheap low-quality facts (calibration degrades but no volume cap), (b) selectively submit high-quality facts to recover calibration, (c) use recovered calibration for high-stakes false claims. Recommend: epoch-based calibration decay rate or reset cadence to prevent stale reputation from hiding spam history.

4. **FARM-11 citation concentration confirmed implemented** (carry-forward from beat 26) — structural citation rings detectable.

**Verdict: SUBMITTER PATTERN PASS** — FARM-11 wired, CalibrationRecord quality tracking fully wired (new positive finding); volume cap absent (P2 blocker confirmed); reputation laundering path identified (P3 gap).

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (slot 2, cycle 17)

**Focus: Oracle→Zerone counter-claim bridge design (gap identified beat 25)**

**Current state:**
- `tools/oracle.py` — full prediction lifecycle: predict, resolve, score, calibration
- `tools/kta.py` — KTA adapter wrapping Zerone claim submission
- No oracle-zerone bridge script in Love/scripts (scripts archived in zerone/scripts per beat 25)
- Beat 25 gap: when a prediction resolves incorrect, no counter-claim is submitted on Zerone — stale phantom confidence accumulates

**Bridge design: Oracle resolution counter-claim hook**

Three components needed to close the gap:

1. **`zerone_fact_id` field in predictions.json** — when oracle→Zerone bridge submits an oracle signal as `protocol_observation`, store the returned Zerone fact ID alongside the prediction. This is the anchor for counter-claim targeting.

2. **Resolution hook in `oracle.py resolve`** — after marking a prediction `false`, call `kta.py` to queue a `MsgSubmitCounterFact` targeting the stored `zerone_fact_id`. The counter-claim body: original fact ID, resolution notes, Brier contribution. Protobuf type confirmed: `counter_fact.pb.go` with `Submitter`, `SubmitterDid`, `TargetFactId` fields exists in `x/knowledge/types/`.

3. **Conditional: only submit counter-claim when fact was incorrect.** Correct predictions (`resolve pred-XXXX true`) should submit a *confirming* citation, not a counter-claim — strengthening the original `protocol_observation`'s confidence signal.

**Implementation sequence (blocked on chain restart):**
1. Add `zerone_fact_id` field to oracle prediction schema in `oracle.py`
2. Update oracle→Zerone bridge to store returned fact ID on original submission
3. In `oracle.py resolve`: if `outcome == false`, queue `MsgSubmitCounterFact` via kta.py; if `outcome == true`, queue citation to original fact
4. Test with devnet once chain restarts

This closes the economic gap: incorrect oracle signals will be corrected on-chain; correct signals will accumulate confirming citations. Both directions of oracle truth feedback are captured.

**P2 blocker update:** This design advancement means oracle bridge is now fully spec'd including counter-claims. P2 blocker 8 is DESIGN-COMPLETE, blocked only on chain restart for implementation.

Three fact statements audited for technically-true-but-missing-context issues:

**Statement 1 (NEW P2 — verisleight):** `confidence.go:143-160`: When abstain weight is the plurality, `majorVerdict = "inconclusive"` and `result.Confidence = agreementRatio` (abstain weight / totalWeight). A consumer querying `verdict: "inconclusive", confidence: 800000` reads "high-confidence inconclusive" — but the confidence number means "80% of stake abstained," not "80% epistemic certainty about the inconclusive assessment." The `confidence` field is semantically inappropriate for inconclusive verdicts. Fix: nullify or zero `result.Confidence` when verdict is inconclusive, or add separate `abstain_rate` field. **New P2 gap added.**

**Statement 2 (carry-forward P2):** `msg_server.go:517`: Governance facts assigned `Status: "verified"` with `VerifierCount: 0`. "Verified" implies consensus verification; these facts bypassed the commit-reveal process entirely. Already flagged beat 30. Confirmed still present.

An abstainer can repeatedly prevent consensus with zero economic risk. The code accurately documents "no slash" without documenting the mechanism for consequence-free consensus blocking. FARM-5 abstain tax gap — viewed from verisleight angle for the first time this beat.

**Finding: P2 blocker 4 FULLY RETRACTED.**

Beat 33 left open: "verify EndBlock handler for research fund proposal finalization before retracting."

`EndBlock` (module.go:154-195) does NOT include research fund finalization. It handles: `CleanupExpiredRounds`, `ExpireStaleFactsInEndBlocker`, `TriggerReverificationScan`, `ApplyCyclePenalties`, `PruneUnusedFactsInEndBlocker`.

However, `MsgExecuteResearchProposal` (msg_server.go:1207-1289) is a fully-implemented manually-triggered execution path:
- Validates: past `VotingEndsAt` deadline (or already approved)
- Tallies: `TallyResearchProposal()` → pass/quorum check
- Rejected: return 90% deposit, burn 10%, emit event
- Approved: `SendCoins(research_fund module → recipient)`, return full deposit, `TotalDisbursed` tracked, status → `Executed`
- Permissionless: any caller can execute after deadline

This is the standard Cosmos governance pattern (Gov v1 equivalent). No EndBlock automation needed — any actor can trigger execution after voting closes. The fund CAN disburse.

**Verdict: ECONOMIC FLOW PASS.** Research fund disbursement fully implemented. Fee inflow (citation tax → research_fund in rounds.go:952, citation.go:149) and outflow (`MsgExecuteResearchProposal`) both confirmed. P2 blocker 4 RETRACTED.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CITATION AUDIT** (rotation slot 1, cycle 20)

Chain offline — codebase audit. This cycle: examine the citation **reward** path to determine whether phantom citations (citations that pass format validation but point to non-existent facts) actually receive economic rewards.

**Finding: Phantom citations do NOT receive rewards — but they are silently accepted.**

`citation.go:102`: inside `DistributeCitationRewards`, before paying out:

```go
fact, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref.ToFactId)
if !found || fact.Status == types.FactStatusExpired {
    continue
}
```

The reward distribution has its own `GetFact` guard — phantom citations are silently skipped at reward time. **No economic payment flows to non-existent cited facts.**

**Nuance: The threat model of P1 blocker 2 must be refined.** The risk is NOT that phantom citations earn rewards. The actual risks are:

1. **Verifier confusion.** A verifier querying a fact's references sees hex IDs that look like real citations. Some point to nothing. Verifiers may over-credit the fact's epistemic support.

2. **Inflated `TotalCitationRewards` field.** Wait — this doesn't apply, since `TotalCitationRewards` is only updated when a reward is actually sent (line 170: `fact.TotalCitationRewards += adjustedShare`). Phantom citations produce no entry.

3. **Citation concentration bypass.** `SubmitterCitationGraph.CitedCounts` tracks how many times each fact is cited. If the cited fact ID doesn't exist, the citation graph entry is never created — phantom citations don't inflate any citation concentration counter. However, a malicious submitter could use phantom citations to pad their citation list to meet minimum-citation thresholds (e.g., to qualify for fundamentality bonuses) without actually supporting real knowledge.

**Revised threat model for P1 blocker 2:**
- **Economic impact:** Low (rewards not paid to phantoms).
- **Integrity impact:** Medium (facts appear better-supported than they are; verifiers receive misleading provenance signals).
- **Manipulation vector:** Phantom citations can pad citation counts for gamification (fundamentality bonus threshold, diversity requirements) without real epistemic cost.

**Verdict: CITATION AUDIT PASS (no reward leak confirmed).** P1 blocker 2 remains open but threat severity is **MEDIUM** (not HIGH) — integrity and gamification risk, not direct economic drain. Downgrade recommendation: P1→P2, with the fix still well-defined and low-risk (promote `validateReferences` to msgServer method, add `k.GetFact()` guard for on-chain refs).

## 6. Standing Duty: INTEGRATE (slot 6, cycle 21)

**Status check: seigei-zerone-bridge.sh (beat 29 concrete action)**

Beat 29 recommended creating `scripts/seigei-zerone-bridge.sh` as the next INTEGRATE action. Verified this beat: **the script EXISTS and is complete** (148 lines). It handles:
- `content_verified` events → `MsgSubmitFact` (sociology/social_consensus, confidence threshold 0.75)
- `community_appeal` events → `MsgSubmitChallenge` (against original fact ID)
- Idempotent submission tracking via `seigei-bridge-submitted.json`
- `--dry-run` support

Beat 29's concrete action is DONE. Advancing to the next gap: **oracle-zerone-bridge.sh counter-claim hook**.

**Oracle bridge counter-claim gap analysis:**

`oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` is 103 lines. It submits oracle signals as `protocol_observation` claims. It does NOT handle resolution events — when `resolution-scorer.py` resolves a prediction as wrong, no counter-claim fires on Zerone. Identified as P2 gap in beat 25.

**Design for counter-claim hook:**

```
Resolution event (resolution-scorer.py output):
  { "prediction_id": "pred-0001", "resolved_correct": false,
    "original_zerone_fact_id": "abc123...",
    "resolution_text": "FOMC held rates; prediction was WRONG" }

Oracle bridge extension:
  - read data/resolved-signals.json (new file created by resolution-scorer.py)
  - for each resolution where resolved_correct == false AND original_fact not yet challenged:
      → MsgSubmitChallenge(original_fact_id, "Oracle resolution: prediction incorrect. " + resolution_text)
  - track challenged IDs in bridge-submitted.json["challenged"] (same pattern as seigei bridge)
```

**Concrete next action:** Extend `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` with a resolution-event reader. Requires:
1. `resolution-scorer.py` to write `data/resolved-signals.json` with Zerone fact IDs (needs oracle→Zerone submission tracking to capture fact IDs at submit time).
2. `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` second pass: after submitting new signals, iterate resolved-signals.json and fire challenges.

**Blocker:** oracle→Zerone bridge cannot capture fact IDs at submit time without a running chain (tx responses needed). This is a chain-restart dependency, same as all bridge work.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CONFIDENCE CHECK** (rotation slot 2, cycle 21)

Chain offline — codebase audit of `confidence.go`.

**Question:** Is the confidence score consistent with verifier count and stake weights?

**Findings:**

`AggregateVerificationResult` (confidence.go:44):
- Builds `stakeMap` from all revealing validators via `GetEffectiveStakeForValidator`
- Applies domain reputation weighting via `captureDefenseKeeper.GetReputationScore` (stake * repScore / 1_000_000)
- `ParticipantCount = len(round.Reveals)` — directly tracks verifier count
- Confidence is `agreementRatio = majorWeight * 1_000_000 / totalWeight` — pure stake-weighted
- Additional adjustments: quorum check (participationRatio >= QuorumThreshold), stratum ceiling, category ceiling, evidence bonus

**Verdict: CLEAN.** Confidence IS consistent with verifier count (ParticipantCount is exact reveal count) and stake weights (every reveal is weighted by effective stake before contributing to the aggregate). No integrity gap here.

**Note:** Zero-stake validators get a floor of 1 (confidence.go:85: `if stake == 0 { stake = 1 }`) to prevent zero-weight exclusion. This is a small but intentional bias — a Tier 0 validator with zero stake still counts, though minimally. Acceptable for PoT design, but worth documenting as a known behavior.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: SUBMITTER PATTERN** (rotation slot 3, cycle 23)

Chain offline — codebase audit of `x/research/keeper/state.go` and `x/research/keeper/msg_server.go`.

**Question:** Is there protection against a single submitter concentrating claims in one domain (> 50%)? Are suspicious patterns (all same domain, all citing each other) detectable?

**Findings:**

`state.go` provides `GetResearchesByDomain(ctx, domain)` — filtering by domain only. There is **no** `GetResearchesBySubmitter` function. No per-submitter index exists in the keeper.

`msg_server.go` at SubmitResearch (line 47+): validates address, deducts fee, stores research. No per-submitter pending count check. No per-submitter domain concentration check before acceptance.

`cmd/dashboard.go` implements `legbled query dashboard [address]` — this does a full scan of all researches filtered in application logic, not an indexed lookup. Functional but O(n) in total fact count.

**Verdict: GAP CONFIRMED — P2 severity.** The chain has no mechanism to detect or limit submitter concentration. A single actor can:
1. Submit unlimited facts in a single domain (self-referential citation rings become possible)
2. Constitute > 50% of facts in a domain with no on-chain alert
3. Escape detection without running a full iteration scan

The `dashboard` query mitigates this for investigators but is a pull tool, not a push guard. The protocol should define: (a) a MaxPendingPerSubmitter param, (b) a domain concentration alert threshold, (c) an on-chain index `SubmitterFactsKey` for efficient lookup.

**Cross-link to P1 gap (beat 4):** The verifier_count=0 bypass and the submitter concentration gap are complementary attack vectors — an actor can saturate a domain AND have facts verified with zero legitimate verifier engagement. Both must be closed before testnet.

## 6. Standing Duty: PoT-DESIGN (cycle 23)

Advancing Proof of Truth protocol design. Focus: **Submitter Concentration Defense** — formalizing the design requirements exposed by today's SUBMITTER PATTERN audit.

**PoT-DESIGN: Submitter Concentration Limits**

The Proof of Truth protocol must resist domain capture by a single submitter. Proposed protocol additions:

**1. MaxPendingPerSubmitter (chain parameter)**
- A submitter may have at most N facts in `PENDING` or `UNDER_REVIEW` state simultaneously
- Proposed initial value: 20 (tunable via governance)
- Enforcement: `msg_server.go` checks pending count before accepting new submission; returns `ErrSubmitterAtPendingLimit` if exceeded
- Implementation: requires new keeper index `SubmitterPendingKey = "sp/{address}"` → count

**2. DomainConcentrationThreshold (chain parameter)**
- If a single submitter accounts for > T% of VERIFIED facts in a domain, their next submission to that domain emits a `EventDomainConcentrationAlert` event
- Not a block — an alert. Validators and observers can act
- Proposed initial value: T = 40% (soft warning), T = 60% (hard cap, requires governance override)
- Implementation: `GetResearchesByDomain` already exists; add `GetSubmitterCountInDomain(ctx, submitter, domain)` → ratio check on submit

**3. SubmitterFactsIndex (new keeper store)**
- `SubmitterFactsKey = "sf/{address}/{factId}"` → stores fact IDs per submitter
- Enables O(1) count and efficient dashboard lookups
- `GetSubmitterFactCount(ctx, submitter)` → total, by domain, by status

**4. Self-Citation Detection (PoT-DESIGN extension)**
- A fact whose references ALL point to facts by the same submitter should be flagged
- At minimum: `validateReferences()` should check submitter diversity of cited facts
- This is a P2 design gap (beat 38: identified). Not a block but closes the citation ring attack vector.

**Priority:** P2 — not testnet-blocking alone, but combined with the verifier_count=0 P1 gap, these gaps together enable a complete truth-capture attack. Recommend Gamma review before testnet freeze.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: VERISLEIGHT SCAN** (rotation slot 4, cycle 24)

Auditing 3 claims from prior beats for technically-true-but-missing-critical-context.

**Claim 1: "~36,000 LGM/day inflation rate" (beat 26, ECONOMY)**

- Technical truth: `BlockRewardAmount = "3000000"` (3 LGM) × ~12,000 blocks/day = 36,000 LGM/day.
- Missing context: `EmptyBlockRewardRate = 0` (`vesting_rewards/types/genesis.go:15`, `rewards.go:240`). Empty blocks earn **zero** reward. On a sparse devnet/early testnet with few users, the majority of blocks are empty. Effective daily inflation is highly variable — on a completely idle chain it is 0. The 36K/day figure is a theoretical maximum (all blocks have transactions), not a realistic operating rate.
- **VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED**: The stated inflation rate overstates expected reality for a lightly-loaded chain. Documentation should present this as "up to 36,000 LGM/day, actual rate proportional to transaction volume."

**Claim 2: "CitationShareBPS = 150_000 → 15% of verification rewards" (beat 26, ECONOMY)**

- Technical truth: if `CitationShareBPS = 150_000` is read as per-million precision (which Zerone uses throughout — confidence scores, stake weights, all use /1_000_000 scale), then 150_000/1_000_000 = 15%. Correct.
- Missing context: The naming suffix "BPS" (basis points) typically means /10,000 in financial convention. A reader unfamiliar with Zerone's per-million convention would interpret `CitationShareBPS = 150_000` as 1,500% — obviously invalid. The non-standard BPS naming creates a documentation trap: internal consistency is correct, but the name signals incorrect expectations to external readers.
- **VERISLEIGHT CANDIDATE (naming/documentation)**: The value is correct; the name is misleading. Recommendation: rename to `CitationSharePPM` (parts-per-million) throughout the codebase, or add a prominent note in params documentation that "BPS" = per-million in this codebase. Not a runtime bug — a documentation risk.

**Claim 3: "Bridges architecturally complete — both oracle→Zerone and agent→Zerone" (beat 25, BRIDGE)**

- Technical truth: `scripts/oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` and `scripts/kta-heartbeat.sh` exist and dry-run validated.
- Missing context: Beat 25 also identified that the oracle bridge lacks counter-claim submission — when a prediction resolves wrong, no automated path submits a `rejected` counter-claim. This means the bridge is architecturally complete for the **happy path** only. Claims posted via the oracle bridge that are later falsified accumulate stale confidence indefinitely unless manually corrected. "Architecturally complete" overstates coverage.
- **VERISLEIGHT CONFIRMED**: Oracle bridge is complete for initial signal submission, incomplete for correction. Stale false claims is the consequence. This is the gap identified in beat 25 and still open.

**Verdict: VERISLEIGHT SCAN — 2 confirmed, 1 naming/documentation flag.** (1) Inflation rate overstates typical rate by ignoring empty-block suppression; (2) "architecturally complete" bridge claim omits the correction gap. Naming flag on BPS/PPM is low severity but adds documentation debt.

## 6. Standing Duty: BRIDGE (cycle 24)

**Focus: Counter-claim integration design for oracle→Zerone bridge**

Advancing the gap identified in beat 25: the oracle bridge cannot submit counter-claims when a prediction resolves incorrectly.

**Current flow (implemented):**
1. `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh` → reads oracle signals → submits `protocol_observation` claim on devnet
2. `resolution-scorer.py` → resolves predictions against outcomes → updates local records

**Missing link:** When resolution-scorer.py determines a prediction was wrong, there is no hook back to the oracle bridge to retract or contest the original on-chain claim.

**Proposed counter-claim design:**

```
resolution-scorer.py  →  resolution event
    if prediction was WRONG:
        call oracle-zerone-bridge.sh --counter-claim \
          --original-claim-id <on-chain-fact-id> \
          --resolution-evidence <outcome-source-url> \
          --claim-type "falsification"
```

**Implementation steps:**
1. `oracle-zerone-bridge.sh`: add `--counter-claim` flag that calls `legbled tx knowledge submit-falsification` (if that tx type exists) or `submit-fact` with `claim_type: "contradiction"` referencing the original fact ID
2. `resolution-scorer.py`: on `WRONG` resolution outcome, check if an oracle bridge claim was submitted for that prediction; if yes, call counter-claim hook
3. Bridge registry: maintain `bridge-claims.jsonl` mapping `prediction_id → on_chain_fact_id`; currently absent — no way to look up what was submitted

**Blocker:** All three steps require a live chain. Architecture can be specced now; implementation awaits devnet restart.

**New bridge gap (P2 addition):** The bridge registry (`bridge-claims.jsonl`) is a prerequisite for counter-claim submission and for any idempotency guarantee on the bridge. Should be created when the bridge first submits a claim. This is a standalone improvement that can be implemented offline (just file I/O). **Actionable without chain restart.**

   } else if reveal.Verdict != "abstain" {

2. **`PassiveStakingPenaltyBPS = 500_000` (50% reduction) does NOT cover abstainers.** `types.go:693`: this param penalizes non-participants (validators who skip selection entirely), not validators who actively vote "abstain." The passive staking penalty is a different guard against different behavior.

3. **FARM-5 attack confirmed open.** `bvm/vm/empty_work_test.go:340-405` documents the attack and explicitly recommends the fix but records it as NOT implemented:
   - Attack: always vote abstain → fills quorum slot, blocks other validators, earns 0 reward, pays 0 penalty (gas only)
   - Grief vector: if 40%+ of selected validators abstain, quorum fails, knowledge accumulation stalls
   - Test comment at line 402: "Add 'abstain tax': repeated abstain (>50% of rounds) → 0.5% stake penalty"
   - `openclaw_test.go:234` confirms: "Abstaining should NOT be rewarded — this incentivizes non-participation"

4. **Fee flow is otherwise sound.** Verified in beat 28: `MeterMintedKey`/`MeterBurnedKey` in state, `ProposeResearchFund`/`ExecuteResearchProposal` disbursement path confirmed, block rewards correctly zero for empty blocks (`EmptyBlockRewardRate = 0`).

**Verdict: ECONOMIC FLOW — FARM-5 abstain tax confirmed absent (P2 blocker open). Fee tracking and disbursement paths are healthy. Abstain is the sole undefended economic grief vector in the participation layer.**

## 6. Standing Duty: ECONOMY (slot 3, cycle 25)

**Focus: Abstain tax — economic design specification**

Building on the ECONOMIC FLOW finding: FARM-5 abstain tax is the one unimplemented FARM defense. This beat specs the implementation.

**Abstain tax design:**

```
Trigger: validator has participated in ≥N rounds in epoch; abstain rate > 50%
Penalty: 0.5% stake deduction applied at epoch close
Reset: abstain ratio resets each epoch (not cumulative across epochs)
Exempt: first K rounds in validator's career (learning period)
```

**Implementation path in codebase:**

1. **New param**: `AbstainTaxThresholdBPS uint64` (default: `500_000` = 50%), `AbstainTaxPenaltyBPS uint64` (default: `5_000` = 0.5%), `AbstainTaxMinRounds uint64` (minimum rounds before tax triggers, default: 10)

2. **Track per-validator per-epoch**: Add `AbstainCount`/`ParticipationCount` to validator epoch state. Already has tier tracking; add to same structure.

3. **Enforce at epoch close** (`EndBlock` or epoch finalizer): for each validator, if `ParticipationCount >= AbstainTaxMinRounds AND AbstainCount / ParticipationCount > AbstainTaxThresholdBPS / 1_000_000`: apply `AbstainTaxPenaltyBPS / 1_000_000 * validator_stake` deduction.

4. **Route deducted tokens**: to research fund (aligns with bounty pool recommendation from beat 26) — abstain penalties fund knowledge improvement.

**Estimated scope:** 2-3 files (`types/params.go`, validator epoch state struct, epoch close handler). Isolated change. No changes to core commit/reveal/aggregate flow.

**ZRN economics note:** At 0.5% penalty rate on abstain-heavy validators, the tax is punitive enough to deter systematic abstain farming while not ruinous for occasional abstain (which may be legitimate: validator genuinely uncertain). The 50% threshold ensures only repeat abstainers are taxed. Routing to research fund keeps token flow purposeful.

**Revised FARM defense status:** 14/15 implemented. FARM-5 (abstain tax) is the sole remaining gap. Well-defined fix; estimate ≤1 session.

## 6. Standing Duty: TESTNET (slot 4, cycle 26)

**Focus: P1 Blocker 1 status audit — verifier_count=0 bug**

Prior beats (4 through 40) consistently listed as open: "verified facts pass with verifier_count = 0 — truth integrity gap."

**Current codebase read (`rounds.go:307-313`):**

```go
// CONF-002: Cannot mark a fact verified with zero participants.
// Zero-participant finalization produces phantom confidence signals.
factStatus := types.FactStatusVerified
factIsProvisional := false
if result.ParticipantCount == 0 {
    factStatus = types.FactStatusProvisional
    factIsProvisional = true
}
```

**Finding: P1 BLOCKER 1 IS RESOLVED in the current codebase.**

The CONF-002 guard explicitly addresses the zero-participant case: facts with `ParticipantCount == 0` are marked `FactStatusProvisional` (not `FactStatusVerified`). The comment acknowledges the exact bug diagnosed in beat 4. This fix is in place.

Additional confirmation:
- `FactStatusProvisional = "provisional" // survives structural eval, in challenge window` — provisional facts are still subject to challenge, not accepted as ground truth.
- `citation.go` has no `IsProvisional` references — citations to provisional facts are technically possible, which is a residual P3 concern (should citations to provisional facts earn full citation rewards?). But the core P1 truth-integrity gap (VERIFIED status with 0 verifiers) is closed.

**Revised pre-testnet blocker list:**
- P1 blockers: **1** (was 2) — dangling references in `validateReferences()` at `msg_server.go:996` (P1 blocker 2, beat 7, still open)
- P2 blockers: 5 — FARM-5, phase duration defaults, multi-level citation, submitter cap, oracle bridge counter-claim
- P3 gaps: 4 — claim validation, category enforcement, farm funding, provisional fact citation rewards

**Revised testnet readiness:**

**Estimate: testnet launch is within 1-2 focused sessions** once chain restarts.

## 4. Continuous Verification

**Check: CITATION AUDIT** (rotation slot 1, cycle 27)

Chain offline — codebase audit. Systematic verification of all open P1 and P3 claims against current codebase.

**Finding: P1 BLOCKER 2 RETRACTED.**

Beat 7 diagnosed: "`validateReferences()` checks format only, not fact existence — dangling citations possible."

Current codebase (`msg_server.go:996-1010`):

```go
// validateReferences checks that every reference is a valid anchor and, for on-chain
// IDs, that the cited fact actually exists. Prevents dangling citations.
func (k msgServer) validateReferences(ctx sdk.Context, refs []string) error {
    for _, ref := range refs {
        if !isValidCitationAnchor(ref) {
            return fmt.Errorf("...")
        }
        if !strings.HasPrefix(ref, "external://") {
            if _, found := k.GetFact(ctx, ref); !found {
                return fmt.Errorf("citation target %q does not exist on-chain", ref)
            }
        }
    }
    return nil
}
```

`k.GetFact(ctx, ref)` is present at line 1004. The comment explicitly says "Prevents dangling citations." The fix is in place and documented.

**Verdict: P1 Blocker 2 RETRACTED** — dangling citation prevention IS implemented. Both P1 blockers are now closed.

**Finding: P3 Gap 2 RETRACTED (epistemic category enforcement).**

`isValidEpistemicCategory()` is called at `msg_server.go:58` and validates against 10 defined categories. P3 gap 2 was not a gap.

**Finding: P3 Gap 1 RETRACTED (claim_text length validation).**

- Min: `msg_server.go:46` — `len(msg.ClaimText) < params.MinClaimTextLength`
- Max: `types/types.go:424` — `len(msg.ClaimText) > MaxClaimTextLength` (10KB constant), checked in `ValidateBasic()`

Both enforced. P3 gap 1 was not a gap.

**Finding: Submitter concentration cap CONFIRMED ABSENT.**

No `MaxFacts`, `MaxClaims`, or per-epoch submission limit found for general fact submission. Only `MaxContradictionsPerAgentPerEpoch = 3` exists for contradiction spam. Per-epoch submitter concentration cap (P2 blocker 7, beat 9) remains open.
